# Gyneconomy — archive

Everything that is no longer true, kept so it can be reached. **Nothing in here is a current rule.** The one
working document is `CLAUDE-CODE.md`; if this file and that one disagree, that one is right.

Five things live here: the one-line history of every version, the long narratives behind the versions that
needed one, the figures and glyphs that were removed from the app, the superseded doc set that preceded this
pair, and the research the app was built from.

---

# Part 1 — Version history

One line per version, newest first.

- **538** · Sep 26, 2026 · **index.html is built from `src/`** (Keren: "I don't feel comfortable with the fact that we have a very long index"). The one file becomes 17 parts — page shell, stylesheet, and the script in 13 files by layer — assembled by `npm run build`. The artifact and the service worker still get one self-contained file; a person now gets a 1,600-line largest part instead of 13,000. The build is `parts.join("\n")` and nothing else: the script is one IIFE sharing a closure, so concatenating in order reproduces that scope EXACTLY, which is why the split is provable rather than plausible — the first build reproduced the previous `index.html` byte for byte, sha256 56d5ce36, and all 17 parts hash-identical across two machines. No bundler, no module wrapper, no import order to reason about. `src/manifest.json` holds the order, and the order IS the semantics, since module vars are assigned between parts. `index.html` stays committed — it is what ships and its diff is worth reading — and `npm run build:check` runs in CI ahead of the suite, so editing the output instead of the source fails the build. Round-trip proved both ways: an edit in `src/` reaches the output, and a byte appended to the output is caught. Suite 60/60 against the BUILT file. (Keren)
- **537** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The sources generator, and what it found** (Keren: "don't leave any backlog"). `sources.html` had been documented as generated by a script that was never committed, so it could not be rebuilt from the repo — the classic way a generated file quietly becomes hand-edited. `tools/gen-sources.js` (`npm run sources`) now opens the app, lets it build its OWN Sources screen and reads the result, so the grouping patterns stay in `index.html` and nowhere else; it refuses to write if anything lands in an "Other" group. Its first run refused, correctly: two citations matched no pattern, which means the LIVE app was showing an "Other" group. They are First Trust and Fisher Investments — asset managers' commentary, carrying the typical-cycle-length figure the ring is scaled to, and the only citations in the app that are neither primary nor a labelled compilation. Grouped so the screen is correct, and logged as an open question for Keren, since dropping a source is not Claude's call. The rebuild also dropped FOUR stale Gallup citations the app stopped using in Version 392 and had still been publishing, and fixed "Fear &amp;amp; Greed" — a double-escape visible on the in-app screen too, because the source title carried the entity. 61 rows → 59. Suite 60/60, 32/32 snapshot identical. Artifact version 524. (Keren)
- **536** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The kinds get measured instead of guessed** (Keren: "ok go", on the nine mixed steps). Before splitting them, they were measured — and the classifier turned out to be wrong. Counting DOM writes in a body counts the writes inside its event HANDLERS, which fire later and say nothing about the step. Patching `addEventListener` and running each step showed six of the nine bind listeners and touch no DOM at all: pure wiring, mislabelled. Two are genuinely mixed (`renderPressurePage`, `renderSpreadHistory`). And the 1,228-line `renderPagesAndNav`, the monolith the whole refactor was supposed to be about, binds NOTHING and converges at 414 and 1280 — it was already repeatable. Mixed falls 9 → 2, wire rises 1 → 7, and repeatable steps go 14 → 15, all converging. No surgery was needed; the measurement was the work. `tools/classify.js` (`npm run classify`) does it and flags any declared kind it disagrees with. Suite 60/60, 32/32 snapshot identical, stylesheet 1,166. Artifact version 523. (Keren)
- **535** · Sep 26, 2026 · **Idempotent sources, and honest kinds** (Keren: "let's do the cleanup"). Eight places concatenated onto `allSources`, six of them inside render steps, so anything re-rendering listed the same citation twice or four times — a real bug on the Sources screen, not only a refactor blocker. `addSources()` de-duplicates by URL and keeps first-appearance order. Then the three steps that could not repeat were RECLASSIFIED rather than forced: `renderSignsList` moves the static markup into the category rows and consumes its source, `renderSubjectRows` writes into hosts that move, `renderPsychologyTag` reads a note a later step fills. All three are one-shot by design, so they became kind `build`, and exclusions are now by kind rather than by name — a named exception goes stale, a kind is a fact. The suite gained the invariant that makes the registry trustworthy: every repeatable step must CONVERGE (settle, run again, nothing changes), `GYN.render()` must leave the DOM untouched, and the kind counts are pinned. Convergence rather than equality because a width-aware chart re-measures its host — `renderHorizonPage` legitimately redraws 334 then 360. Also: the first Data run succeeded with all four documents and zero failures, but wrote `09/25/2026` for the curve while everything else is ISO, so the fetcher now normalises and REFUSES an unparsable date; runners pinned to `ubuntu-24.04`. Suite 56 → 60. 32/32 snapshot identical, stylesheet 1,166. Artifact version 522. (Keren)
- **534** · Sep 26, 2026 · **Data arriving on its own** (Keren, finishing the architecture). A scheduled Action runs `tools/fetch-live.js` against Treasury and FRED and COMMITS `data/live.json`, so every refresh is a diff that can be read, blamed or reverted — and because it lands on `main`, CI runs and data cannot reach the site without passing the suite. The page gained `fetchSiteData()`: same-origin, no key, no CORS, inert in the Artifact (framed) and on `file://`. Two sources now, one decoder, one repaint layer — both the database and the file hand documents to `applyLive`. `vixClose` and `hyOas` are published as bare scalars because they are single readings inside objects whose bands and prose are editorial; `applyLive` places them. Four documents automate; CAPE (no FRED series) and CNN Fear & Greed (unfetchable) stay with the nightly task, because a scraped value would be worse than none. A failed fetch or an out-of-band value is left out and the previous value stands. Proved end to end over HTTP: the file alone moved the yield pair mid-session, the cache took all four documents, and a second load rendered from the cache synchronously. 32/32 snapshot identical at load, suite 56/56, stylesheet 1,166. Artifact version 521. (Keren)
- **533** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The repaint layer — live data lands without a reload** (Keren: "let's finish it"). `applyLive(name, value)` assigns the arriving document to its module var, RE-DERIVES what was computed from it at load — `valuation.tag`, the Volume and Pulse tags, reusing the derive steps by name — then runs that document's repaints. `REPAINT` maps documents to repaints, and an empty list is a statement: the VIX and Desire/Volume/Pulse rows are on inner pages that redraw on open. Repaints edit a text node and a tag in place, never the row's innerHTML, because `catItem` normalises `.unit` to `.ci-unit` when it moves a row and rebuilding would undo it. The refresher now applies every CHANGED document rather than the policy rate alone, with `LIVE_CACHE` swapped in first so `LIVE()` remains the only shape-decoder. Proved mid-session with no reload: 36 → 82 and the derived mood class `serious-ink` → `critical-ink`, the yield pair 5.18/4.24 → 4.05/5.55, and null, a wrong shape and an unknown document each refused. Suite 50 → 56 checks; 32/32 snapshot identical, stylesheet 1,166. Artifact version 520. (Keren)
- **532** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The detail store gets keys, and re-rendering gets measured** (Keren, continuing the architecture). `detailTexts` becomes content-addressed: `detailSlot(html)` keys a slot by the note's own HTML, so identical content reuses it and the array grows with DISTINCT notes instead of with every render — a real leak, since it had been growing as a reader browsed. `renderSignsList` also stopped appending a second `.sign-detail` into the Temperature sheet. Then the registry was used to measure each step rather than guess: of 14 repeatable steps **10 are already idempotent**; `renderSignsList` accounts for essentially the whole 22KB, `renderHorizonPage` grows 38 bytes, `renderPsychologyTag` changes content at equal length, and `renderSubjectRows` THROWS — because `renderSignsList` moves the DOM it built into the category sheets, so its hosts are gone by design. That reframes the goal: full re-rendering is the wrong target, and the right one is a small repaint per live figure, which is what `repaintPolicy()` already does and why it works. 32/32 snapshot identical, suite 50/50, stylesheet 1,166. Artifact version 519. (Keren)
- **531** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The step registry: a seam where there was none** (Keren, choosing the architecture). The 28 anonymous IIFEs become named functions with a measured kind, registered in `GYN` and still invoked exactly where they stood — module vars are assigned between them, so the order is load-bearing. Counted, not asserted: 6 check, 3 derive, 1 wire, 8 render, 9 MIXED (listeners and DOM together, which is what actually blocks re-running), 1 live. Behaviour identical, proved by a new 32-state DOM snapshot harness (`tools/snapshot.js`) that is itself deterministic — two captures of one file match — so a difference means a difference: 32/32 identical against V530, suite 50/50, stylesheet 1,166. The seam immediately paid: `GYN.render()` run twice grows the DOM by ~22KB and `data-detail-idx` climbs 7→25, because `expandBtn`/`infoIcon` APPEND to `detailTexts`. So the one thing blocking repeatable rendering is now named and measured — the detail store needs a stable key per call site rather than an append-only index. `deriveUninversionDetail` has the same shape, appending to `allSources`. Artifact version 518. (Keren)
- **530** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The app becomes a site, and installable** (Keren, choosing to outgrow the artifact: "hosted, installable, its own domain, data arriving on its own"). A PWA manifest and a service worker, plus `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, which runs the email gate, the 50 checks and the map freshness check, and deploys to GitHub Pages ONLY if they pass. One `index.html` serves both the site and the artifact: the registration sits in a second `<script>` block outside the app's IIFE, guarded on not-framed and https, so it is silent inside the artifact's sandboxed iframe. The worker is network-first for HTML and cache-first for the rest — the figures are IN the html, so a cached page is a stale reading, while an icon is not. Verified served over HTTP: manifest 200, worker active at the root scope, no page errors, and an offline reload still renders the app. The suite's parse check had assumed ONE script block and its greedy regex swallowed the boundary — caught by the suite itself, now per-block. Artifact version 517. (Keren)
- **529** · Sep 26, 2026 · **One declaration per constant, found by the new map** (Keren, on the 12,700-line file). `docs/MAP.md` is generated from `index.html` by `tools/make-map.py`: the five regions, all 99 banner sections, every top-level function and var grouped under its section, the IIFEs, and the registries — about 1,000 lines indexing 297,000 tokens, each entry carrying a grep anchor because line numbers shift on every insertion. It paid for itself twice on its first run. `PULSE_WINDOW` and `PULSE_PRE2008` turned out to be declared TWICE at top level, same scope, same values — one number in two places, which ONE FIGURE / ONE NUMBER exists to forbid; the second pair is gone, with a comment where it stood. And the working document's "31 top-level IIFEs that render at load" was wrong: 28 render, 4 compute a value, 32 in all. The 31 was V526's TOTAL, before `LIVE_CACHE`, recorded as if it were the render count. The map is now named as the authority for that figure. Artifact version 516. (Keren)
- **528** · Sep 26, 2026 · **Every figure goes live, with no render refactor** (Keren: "prepare the app for an API integration"). V525 got the policy rate live because nothing derives from it; the other five documents could not follow, since 31 render IIFEs and no boot function mean a derived value cannot be repainted. The refactor was the wrong tool. Instead the page caches the previous visit's database answer in `localStorage["gyn.live"]` and applies it SYNCHRONOUSLY, above every consumer — so all six of `fedFunds`, `yieldCurve`, `sentiment`, `valuation`, `coincident` and `fearGreed` feed their derivations correctly with no repaint code at all. Stale-while-revalidate: the literals remain the fallback for a first visit or a viewer without the grant, and the nightly republish still fixes what a new reader sees. Cost: a returning viewer is one load behind. Proved on both shapes — an object doc moved 36/`serious-ink` to 82/`critical-ink`, a series doc moved `5.18/4.24` to `4.05/5.55` — and five malformed caches each fell back silently. The eight probes were folded into `gyn-test.js`, 42 checks to 50. Artifact version 515. The app then moved into git at `github.com/kerzaiden/gyneconomy`, ending the `curve-and-cycle-vNNN.html` chain — versions are commits now, their numbers kept because every rule in the docs is anchored to one. `CLAUDE.md` is the lean entry point, the working document and this archive sit under `docs/`, and the suite runs anywhere with `npm i && npm run setup && npm test`: verified 50/50 on Keren's Mac. Which copy is canonical — artifact for the app, repo for the docs, history append-only from both — is written into both `CLAUDE.md` and the working document. (Keren)
- **527** · Sep 26, 2026 · **A permanent test suite, and the history cut to its own rule** (Keren, asking what to improve). Every version until now was verified by probes written and thrown away; `gyn-test.js` makes them 42 committed checks with a baseline, run by one command. Proved by deliberately breaking `--gap` and `COL_FILL` — both caught. The 184 over-long entries here were compressed 58,109 → 12,946 words, every version number verified unchanged; the median entry is now 66 words against the stated 60. (Keren)
- **526** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The whole dataset moves into the artifact's database** (Keren: "I would want to put as much data in the database as possible, since this is preparing the app for an API integration"). 51 documents under `data/`, ~126 KB — every series, every reading object, the cycle model and every source list, document id = variable name. Exported by appending an exporter inside the script's IIFE, reading the objects out of a live page in Playwright, and writing them in two pinned batches. Arrays store as `{kind:"series", n, rows}`, objects as `{kind:"object", …fields}` so top-level fields survive. The PAGE still reads only `data/fedFunds` live — storing is not wiring — but nothing has to be re-shaped when a pipeline starts writing these. Verified readable at `as_level:"interact"`. (Keren)
- **525** · Sep 26, 2026 · **The first series comes from outside the file** (Keren: "prepare the app for an API integration"). A published artifact cannot call FRED, so the route is the artifact's own database: Claude writes it, the page reads it with `claude.use("db")`. Proved on `fedFunds`; the file's literal stays as the fallback. Artifact version 514. (Keren)

- **488** · Sep 25, 2026 · **The fiscal table becomes a panel, read against CBO’s 50-year averages** (Keren, continuing the rollout). Power’s lab TABLE is now four stacked panel rows — name and figure left, spectrum right, hairlines between — which is the reference’s own shape and the reason a clinical report is readable: every track starts at the same x. The three hand-drawn fiscal bands are gone: CBO publishes the figure each was reaching for, so debt, interest and the deficit all read against **what the last fifty years averaged** — 51% of GDP, 2.0%, 3.8%. They also became ONE-SIDED, the bigger correction: a two-sided band called a 1%-of-GDP deficit abnormal, and the reading on all three is how far ABOVE the average they sit. `stressScoreFor` normalises on min/max and never reads `optimal`, so Economic power is untouched — checked before changing anything. (Keren)
- **487** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Money velocity, and Activity’s band gets a source** (Keren: “just write money velocity, the M2 data is already in the info”, and continue the rollout). Activity drops its read-card for a panel row and its 3.5–5% finally says what it is: a bracket around the CBO’s noncyclical rate of unemployment, currently ~4.2%, with the (i) stating plainly that the CBO figure is published and the two edges are round numbers set either side rather than a computed interval. Its ends read the opposite way to most bars — **Tight** at low unemployment, **Slack** at high. (Keren)

Neither band moved: Volume’s 3.5–10% turns out to be the 10th–90th percentile of 1960–2019 M2 growth (computed from the page’s own series — mean 6.80%, deciles 3.3–10.3%), and Pulse’s 1.7–2.2× is the pre-2008 era’s range. - **484** · Sep 25, 2026 · **The reading panel goes white** (Keren). `.spread-tile`’s `--surface-2` reads as a well sunk INTO the card; this is a panel sitting ON it, so the fill is `--surface` with the border kept. - **474** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Refresh to Sep 24, and the nightly task's stop clause removed.** The curve had been stuck on Sep 21 while the 10-year ran 4.96 → 5.18%, its highest in the app's twenty-one-year record; OAS 2.66 → 2.73, CAPE 41.6 → 41.3, VIX left at 14.21 because FRED has posted nothing after Sep 22. The prompt is self-contained now.
- **473** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Horizon: the spread becomes Mood's fourth reading** (Keren: "judge if it's optimistic or pessimistic… so it belongs to Mood"). - **469** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Pressure's preview takes a ring** (Keren). - **468** · Sep 25, 2026 · **The barometer: Weather's combined reading** (Keren, from the app's own record: "total growth and total change in prices are equal at the end of each cycle, more or less"). - **467** · Sep 25, 2026 · **The VIX is the fear index, under an umbrella in the rain** (Keren, both). It also exposed a rule with a false premise — `.metric-sheet .body-term` is hidden because the top bar names the page, which is untrue of a card that is a GUEST on another reading's page, so the card read "THE FEAR INDEX" under a gauge called Fear & Greed with nothing saying VIX. - **466** · Sep 25, 2026 · **The index reads 36, and the VIX goes back inside it** (Keren, checking CNN against the page: "the VIX is called the fear index — we don't need two fear meters on Mood"). The VIX is one of the index's seven components, so it cannot be its peer: it is a reading under the gauge again, with its note as the second Highlights card. (Keren)
- **465** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Fear & Greed takes the dial** (Keren, with the glyph). The half-dial the index is published as, which is also the picture on its own page, so the mark and the page it opens are the same object — no other mark in the set manages that. The waves it replaces were drawn in V457 for a row still called Sentiment: they named a mood, and this names the instrument. The needle is filled and tapered rather than stroked, because an even-weight line is a clock hand. (Keren)
- **464** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Mood loses Sentiment and gains Fear & Greed and the VIX** (Keren: "sentiment is mood, so I don't need another subcategory named sentiment — and is Desire's spread the same thing?"). - **463** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Energy is three rows: Power, Households, Activity** (Keren: "debt service is too general — there is government and household debt service", and "I want another subcategory named activity"). - **462** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Load folds back into Energy, as a Debt service page** (Keren: "we don't need a new category named Load — stress is connected to energy"). She is right, and the app says so: `energyFromReserve()` inverts the fiscal STRESS score, so Power's word is computed FROM the debt markers, and two categories put a verdict in one box and its own inputs in the other. Energy now holds what is left (Power), what is owed (Debt service) and what the energy goes into (Activity's page, absorbed). (Keren)
- **461** · Sep 25, 2026 · **One width for every peek art** (`PEEK_W`). All four builders stretch their viewBox with `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, and V459 matched only the gauge to the 80px slot; the other three stayed at 152, so their marks shrank by 0.73 while their spacing shrank by 0.53 and closed up. A column now fills 62% of its slot where it filled 76% — Apple's fill 58%. (Keren)
- **460** · Sep 25, 2026 · **Load, the fifth category: Power and Households** (Keren, after reading Wild Power and asking whether the four were complete). The book's chapter on armouring — the skin thickens on the way up and is shed on the way down — is leverage, and the app measured the PRICE of credit and the QUANTITY of money but never the STOCK of debt, except the government's, which sat inside Power. Power moves to Load; Energy becomes capacity alone and opens Activity's page directly. New reading: household debt service against the saving rate, both shares of disposable income, so one axis carries both. (Keren)
- **459** · Sep 25, 2026 · **The category row's miniature becomes a thumbnail** (Keren, holding Apple Health beside us: "the infographic is small and below the date, so it's not like there's a lot of white space"). - **458** · Sep 24, 2026 · **Energy is one page: Power at the top, Activity's page below it** (Keren: "I don't need a subcategory of Activity"). - **457** · Sep 24, 2026 · **Sentiment takes three waves, and Activity becomes Energy with Power inside it** (Keren, with both glyphs). - **455** · Sep 24, 2026 · **The Circulation mark loses its dollar** (Keren: "simplify it to be just this without a dollar inside"). - **429** · Sep 24, 2026 · **The row is just "Total", and Highlights opens by saying what the reading is** (Keren). - **428** · Sep 24, 2026 · **The key goes back inside the chart, set in whitespace, and there are two control heights** (Keren). - **427** · Sep 24, 2026 · **The chart's reference lines move into a key under it, and the controls all match** (Keren: "it's currently very confusing"). - **417** · Sep 24, 2026 · **Temperature can be read by the year of the cycle** (Keren, wanting the cycles connected to the history). One line changes — `insertBefore(press, row)` rather than `row.nextSibling` — and the sheet still follows the row, so every row on this page is immediately followed by its own page. **A build-script trap recorded**: in this Python, `r"...\u2014..."` does NOT preserve the literal escape — a raw string still resolves `\uXXXX`, so an anchor written that way silently becomes an em dash and never matches the file's literal `\u2014`. Anchors on those lines must use `"\\u2014"`

- **345** · Sep 21, 2026 · **Risk / Reward Ratio, and the card closes**. Keren, overruling Version 343's trimmed title and asking for padding under the container. - **341** · Sep 21, 2026 · **the Sentiment ring wears the face**. - **333** · Sep 21, 2026 · **the bar's margins start working** (Keren: "add spacing between the bar and the text"). `.peek-chart` is `display:inline`, and an inline box ignores vertical margins, so every margin written into `.press-row .peek-chart` since Version 327 was decoration. - **294** · Sep 20, 2026 · **one unclosed brace, three symptoms** (Keren: a square info icon, a click that does nothing, and the legend somehow inside the yield curve). So they keep the card — same component, same two-column grid, which is what makes them comparable with each other at a glance — and move down among the signs, between Sentiment and Desire. **The distinction worth keeping is that "card" and "headline" are two different decisions**: the form says what kind of reading it is, the position says how much it matters. - **291** · Sep 20, 2026 · **Effort and Pulse join the row, on a fourth kind of picture** (Keren, wanting Pulse and Effort side by side, on whatever infographic fits). - **286** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the menu leaves at full opacity** (Keren: "when I close it, it closes really, really fast"). The fade goes, and the two directions get their own curves.
- **285** · Sep 20, 2026 · **one direction for everything that stacks** (Keren: "the menu should slide from right to left, not top down"). Version 284 had given the menu an upward sheet motion and its inner screens a sideways one, on the theory that two directions would carry the hierarchy. One direction carries it better, because the reader already has the convention from every phone they own: **going deeper pushes in from the right, coming back slides out the same way.** The menu and its screens now share one rule, which also deletes the `.sources-view` transform override and the reduced-motion exception that went with it
- **284** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the menu arrives, and Temperature's word matches its bar** (Keren, two notes). Horizontal padding grows with the radius, 16px to 22px, so the words keep clear of it
- **282** · Sep 20, 2026 · **one rule, one container** (Keren, two reports). `.hi-card:last-of-type` matches the last sibling of the same tag, so no card was ever last of its type and `.hi-cycles` drew a second border; fixed with `:last-child`. A detector now walks every inner page for doubled rules. And the Sentiment page loses its outer box, which restated the bar and the gauge.
- **281** · Sep 20, 2026 · **one line, one button** (Keren: "the trend button needs to look like a button — make it so it's only one line; I want to read *trend falling across fifty-five months*"). `trendOf` now returns a `span` rather than a `detail`, and the row reads **Trend — falling across 55 months** on one line. **The magnitude left with the second line and lost nothing**, which is the point: pressing the button labels *both ends* of the fit on the chart, so the distance is shown rather than asserted. That also retired `trendSpan`, the V276 helper whose whole job was keeping the row's rounded figure in step with the chart's — with the row no longer stating a figure, there is nothing left to disagree
- **280** · Sep 20, 2026 · **a reading on a ring** (Keren: the VIX and the spread as rings, text beside them, bars dropped). Open question left with Keren: a log scale.
- **279** · Sep 20, 2026 · **Temperature gets its trend button, and the empty tab gets a mark** (Keren, listing what was outstanding). V274 refused this toggle because two plum lines on one picture is a fault; the V276 toggle dissolves it, since asking for the trend steps the average back with the columns. The chart parks its own fit on `tempState`. The Portfolio placeholder gets the tab's own briefcase.

- **277** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the Fear & Greed half-dial, in this app's hand** (Keren, asking for the conventional gauge in our own language). - **276** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the trend is a button** (Keren, with Apple Health: "it's a button, not a container. If I press on it, it shows me the trend"). Pressing the row now dims everything that states a number and gives the fit the picture, both ends carrying the fit's own values instead of a legend.
- **276b** · the same version · **what each cycle did to output and to prices, side by side** (Keren, on seeing the pair: "this is so interesting — put it in the analysis tab per cycle"). Two chips on every row of the Analysis tab, computed the same way over the same closed years, so the comparison is real rather than rhetorical: **AI +11% / +16%, COVID-19 +10% / +13%, Subprime +17% / +17% dead even across a decade, Dot-Com +24% / +25%, and the Long Boom +37% / +34% — the only cycle of the five where output beat prices.** They sit on their own line above the status line so they read against each other rather than against the market total below them
- **275** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the bar has an edge, and Temperature has its total** (Keren). And the Growth card's "This cycle" box becomes a component, applied to Temperature as Keren asked: `eraInflation` compounds the December readings across a cycle's closed years, so the card says what the cycle cost.
- **274** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the trend stops being a sentence and becomes a line** (Keren, on Valuations: "just put a trend line, a purple trend line", with the verdict moved into the trend row). `trendOf` now hands back the fit it fitted, so the chart draws the very line the pill describes, both in the same purple. The pill becomes a filled row; Temperature and GDP are left out.
- **273** · Sep 20, 2026 · **Valuations, and Years / Cycles** (Keren). The panel is **Valuations**, plural, everywhere it is named: the peek kicker (which also sets the top bar and the Structural page's row), the section heading inside the page, and the Sources screen's own section. The prose was made to agree rather than left contradicting the name — "Valuations say little about the next twelve months", "Valuations are close to useless as a timing signal". And the range control's second segment becomes **Cycles**: "Years" beside "By cycle" was a noun beside a prepositional phrase, which is why the pair never sat straight. Both segments of a control should be the same part of speech
- **272** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the bar names the page, in the app's own voice** (Keren, on Economic power: the title duplicated, and the page's name wanted in Cormorant). With Cormorant italic in the bar, both pages drop `.card-head` and their subtitle.
- **271** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the timing chip becomes a door, and wears its place in the order** (Keren: the chip should open all the metrics by indicator type, in colours from the existing system). The hint sentence moves to the page the chip opens, so V269's deleted headings come back as pages; this needed a back stack. The classes are ordered, so they take the yield maturities' one purple.
- **270** · Sep 20, 2026 · **a page carries only what it alone can say** (Keren, on the Temperature page: duplications, too much text, and the source line). Three cuts: the detail drops the head, figure and reference bar the chart already carries; `lead` keeps only the two metaphors, with buried figures lifted into `facts` rows; and the per-page source links go to the Sources screen.
- **269** · Sep 20, 2026 · **every sign gets a page, and the timing travels with it** (Keren: inner pages for the remaining indicators, the section titles gone, the timing as a pill). All six drawers become rows that open pages, Yield curve and Sentiment converted where they stand. And `.metric-sheet{display:flex}` was out-weighing `hidden`, so closed pages collected the parent's gap.
- **268** · Sep 20, 2026 · **a run is a capsule or a dot, never the shape in between** (Keren, on the COVID-19 cycle: "if it's too short, make it a dot"). V267 left a shape too short for a stretch of time, too long for a moment. A run must now be half as long again as it is tall, or it becomes the dot the ring draws.
- **267** · Sep 20, 2026 · **year on year, shown rather than asserted** (Keren, wanting the year-on-year comparison shown, not just asserted). Two columns would have lied: real GDP moves too little for them to differ. `pairChart` draws the gap instead — a connector with a disc at each end — and new `gdpLevels` is stored rather than derived. The trend pill now reports a distance, not a slope.
- **266** · Sep 20, 2026 · **one preview language, one page shape** — two asks from Keren, one idea. The gauge was drawn from a segment count, the columns from a slot, so the two could never line up; `reserveGauge` now takes the columns' rule, so the spacing is the constant and the bar count falls out of it. And every inner page gets one shape: head, chart, Highlights, drawer.
- **265** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the picture slot has one height, whatever fills it** (Keren: "make the economic power preview like the others, meaning that the height of the bars is identical so that it stretches to the entire height of the container"). `.rgauge svg` rendered at 30px against `.peek-chart svg`'s 52px, and the two only agreed at ≥760px where a single rule gives both 66 — so the mismatch was the default and the desktop was the accident. One height for both, and `.rg-seg` gains the round caps the columns already had. The page's own gauge is untouched: `.metric-sheet .rgauge svg{height:40px}` still wins on specificity
- **264** · Sep 20, 2026 · **one card, one order** (Keren: drop the purple bar from the previews, and structure them all like Economic power — title, then picture, then data). The gauged card was the only one reading name → picture → reading, which is why the row of four never settled. That order collapses `peekCard()` into one branch and removes the sideways wide-screen variant.
- **263** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the range bar, the trend, and a minimal preview** (Keren, from Apple Health). `rangeBar` is a segmented control over the chart, and every page offers only the ranges it has — a segment with no data behind it promises what the page cannot keep. `trendOf` is a least-squares fit, reading "not available" below eight readings. `PEEK_MARKS = 12` replaces previews carrying 55, 18 and 57 marks.
- **262** · Sep 20, 2026 · **quiet previews** (Keren, from Apple Health's Steps card: past bars greyed out, only the current one coloured). A preview should say one thing and point at where the rest is. Every bar but the last keeps its height and loses its colour, and `.peek-chev` says the card is a button. `colPeek` grows a `base` so Valuation's peek matches its page.
- **261** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the phase gets its own palette, everywhere it is said** (Keren, yes to the open question). V260 moved GDP's chart onto the dial's seasons but left the word, the tag and the Highlights names in teal and red. The phase is now a token pair, `--phase-up` and `--phase-down`, with `phaseClass(regime)` beside `regimeState()`. And `colPeek` reads the same `quarterRegime()` the chart does.
- **260** · Sep 20, 2026 · **Temperature's four faults, and GDP's phases** (Keren). The peeks were still lines while the pages had been columns on the heat ramp, so `heatStep()` comes out of `drawTemperature` and `colPeek` draws both miniatures. The legend described colours gone since V257 and goes. And GDP's chart leaves the severity palette for the dial's seasons: a phase is a category, not a severity.
- **259** · Sep 20, 2026 · **four tabs: Cycle · Analysis · Portfolio · Content** (Keren, merging the Analysis tab into the Calendar and turning the Calendar into Portfolio). The merge was a correction: both tabs listed the same five cycles, and the Analysis one could not even be clicked. One `.subject` row per cycle now carries what both said. Portfolio is empty and says so, with no placeholder figures.
- **258** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the battery gauge** (Keren, from a "Next Dividend Payout" card she liked for Economic power). `reserveGauge(value, state)` draws twenty round-capped segments, the first N lit — the empty ones carry the meaning, being the charge already gone. The right borrow for this reading and no other, Economic power having been a battery in Keren's own words since V229.
- **257** · Sep 20, 2026 · **four forms, one language** (Keren: the line chart does not match the cycle dial; every design should match its data). The dial's grammar, written down from `drawDial()`, is a track, the reading inside it, a disc for "here", and no axis. Each metric now gets its own form: a battery, diverging bars out of the fair line, the Apple Health average form for Temperature and GDP.
- **256** · Sep 20, 2026 · **the popups become real pages** (Keren: "switch the popups to inner pages, there is a lot of information there for a popup"). Four screens of scrolling is a place you went to, not something you dismiss, so a metric page now works like the Calendar's open cycle and `openMetricPage` replaces `openMetricSheet`, with the back arrow a single shared handler.
- **255** · Sep 20, 2026 · **Highlights, and the history the two structural panels never had** (Keren, wanting insights from past cycles and, for two panels with no series, choosing to go find the real data). New `powerHistory` from 1979, plus `buffettHistory` and `capeHistory`, from the app's own cited sources. One correction: `stressHistory`'s 2007 trust reading was 2006's; Gallup publishes 33. Economic power and Valuation become pages, and `highlightsHtml` computes every sentence from its series.
- **254** · Sep 20, 2026 · **inner pages** (Keren: a white container round the main cycle, and Temperature and GDP growth as inner pages rather than repeats below). The hero gets its card back. And the peeks become doors: a peek that scrolls you to a drawer is a bookmark. Temperature and GDP leave the list for the shared modal, `openMetricSheet(el)` moving live DOM rather than re-rendering.
- **253** · Sep 19, 2026 · **something leads** (Keren: Temperature and Growth side by side with previews and, asked what "too rigid" meant, same visual weight). Ten identical boxes in a column, which no spacing fixes. Three weights now: the dial loses its box and sits on the page's own ground, a new `.peek` component supports it, and the subject rows recede untouched.
- **252** · Sep 19, 2026 · **a trend beside each reading** (Keren, choosing the Apple Health / Oura direction) — `sparkHtml(values, window, state)` draws a 108×26 line with no axis or labels, normalised to its own min/max, refusing fewer than three points, the window named beside it. Only GDP growth, Yield curve and Sentiment get one: only those three have a series. Temperature was refused one, its full chart already open on the page
- **251** · Sep 19, 2026 · **the page stops shouting** (Keren: "I want the look & feel to be like a health app") — every row was stating its severity twice, once as the mark's coloured wash and again as a filled pill beside the figure, and with five of eight readings in the red family the page read as an alarm panel rather than a health check. A health app colours one thing: on a subject's face the status word now drops its ground and wears the state's ink alone (`.subject-summary .tag`). Inside the drawers the filled pills stay — that is the detail view, where a flag should look like a flag
- **250** · Sep 19, 2026 · **the spacing scale enforced** (Keren: "the spacing is off in the cycle page") — measured rather than guessed, and three faults found: the five Coincident/Lagging rows were **touching at 0px** because `#signs-list` was a plain div that never got the flex gap (introduced in V247), the phone ran 30px before a section where desktop ran 32, and the dial sat 22px above the lead chart. All three were a spacing number set somewhere other than the one place that owns it; every container down the tab now carries the same 10px gap, verified at 390/430/1280 — **10 / 32 / 12 everywhere**
- **249** · Sep 19, 2026 · **the Temperature chart opens on the page** (Keren, from a fintech reference: "let some of the graphs be apparent on landing, not on collapse") — directly under the dial, because it is the only monthly chart and it is the season's own instrument; **moved, not copied**, so the Temperature row keeps its figure and sentence and its drawer keeps the reference range and sources, while the Growth chart stays inside GDP growth — two charts on landing would be the wall that 247–248 undid
- **248** · Sep 19, 2026 · **one word, one rule, one rhythm** (Keren) — the section heads lose their poetic second line and become **Structural / Leading / Coincident / Lagging**, each under a hairline with a short plum cap at its left (`.sect-rule`) rather than inside a container, which would have been a fourth level of enclosure; the tab gets **one spacing scale** — 10px between rows, 32px before a section, 12px after its rule, replacing an accidental 46/22; and the dial hub's "This season ›" line folds into the theme itself, which becomes the link: **Inflation ›**
- **247** · Sep 19, 2026 · **the Cycle tab becomes readable** (Keren: "moving the insights into the drawers is a mistake — the screen looks too condensed, I want to air it out; make insights the main feature and have more info, like in Desire, Effort and Pulse") — the Insights strip is deleted, every row gains **one sentence of plain language on its face** (`.subject-say`, from copy the app already had), the four groups become editorial **section heads** in Cormorant (`.sect-head`: Where she stands / Leading / Coincident / Lagging), and Coincident and Lagging are unpacked into **five subjects of their own**, built in JS from the same arrays, each with a new hand-drawn mark — flame, cog, heartbeat, person, thermometer; ten subjects, 2,581px, longer and readable rather than short and closed
- **246** · Sep 19, 2026 · the Calendar tab's two captions go (Keren) — the one under the cycle list and the one under the season strips, both narrating a picture that explains itself; the kicker above each still names it and the (i) still carries the detail, and `.calendar-caption` goes with them, nothing else wearing it
- **245** · Sep 19, 2026 · **the piggy bank** lands on Valuation (Keren: "your call, make the piggy bank yourself" — the Lovable workspace is out of credits, so it is drawn here rather than by the DSM, and should be rebuilt there when credits return) — three fill levels off the two gauges' flags, body identical across them, the tail dropped as unreadable at 24px; **the colour call: the bank fills neutrally and the colour carries risk**, so a full bank is red, keeping one convention with the sentiment faces rather than green-for-abundance

- **243** · Sep 19, 2026 · **Sentiment wears a face** (Keren: "I want the sad face to a smiley face range") — five faces on the app's 24 grid, one per band, only the mouth changing (deep frown → frown → flat → smile → broad smile), rendered through `iconMark` in place of the progress ring, which was the wrong instrument for a scale with no "full"; the face is the mood and the wash is the risk, so Extreme greed smiles on the same red as Extreme fear frowns

- **241** · Sep 19, 2026 · the Fear & Greed (i) now says plainly what the figure is (Keren: "is CNN the most credible source?") — CNN is the originator, so it is the right source, but the index is a media composite rather than an official statistic and is the one figure on the page that **cannot be reproduced from a primary series**; the whole note rewritten as five sentences so `factsFrom` still lands on five facts

- **235** · Sep 19, 2026 · **one bar shape for everything** (Keren: "Fear & Greed should be inside the container like the VIX and the spread, and look like their bars — it doesn't need to be different") — the composite returns to the table as its lead row wearing the standard meter, ends **Fear … Neutral 35–65 … Greed**, and the fourteen emotions survive as `stops`, a new `meterHtml` option putting invisible named hover zones on any track; the bespoke coloured bar and all its CSS are gone, the spread's ends become the market's own words **Tight … Wide** (not Greedy/Fearful) and its sub-line loses the extra clause
- **234** · Sep 19, 2026 · the line under the mood bar goes (Keren: "I don't need this: Anxiety 86 · market falling") — the drawer's own summary row above it already writes the word, the score and the axis, and the Feeling tile says it again; the bar keeps its head, its two end captions and the mark, which now carries the full reading in its title
- **233** · Sep 19, 2026 · the mood **ring becomes a bar** (Keren: "just make it like a bar, a colourful bar — hover shows the emotions, you label the current one, and that's it") — eight stops from maximum opportunity to maximum risk, each carrying the cycle's two names for that level (falling/rising), the mark at the score, one word written out; the Fear & Greed lead row folds into the bar's own head with its (i), and the high-yield spread's note is rewritten to lead with what a narrowing spread genuinely means — cheap credit, healthy companies — before saying why a contrarian panel reads it as compensation instead, with June 2007 as the record low that settles it (Keren: "I think you misdiagnosed the yield spread")
- **232** · Sep 19, 2026 · the wheel's **direction was broken** — it read the S&P's year-to-date return, which cannot turn until the year does — so it becomes a dated one-month trend (−0.75% to Sep 18); with the VIX at **14.81** the score stays 86 but the reading moves from Thrill to **Anxiety**; the (i) now explains why this reads 86 while CNN's Fear & Greed reads 29 (records vs. recent ranges) and the Sentiment and Valuation drawers drop their context lines, which only re-listed the table (Keren)
- **231** · Sep 19, 2026 · **Sentiment and Valuation split in two** (Keren: "the Buffett indicator and CAPE are valuations, not sentiment") — fast and contrarian in one drawer, slow and structural in another beside Economic power — and sentiment gains the **cycle of market emotions** as a ring (her chart): a `Fear & Greed` score 0–100 from the VIX and the high-yield spread, each on its own record and both log-scaled, plus the market's own direction, placing her on one of fourteen emotions (today **86, rising → Thrill**, one step short of Euphoria); margin debt joins as a row but stays out of the score, its scale not being record-anchored; the VIX's one-sided "optimal < 20" becomes a usual band read Complacent–Panicked, and its false LEI claim is gone

The backtest (V90), the palette audit (applied V163) and the type audit (applied V164) are recorded in full in `claude/gyneconomy-version-archive.md` — the backtest under its own heading at the top, the audits under "Palette audit"; their rules live in the design-system doc.

# Part 2 — The long narratives, data audits and retired figures


*The long narrative of every version through 174 (Sep 19, 2026). Frozen: from here on the one-line-per-version log is `claude/gyneconomy-version-history.md`, and this file only gains an entry when a version needs a longer record — a data audit, a rule change, a merge with another session's publish. The app's current rules are in `claude/gyneconomy-app.md`; colour, type and icons in `claude/gyneconomy-design-system.md`.*

## Version 512 — the retired `cycleEndReadings` figures

Removed from the app on Sep 26, 2026 (Keren: "why do we need cycle end readings? If we don't need it, just get rid
of it"). Nothing read the table — the Rates ring and the Feeling/Energy word tiles that consumed it had left the
cycle view several versions earlier. Recorded here so that restoring those tiles is a lookup rather than a research
job. These are the readings as they stood in each closed cycle's LAST month, under the Version 511 dating.

| Cycle | Closed | Fed funds target | Last move | VIX close | ISM Mfg PMI |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dot-Com 1991–2002 | Dec 2002 | 1.25% | −0.50, Nov 6 2002 | 28.62 | 55.2 |
| Housing 2003–2008 | Dec 2008 | 0.00–0.25% | cut to the floor, Dec 16 2008 | 40.00 | 32.4 |
| Big Tech 2009–2018 | Dec 2018 | 2.25–2.50% | +0.25, Dec 20 2018 | 25.42 | 54.1 |
| COVID-19 2019–2022 | Dec 2022 | 4.25–4.50% | +0.50, Dec 15 2022 | 21.67 | 48.4 |

The high-yield OAS is absent for all four on purpose: FRED limits the ICE BofA series to a rolling three-year
window, so those closes are not on record and a closed cycle's Feeling would have read from the VIX alone.

**Sources.** Fed funds and the dates: the Federal Reserve's own open-market operations pages — the [pre-2003
archive](https://www.federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/openmarket_archive.htm) for the Nov 6 2002 cut to 1.25%, the
[current table](https://www.federalreserve.gov/monetarypolicy/openmarket.htm) for 2008, 2018 and 2022. ISM
Manufacturing PMI: the December releases (Dec 2002 revised to 55.2, reported in CNN Money's Feb 3 2003 write-up;
Dec 2008 32.4; Dec 2018 54.1; Dec 2022 48.4). VIX: the close on the last trading day of each December, from a
published year-end table that was **validated before being used** — asked first for 1999, 2007, 2017 and 2021 it
returned 24.64, 22.50, 11.04 and 17.22, which are the four figures the app already held from independent research.


Split out of `claude/gyneconomy-app.md` on Sep 17, 2026 (Version 101) to keep that doc short enough to edit quickly. This file is where the blow-by-blow goes: after each publish, append one short paragraph below with what Keren asked for and what changed. The live doc's Part 1 (current state) and Part 2 (procedure) are the only things that should need hand-editing beyond that — update them only when the current state or the procedure actually changes, not as a matter of habit.

## What the ring showed, Sep 2026 (a snapshot, not maintained)

As of Version 83–99: 2022 Q1–Q3 Summer (inflation at 8–9% while the eight-quarter growth window still held the 2021 rebound), 2022 Q4–2023 Q3 Autumn (disinflation), 2023 Q4–2024 Q1 Summer (prices cooling but still above the range), 2024 Q2–Q4 the Goldilocks Zone (growth up, prices cooling inside the range), 2025 Autumn with one Late Autumn quarter (Q3, CPI 3.0 steady while growth fell), 2026 Late Autumn (stagflation). Note that under Keren's table the red 2022 bleed year is **not** Winter — Winter is deflation, prices below target, and 2022 was the opposite; this was flagged to her before she chose, and she went with the data.

## The backtest (built Version 90, removed from the app in Version 91 — kept here as the record)

Keren asked for an honest take; the first recommendation was to test the framework rather than keep illustrating it, and she said yes; after seeing it she decided the app does not need it ("I don't think I need it"), so the section, its three data arrays and its render are gone from the page — the working files are in the session scratchpad (`backtest/`) and the numbers below stand. It was built with three fixed histories: `cpiYoYEarly` (CPI YoY monthly, Jan 1989–Dec 2020, from FRED CPIAUCSL levels — one decimal before 2007, as BLS published), `gdpQuarterlyYoYEarly` (real GDP YoY, 1988 Q1–2019 Q4, from FRED GDPC1) and `spxMonthlyAvg` (S&P 500 monthly **average** price, Jan 1989–today, from multpl.com). `cpiYoYAll`/`gdpYoYAll` concatenate them with the live arrays without a gap; `seasonHistory` runs `readSeason()` at every quarter end from 1990 Q1 using only data up to that quarter, and gives each quarter the index's change (last month of the quarter vs last month of the previous — a **price** return, before dividends, ≈2 pts/yr, season-neutral); `seasonStats` aggregates. `moonPhase`/`moonShape`/`moonSvg` were hoisted to top level so the dial, the legend and the history share them.

**Result (146 quarters, 1990 Q1–2026 Q2):** Goldilocks Zone +19%/yr, up 86% of quarters (20% of the time); Spring +15%, 81% (14%); Summer +8%, 67% (14%); Autumn +8%, 76% (32%); Late Autumn +2%, 58% (13%), and the quarter after a Late Autumn reading −1.0% on average; Winter +3%, 60% (7%), but the quarter after +4.0%, up 80% of the time — the book's seeding. All quarters +10%/yr, up 74%. Expansion seasons 3.4%/qtr vs contraction 1.4% (t ≈ 1.75); Goldilocks vs Late Autumn t ≈ 1.9. The seasons change every 2.35 quarters on average (62 spells); the worst stretches are 1990 Q1–91 Q1, 2000 Q3–Q4, 2005 Q3–06 Q2, 2007 Q4–08 Q3 (Late Autumn) and 2008 Q4–09 Q3, 2014 Q4–15 Q3, 2020 Q2 (Winter). **Caveats, stated in the (i):** a short history; revised GDP rather than real-time prints; thresholds set in 2026; price-only returns on monthly averages; small samples for Winter (10) and Late Autumn (19). Note the result was computed under the V89 rule; the V91 table changes a handful of cells and would shift the numbers slightly.

## Palette audit (Sep 19, 2026 — proposal only, not applied)

Keren: "one consistent colour palette throughout the app that is both legible and beautiful — feminine, like a design system designed for women who want to invest." Audit published as its own artifact, **Gyneconomy Palette** (https://claude.ai/artifact/B7yhSmxhmDY8r1G21E3yYP), from the Version 162 stylesheet. Findings: ~20 tokens; two greens (`--good` #0ca30c, `--ovulate` #1ea8a3) and three reds (`--critical`, `--bleed-mid`, `--serious`) share jobs; in the light theme most semantic colours fail WCAG AA 4.5:1 as text — rose 3.8, good 3.4, warning 3.1, serious 3.7, teal 2.9, cold 3.4, muted text 3.3 — because paint colours were pressed into service as words in tags/badges/kickers; dark theme passes everywhere; two hard-coded blues/reds in the model table's range bar. The rule proposed: every meaning in three tones — paint (lines/bands, ≥3:1), ink (words, ≥4.5:1), wash (pill grounds). Proposed roles: brand plum (#8a4d97 / ink #5e2a6b; dark #c9a0d6 / #e3c6ea) replacing the blue-purple accent; rose editorial (#c85f8b / ink #a8436f; dark #e08bae / #f0a9c6); up teal (#1ea8a3 / #0f6f6c; dark #3dc2bb / #5fd6cf) replacing both greens; down coral-red (#d63a2e / #b33d29; dark #eb4839 / #f28a78) replacing all three reds for "critical"; caution amber (#c98500 / #8a5a00; dark #eda100 / #f2c24d); serious coral (#e2714f / #b0431f; dark #ec835a / #f5a385); cold periwinkle (#5b7fd6 / #3c5cb5; dark #7f9ce6 / #a6baf0); muted text darkened to #7d7680 (4.5). Keren chose plum and periwinkle; applied in Version 163 (below). **Type audit (same day, not applied):** three faces, well cast — Fraunces italic for the voice (season and cycle names, quotes), Public Sans for UI/body, IBM Plex Mono for figures — but 26 distinct px sizes (half-pixel steps from 10 to 17), 700 on 41 rules (bold is the default, so nothing stands out), and the mono on ~40 rules including every kicker, tag and label, which reads as a terminal. Proposed: keep the faces; one scale (11 / 12.5 / 14 / 16 / 20 / 26 / 40, mono data 11 / 14 / 22 / 30); 600 for UI, 700 reserved for figures; body 15px on the Content tab; mono for numbers and axis ticks only, labels/kickers/tags to Public Sans. Awaiting Keren.

## Version-by-version

**Version 246.** Keren removed both Calendar-tab captions — "Newest first. Open a cycle to see it the way the Cycle tab shows the current one…" under the cycle list, and "Each cycle laid flat: its seasons in order, in the dial's colours…" under the season strips. Both narrated a picture that explains itself: the list is visibly newest-first with the market's years drawn on each ring, and the strips are visibly seasons laid end to end. The kicker above each still names what it is and the (i) still carries the detail for anyone who wants it. `.calendar-caption` was deleted with them, nothing else wearing it. Same instinct as V234 and V239: **prose that describes a visual is the same redundancy as a caption under a number that already says itself.**

---

**Version 250.** Keren: "The spacing is off in the cycle page." It was, and measuring every gap down the tab rather than eyeballing the CSS found three faults — one bad enough to explain the whole complaint on its own.

1. **The five signs were touching.** `#signs-list` was a plain `<div>`, so the Coincident and Lagging rows stacked at **0px** and their heads sat 2px above the first row. The top half of the page had the V248 rhythm and the bottom half had none at all. Introduced in V247, when those five rows moved out of two drawers into a container that never got the flex gap.
2. **The phone ran a different scale.** The ≤640px rule re-declared the head's margin as 20px, making 30px before a section on a phone against 32 on desktop.
3. **The dial sat 22px above the lead chart** — the tab panel's own 16px gap plus `#today-analysis`'s 6px margin, neither number on the scale.

All three are the same mistake in different places: **a spacing number set somewhere other than the one place that owns it.** The fix is structural rather than cosmetic — `.tab-panel[data-tab="cycle"]`, `#today-analysis` and `#signs-list` all carry the same 10px gap, so the rhythm cannot break where one container ends and the next begins, and the head's margin is one value at every width. Measured again at 390, 430 and 1280: **10 / 32 / 12** at every boundary on all three. The other three tabs keep their own 16px gap, checked, since the rule is scoped to the Cycle panel.

**The lesson worth carrying:** a spacing scale is not a set of numbers, it is a set of numbers *plus* the rule that every container in the flow uses them. V248 declared the scale and V249 added a container outside it; neither version was wrong on its own, and the page was broken by the pair. **When a new container joins this tab, it inherits the 10px gap or it does not go in.**

**Version 249.** Keren, with a fintech dashboard as reference, offered three ideas: a chart open on landing rather than behind a collapse, small metrics paired two-to-a-row, and containers on a horizontal scroll. One shipped; the reasoning on the other two is the useful part.

**Shipped — the Temperature chart on the landing page**, directly under the dial (`#slot-temp.lead-chart`). She wondered whether it should be GDP; Temperature is the better choice on three counts: it is **monthly** where GDP is quarterly, so it is the thing that actually changes between visits; it is the **season's own instrument**, since the season is read from where CPI sits and which way growth is going; and its hot/warm/cold colouring says something at a glance that a line of GDP does not. It is **moved, not copied** — the Temperature row keeps its figure and its sentence, its drawer keeps the reference range and sources, and the chart exists in exactly one place. The Growth chart stays inside the GDP drawer: two charts on landing would rebuild the wall that V247–248 took down. A cycle opened from the Calendar still takes both charts back into the cycle view — verified.

**Not shipped — two containers in a row.** The reference pairs *stat tiles*: a label, a number, a delta, no prose. Since V247 every row here carries a sentence, and pairing two sentences across a phone would undo exactly the air Keren had just asked for. The move is right on **desktop**, where the width is free, and it is already drawn that way in the restructure preview (two panels across). If a paired row is ever wanted on the phone, it needs a reading with no sentence — and the app currently has none, by design.

**Not shipped — horizontal scroll.** It suits a shelf of many similar things that the reader browses (the reference uses it for contacts). Gyneconomy's lists are short and are meant to be *scanned*, not browsed: five cycles, ten readings. A rail would hide part of a list that currently fits, and hidden content is where things go to be missed. The one place it could earn its keep later is the Calendar's five cycles as a gallery — worth revisiting only if that list grows.

**Version 248.** Keren, tightening V247: "we only need to keep Structural, remove Where she stands; Leading; and Coincident, which is real time; and Lagging, of course. And each category should be inside its own head container, but it needs to look elegant — or we can do a title and a beautiful underline. And make the spaces even across the app and elegant. Not too white, not too narrow."

**One word per section.** The poetic second line went: the heads are now **Structural, Leading, Coincident, Lagging**, four terms from the book's own framework, which is the only vocabulary this page needs. "Where she stands" was a caption on a caption, and the tracked kickers above each head went with it.

**The underline, not the container.** She offered both and the underline is right: a box around each group would have been a fourth level of enclosure — page, section, card, row — and this app has spent the day removing enclosure, not adding it. `.sect-rule` is a 1px hairline in `--border-strong` under the title, with a 34×3px `--accent` cap at its left end: a section mark as print sets one, separating without boxing.

**One spacing scale, and this is the part that generalises.** The old rhythm was accidental — a 16px flex gap plus 30px of head padding made 46px above a head and 22px below, and nothing else matched either. The tab now has exactly three numbers: **10px between rows, 32px before a section head, 12px after its rule**, verified in the browser rather than trusted (`gapRow: 10, gapBefore: 32`). Row padding evened to 18px, and 16/15 on phones. **Rule for anything added here: don't introduce a fourth number — if something needs more air, move the whole scale.**

**And the hub loses a line.** "Instead of writing 'This season' under each season in the main wheel, just put the chevron next to Inflation." The theme line now IS the link — **Inflation ›** — so the hub says the date, the season, and one clickable phase line instead of four rows. The older `.details-link` rule inside the hub had to be scoped `:not(.who)`, since it sat later in the file and would otherwise have repainted the phase line in Public Sans.

**Version 247.** The restructure, built — and built on Keren's correction rather than my proposal. My preview deleted the Insights strip and left ten collapsed rows; she rejected it: "I think moving the insights into the drawers is a mistake. The entire screen looks too condensed. I want to air it out… make insights the main feature of the current cycle and have more info, like in Desire, Effort and Pulse." She was right, and the diagnosis worth keeping is this: **my version turned the page into a directory** — ten closed doors, nothing readable without opening something. Short is not the same as airy.

So Insights did not move into the drawers; it **became the page**. What shipped:

- **The strip is gone** — markup, render, `kpiTileHtml` and all the `.benchmarks`/`.kpi-*` CSS. Nothing was lost: its historical comparisons ('07 pre-crisis, '20 COVID, the dot-com peak) were already written into the notes of the markers they compare.
- **Every row carries a sentence** (`.subject-say`, hidden again when the drawer opens, since the body then says it at length). This is the ingredient I had underweighted and she had spotted: "41.0×" informs nobody, "just shy of the richest valuation on record" does — and the app already had that line for every marker, sitting behind a chevron. Sources: `longCycleImpressionShort`, `valuation.shortImpression`, `sentiment.shortImpression`, each card's `shortCaption`, and two composed from live values for GDP growth and the yield curve.
- **Section heads** (`.sect-head`): the leading/coincident/lagging spine lifted out of the row eyebrows and set in Cormorant italic at 32px with a tiny tracked kicker above — *Where she stands · Leading · Coincident · Lagging*. The "Leading ·" eyebrows came off the two rows that had them, the head now saying it.
- **Coincident and Lagging unpacked into five subjects**, built in JS from the same `coincident`/`lagging` arrays so there is one source of truth: the row's face is the card's face, the drawer's body is `cardDetailHtml(ind)`. `#slot-temp` stays in static markup because `placeCharts()` runs before this list is built — the Temperature subject adopts it after.
- **Five new marks, drawn here rather than in the DSM** (Keren: "add placeholder icons; if they look good we don't need Lovable. Keep it simple."): **flame** for Desire, **cog** for Effort with the teeth kept short and heavy so it does not read as a sun, **heartbeat** for Pulse, **person** for Activity, **thermometer** for Temperature with the mercury heavier than the glass. Proofed as a set at 40px and at their real 25px, beside the existing four, before any of them was wired in.

**The trade, accepted knowingly:** the page went from 1,825px to 2,581px. Ten open readings take more room than ten closed rows. That is what air costs, and it is the trade she asked for — a scroll you can read beats a list you have to open.

**Still open:** the editorial treatment itself (flat colour fields instead of white cards, the season as a full-bleed cover) and the desktop rail, both drawn in the preview artifact and neither shipped. On desktop the length costs nothing, because the width absorbs it — which is the strongest argument for building the rail next.

## Open proposal: restructuring the Cycle tab (Sep 19, 2026)

Keren: "I feel like the Cycle tab is a bit cluttered. I also like how the Desire/Effort/Pulse containers look… we need to strive to an editorial look and feel, because mind you, the target audience is women." A side-by-side preview was built as its own artifact — **https://claude.ai/artifact/FwBgmfCgD6YfZAtRAHzM2b** (“Cycle Tab Restructure”), today's layout against the proposal, using the app's own tokens and faces. **Moves 1–3 shipped as Version 247; the editorial treatment and the desktop rail have not.**

**The measured diagnosis:** the Cycle tab is 1,825px on a phone — the dial card 452, the Insights strip 459, the seven drawers ~595. **The clutter is duplication, not density.** Economic power, Valuation and the VIX each appear in the Insights strip AND in a drawer one thumb-scroll below.

**Move 1 — delete the Insights strip.** 459px, a quarter of the page, saying what the rows below say. Its historical comparisons ('07 pre-crisis, '20 COVID, the dot-com peak) move into the panels they belong to. Needs no artwork; can ship alone.

**Move 2 — one row shape, ten times.** Five rows carry a mark, a name and one reading; Coincident and Lagging carry a status dot and a spill of chips over two lines, and they are the rows whose contents Keren likes best. Promote Desire, Effort, Pulse, Activity and Temperature to rows of their own, each opening to the card that already exists. **Blocked on five marks.**

**Move 3 — the framework becomes the spine.** Leading/coincident/lagging currently sit as eyebrows on four rows and are missing from three, which is what makes the list look arbitrary. Lift them into section heads set in Cormorant italic rather than tracked capitals — that is also the main editorial move, since it replaces dashboard kickers with a magazine's section heads. Needs no artwork.

**Not changing:** the dial card, the drawers, or charts living inside them. This is the page's skeleton, not its contents.

**Version 245.** Keren: "Your call — make the piggy bank yourself." With the Lovable workspace out of credits, the mark was drawn here in the design system's language rather than by it: `piggyBankSvg(level)`, a body ellipse with a snout, an ear, a coin slot, two legs and an eye, and the fill a band clipped to the body so the outline never moves between levels — the battery's logic, which she liked for Economic power. **What had to go: the tail.** Its curl turned to mush below about 40px and cost more than it carried; everything else survives at 24px, checked at 44, 27 and 24 side by side before wiring in. The level comes from how many of the two gauges are flagged — neither is cheap, one is fair, both is richly priced — so it is today at **full**. `dot("valuation", …)` became `iconMark`.

**The colour decision, which she left to me.** Her instinct was green for richly priced: a full bank reads as abundance, and as a picture of stored value that is the obvious choice. I kept the app's existing rule instead — **the drawing says how much, the colour says how comfortable** — so a full bank is red. The alternative would have put a green piggy bank two drawers away from a broad smile on red, both meaning "expensive market", which is the kind of internal disagreement this app has spent a day removing. It is also the honest reading: a market at its record price is good news for whoever already owns it and bad news for the next decade of returns, and the app has taken the second view throughout.

**Open:** rebuild it as `src/components/gyneconomy/icons/PiggyBankMark.tsx` when the Lovable workspace has credits again, and re-import, exactly as the faces came back in V244. The brief sent was: one component, `level={0|1|2}`, 24 grid, outline body at 1.7 in `currentColor`, fill clipped to the body at low opacity, `aria-hidden`, no hard-coded colour.

**Version 244.** Keren, on the faces of Version 243: "I don't like the icons you produced. Use Lovable and put the icons in the appropriate place in the DSM. Make the DSM simple, but I need good smiley faces." So the set was rebuilt in the Gyneconomy design system and imported path for path, the same discipline as SproutMark in V215: **`src/components/gyneconomy/icons/SentimentFace.tsx`** — one component, one `level` prop, five named bands. **Change it there first, then here.**

What the DSM's version does better, and worth carrying into any future mark: the eyes are **1.55 radius and sit higher** (mine were 1.15), and the **mouth is stroked heavier than the face** — 2.1 against 1.9 — so the expression survives at 24px, where an even-weight mouth reads as a smudge. The mouths also commit: the widest frown and broadest smile run nearly the full width of the face. The agent drew, rendered all five at true 24px on desktop, phone, light and dark, judged the two risky adjacent pairs side by side, found Greed too close to Extreme greed and narrowed it before finishing — the same check that fixed my own first pass, done properly.

The DSM also gained what Keren asked for structurally: **`src/components/gyneconomy/icons/`** as the one home for marks, with `LotusMark` and `SproutMark` moved into it and their imports updated. No index file, no icon factory, no icon-library dependency — one file per mark, which is the "simple" she asked for.

**Not done, blocked:** a three-level **piggy bank** for the Valuation panel (Keren: "a green piggy bank would be rich priced and the red poorly priced, or whatever wording we use"). The first attempt ran out of session before writing any files; the retry was refused — **the Lovable workspace is out of credits**. The brief is written and ready: `PiggyBankMark level={0|1|2}`, body identical across the three with only the fill changing, 24 grid, `currentColor`, outline body at 1.7 with the fill clipped to it at low opacity. **Open question to settle before building it:** Keren's instinct is green for richly priced, which reads as "the bank is full". The app's convention everywhere else is that colour carries RISK, not quantity — which is why Valuation's tag is "Richly priced" in red today, and why Extreme greed smiles on red. Green for rich would invert that. Worth deciding once, for both panels.

**Version 243.** Keren: "Let's work on the icon of the sentiment. I want the sad face to a smiley face range," with a five-face gauge as reference. The Sentiment row had worn a progress `ring()`, which was wrong twice: a ring reads as *how full*, and this scale has no full — both ends are extremes. **`moodFaceSvg(face)`** draws five faces on the app's own 24 grid (outline, `currentColor`, round caps, 1.8 stroke), reference art not copied — a 9.1 circle, two solid 1.15 eyes (hollow ones vanish at 27px), and **only the mouth changing**, so the set reads as one face moving rather than five drawings: `MOOD_MOUTHS[0–4]`, with the greed mouths the mirrors of the fear ones. `moodFrom` gained a `face` index; the row renders through `iconMark`, the mechanism the sprout and the battery already use.

**A first pass was too subtle** — Extreme fear was barely distinguishable from Fear, and Greed from Extreme greed — caught by rendering all five side by side in a throwaway proof sheet (`faceproof.html`) at both 36px and 27px before touching the app. The curves were widened and deepened at the extremes and pulled in at the middle two. **Worth repeating for any icon set: render the whole set together, at its real size, before wiring any of it in.**

**The one deliberate oddity:** the face is the mood and the wash under it is the RISK, so Extreme greed wears a broad smile on the same red as Extreme fear's frown. That is the contrarian reading the whole panel is built on, and it is the one place in the app where a smile is a warning. (Her reference art ramps red-sad to green-happy, a plain good/bad scale; that was not followed, and the divergence was flagged to her.)

**Version 242.** Keren: "Twenty-nine out of a hundred is twenty-nine percent. Also under the CBOE VIX, you don't really need to write close — of course it's the close." The second is plainly right: every price on the page is a closing price and no other row says so, so the word went. The first is her V230 call applied again, and it went in everywhere the figure appears — the collapsed row's unit, the lead row's flag, the meter's band label (45–55%) and the note's bands and look-backs.

**Flagged to her rather than argued:** this is the one place the app's notation now differs from its source. CNN publishes the index as a bare number — every outside page she checked said "29", not "29%" — and matching outside sources is precisely the standard that drove Versions 232 and 237. Economic power's percent is on firmer ground, being a share of a defined full charge; the Fear & Greed index is an ordinal composite that happens to run 0–100. A bare "29 · Fear & Greed", with the "/100" simply dropped, would satisfy her objection to the notation without inventing a unit the source does not use. One republish either way; hers stands unless she says otherwise.

**Version 241.** Keren: "Is CNN the most credible source we can use?" Two questions in one, and they have different answers. For the Fear & Greed Index specifically CNN is not a reporter but the originator — the index is theirs — so citing CNN for it is exactly like citing Cboe for the VIX or Shiller for CAPE, and there is no better source. The index ITSELF, though, is a media product rather than an official statistic: the exact formula is not disclosed, there is no downloadable history, and the method could change without notice. It is the only figure on the page that cannot be reproduced from a primary series, while sitting in the same visual grammar as the VIX from Cboe, the spread from ICE and CPI from the BLS. The (i) now says so, and tells the reader to treat it as a widely watched gauge rather than a measurement. The alternatives with a research pedigree — the AAII bull-bear survey (weekly, 1987–) and Investors Intelligence (1963–) — were put to her and not taken: recognisability is the point for a book companion, which is why she asked for the conventional index in the first place.

**A craft note:** the caveat first went in as one added sentence and `factsFrom` split it into three, pushing the popup to eight facts, past the five or six Keren set in V227. The whole note was rewritten as exactly five sentences so it lands as five. **When adding to a note rendered by `factsFrom`, count the sentences, not the words.**

**Version 240.** Keren: "Put rates — meaning the Fed funds rate — in the appropriate container. I don't need to see it standing alone." The right container was never in doubt: the Lagging drawer's **Temperature** card is "Inflation & monetary policy", its caption already recounted the September hike, and the rate is the answer to the temperature — the lever pulled after, not ahead. So the rate became that card's stat and the vitals strip, the last thing standing between the dial and the drawers, is gone with it: `#vitals-strip`, its two CSS rules, the Rates ring render, `vitalTileHtml`, and `placeCharts`'s `insertBefore(v)` (now an `appendChild`). `m.end.fedFunds` went with it, so `cycleEndReadings` is now unread — kept as editorial history, but nothing renders it.

Two small general additions rather than one special case: **`aux` accepts an array**, so a card can carry more than one stat in its detail modal (Temperature has Core CPI and the Fed funds target), and **`faceAux`** puts one stat on a minimal card's own face, between the caption and the Details link. That second one existed because the first attempt put the rate in `aux` alone — which only ever renders inside the detail modal — and buried it behind an (i) instead of housing it, caught in testing before publish.

**Where the Cycle tab has ended up after 228–240:** the dial, the Temperature chart, the Growth chart, and the drawers. Every free-floating tile and ring under the dial is gone, each removed for the same reason — it restated something a drawer said better, or it stood alone when it belonged inside something.

**Version 239.** Keren: "Drop the Ripening action and Fear feeling below the rates. Also, the Ripening action across the app, you can remove it. It's not helpful." Both tiles and the whole Action concept came out. Action was the book's farming word for each season — Seeding, Growing, Ripening, Harvest — printed beside a season that the dial, the hub, the header and the season reading already name four other ways, so it added a word rather than a fact. Feeling had become the Fear & Greed word from the drawer two inches below it, said twice. Removed: the `#words-strip` div and its grid rules, `wordTile` and the whole `.word-tile` CSS block, `actionIcons`/`actionIconSvg`/`moodIcons`/`moodIconSvg`, the `action:` and `icon:` fields on all six `wheelMeta` seasons, the `icon` field on `moodFrom`'s five returns, the trailing farming sentence in three `seasonReading` economy texts ("Ripening: the crop is at its fullest and the heat is on", "Harvest, late: what was grown is being taken in under worsening weather", "Seeding: the field lies bare, and what comes next is already in the ground"), the Content caption's "action: … seeding, growing, ripening, harvest" clause, and the menu guide's Rates/Action/Feeling paragraph, rewritten to point at the Sentiment drawer instead. The **Rates ring is now the only thing under the dial**, and a cycle opened from the Calendar shows the dial, the charts and Rates — verified on the Dot-Com Cycle, no errors. Also on her word, the Economic power panel's lead row becomes **Power supply** (was Power in reserve). The build asserted both directions — seven symbols gone, seven that must survive (`seasonTitle`, `energyFromReserve`, `sproutSvg`, `batteryIconSvg`, `sp500AnnualReturns`, `vitals-strip`, `fearGreed`) — after V236's over-wide slice deleted 27 unrelated lines.

**The pattern across 228–239, worth naming:** the vitals strip has now lost every word tile it had — Energy (V229), Feeling and Action (V239) — and each time for the same reason: the word restated something the page said elsewhere, usually better. The tiles were an attractive shape looking for content. What survived is the ring, which carries a figure nothing else shows.

**Version 238.** Keren: "Drop the margin debt. Let's stick with the three we have right now, and if I want more in the future, I'll add." Done, and it was the right row to lose: it was the only bar on the page whose scale was not anchored to published record extremes, because finra.org is refused by the egress proxy, and a caveat that has to travel with a row is a sign the row is not ready. The marker, the FINRA source and its sources-page regex all went together; nothing else referenced it. Sentiment is now three rows — the published Fear & Greed index, and the two markets it is mostly built on, the VIX and the high-yield spread, each measured here against its own record. The panel's impression was rewritten around the one tension worth naming: the headline reads Fear because it is mostly momentum and breadth and the market has been sliding for a month, while the two markets underneath read almost no fear priced in against forty years of history.
