Both on screen, each measuring what it says. **If margin debt comes back,** it needs FINRA's full monthly series first — Keren can attach the spreadsheet; the figures as of this build were $1.454T (Aug 2026), $1.060T a year earlier, +37.2%.

**Version 237.** Keren checked the number against a second outside source and came back: "it still says 29, fear. What am I missing?" Nothing. The page said 86, Extreme greed. The arithmetic behind both was sound — ours measured the VIX and the high-yield spread against their RECORD extremes, CNN measures seven components against their own RECENT ranges, and only about one and a half of the seven overlap (even the VIX leg differs: CNN scores it against its own 50-day average, so 14.81 reads roughly neutral there and 78 here) — but soundness was not the issue. A number that has to be defended every time someone checks it is the wrong number to show, and she had now checked it twice. Asked how to resolve it, she said: **"I want the conventional Fear & Greed index."**

So the composite is gone — `greedFrom`, `logPct` and the whole log-normalisation argument deleted — and **CNN's published index takes its place as a dated reading**, carried exactly like the VIX close, the CAPE, the Buffett figure and the margin-debt print: `fearGreed = {value:29, label:"Fear", asOf:"Sep 19 2026", weekAgo:33, monthAgo:55}`, a REFRESH item, with CNN's own band word copied rather than derived. `moodFrom(score)` now takes a score instead of computing one, the meter's neutral band becomes CNN's own 45–55, the collapsed row reads "29 /100 · Fear & Greed · Fear", and the (i) sets out the seven components and says plainly why the index can read Fear while the VIX is calm and spreads are near their tightest ever: five of the seven are momentum and breadth. Margin debt's "context, not in the score" caveat went with the score; its scale caveat survives as one clause of its note. CNN moved from a contrast in the sources list to the source of the figure.

**The cost, accepted:** the index is today's only, so a closed cycle opened from the Calendar shows Action alone — no Feeling tile (verified on the Dot-Com Cycle). Same shape as Energy since V228. A VIX-at-the-close fallback would bring the word back for past cycles and is the obvious move if Keren wants it.

**The lesson, worth more than the code:** V231–236 built, refined and then dismantled a bespoke sentiment composite over six versions, and the whole exercise was answering a question nobody had asked. The name should have been the tell at V231 — calling something "Fear & Greed" when a famous index of that name publishes a different number daily is a collision you cannot explain your way out of. **Before computing a figure, check whether a conventional published one exists; if it does, carry it and cite it.** The three markers beneath it are still ours to measure, and that is where original work belongs.

**Version 236.** Keren: "Drop the emotions. Just — let's use conventions. Fear and greed. We don't want to overcomplicate things. They are already so complicated." Out came the whole cycle of market emotions: `MOOD_WHEEL`, `MOOD_ICON`, `moodStateFor`, `moodPairAt`, `moodNote`, the `stops` array on `fgMeter`, and — because its only job was choosing between the two emotions that shared a level — `marketTrend` and `marketRising` with it. `moodFrom(vix, oas)` now returns the **five conventional bands**: Extreme fear ≤ 24, Fear ≤ 44, Neutral ≤ 55, Greed ≤ 75, Extreme greed above. Both extremes are `critical` and the middle is `good`, which keeps the contrarian logic — it is the ends that carry information — without asking the reader to learn anything. Today: 86, **Extreme greed**. The drawer's unit becomes `/100` (it was "fear → greed", which the tag then said again) and the Feeling tile and the (i)'s closing sentence follow.

Worth recording plainly, because it was five versions of work: the emotional cycle was a good idea in the wrong place. It is genuinely the right frame for a chapter of the book, where there is room to teach fourteen words and the level-versus-position distinction that makes them mean something. On a panel whose job is to report two market figures to someone checking in, it was vocabulary between the reader and the number. Keren said "they are already so complicated" about the markets, not the app, and that is the test that should have been applied at V231: does this help someone read the number, or does it ask them to read something else first. **Build bug caught in testing:** removing the direction block by slicing between two anchors deleted 27 lines in between — `energyFromReserve`, the mood and action icon tables, `sproutSvg`, `batteryIconSvg` and the whole `sp500AnnualReturns` series — and the page threw on load. Fixed with a bounded regex plus an assertion that those four symbols survive. **Rule: never delete a range by slicing between two `index()` anchors; match the block itself and assert what must remain.**

**Version 235.** Keren, undoing the one indulgence of 231–234 in a single message: "I think the design of Fear & Greed should be like the VIX and the high-yield spread, inside the container. And visually it should look like their bar. It doesn't need to be different. Instead of maximum opportunity and maximum risk, just write fear or greed — I'll deduce myself what it means — and you can put a hover so if I hover over one bar I can see what it means. Also drop 'read as compensation, not as health', it's too much text. And I don't think greedy and fearful are right descriptions for the high-yield spread. I think tight and wide are the words we need to use. We need to use the conventions." Every point correct. The page already had a shape for a composite reading — the Economic power panel's lead row — and building a bespoke component for this one was the mistake; a ring in 231–232 and a coloured segmented bar in 233–234 were both answers to a question the design system had already settled.

So **Fear & Greed is a lead row again** (`.stress-row`, `fgMeter`) wearing the same `meterHtml` bar as every other marker, its ends reading **Fear … Neutral 35–65 … Greed** — CNN's own scale words, which is the convention. `moodBarHtml` and every `.mood-bar*` rule are deleted. The fourteen emotions survive as **`stops`**, the one genuinely new idea and now a general `meterHtml` option: an array of names rendered as invisible absolutely-positioned hover zones over the track (`.rbar-stop`, 7px of overhang top and bottom so they are easy to hit), so a bar can name its positions without looking any different from a bar that cannot. The eight stops come from `moodPairAt`, each still carrying the cycle's two emotions for that level — "Anxiety (market falling) · Thrill (market rising)" — so the whole wheel is one hover away and costs no height at all. Any future bar with meaningful positions should use `stops` rather than grow a component.

On the words: **Tight … Wide** replaces Greedy … Fearful on the high-yield spread, because that is what the market calls a spread and the app should speak the market's language, not its own metaphor, on a row whose whole point is a market figure. (The greed reading has not gone anywhere — it is in the note and in the composite that the spread feeds.) The row's sub-line drops "read as compensation, not as health" and goes back to plain "ICE BofA option-adjusted, Sep 18 2026"; the explanation belongs behind the (i), which V233 already rewrote to hold both readings, not on the face of the row. **Standing rule from this one:** a marker row's end words are the convention for that instrument — tight/wide for spreads, complacent/panicked for the VIX — never the app's editorial framing; the framing lives in the note.

**Version 234.** Keren: "I don't need this: Anxiety 86 · market falling." The word was on screen three times — the drawer's summary row two inches above (visible the whole time the drawer is open), the Feeling tile in the vitals strip, and then again in Cormorant under the bar. So `.mood-read` and its three rules are gone; the bar is its head, its two end captions and the mark, and the mark's `title` carries word, score and direction for anyone who hovers it. Third time in this session that the right answer was "the app already says this once" (the Energy tile in V229, the drawers' context lines in V232, this). **Worth remembering as a habit:** when a new component is added above an existing summary, check what the summary already says before writing a caption under it.

**Version 233.** Keren: "I don't like the additional cycle design. Just make it like a bar, a colourful bar. And if I hover over I see the different emotions, but you label the current emotion, and that's it. It's enough." Right on both counts. A second ring competed with the season dial for the same job and the same corner of the eye, and it spent a lot of height to say one word. **The bar** (`moodBarHtml`, `moodPairAt`, `.mood-bar*`) is the Fear & Greed axis laid flat: maximum opportunity on the left in teal through to maximum risk on the right in red, eight stops with 2px of surface between them, the mark at the score's own position — which a loop could not do honestly — and the emotion written once beneath in Cormorant italic with the score and the market's direction beside it. Each stop's `title` carries the cycle's **two** names for that level, one for a market going down and one for a market coming back up (86 is Anxiety falling and Thrill rising), which is the wheel's real lesson and the app's own: a level is not a position. The ring, `moodRingSvg` and all its CSS are gone. Then, since the coloured bar carries the score, the table's Fear & Greed lead row was saying 86 twice an inch apart, so the row was dropped and its title and (i) moved onto the bar's head (`fgDetailHtml`); `fgMeter` went with it. Economic power keeps its lead row — it has no bar of its own — so Sentiment is the one panel whose composite lives above the table rather than in it.

Keren also challenged the credit reading: "I think you misdiagnosed the yield spread — narrowing spreads mean strong corporate health and economic optimism." She is right about what a narrowing spread means, and the Sentiment row had not said it; a reader landing there saw "Greedy ▼ 2.70%" in red with no hint that the same number is tagged green two drawers down. The note now **leads with her reading** — lenders see little default risk, companies borrow cheaply, which is why the Desire card reads the identical figure as high risk appetite — then says why this panel asks the other half of the question (what an investor is PAID for the risk, and how much cushion sits before conditions change), and lets one fact carry the argument: the tightest spread ever recorded was **June 2007**, fourteen months before Lehman. It also concedes the honest limit — spreads were tight 2004–2007 and 2013–2019 and most of those years were fine, so tight spreads are a condition, not a signal. The row's sub-line now says "read as compensation, not as health", and the Desire card's caption gained a sentence pointing at the other reading so the green tag and the red flag never look like a contradiction. **The rule this establishes:** where two panels render one figure, each must name the question it is asking, and neither may imply the other is wrong.

**Version 232.** Keren, checking the app against the world: "the current Fear & Greed index is 29, and it sits on fear", then "the VIX is 14.81." Both correct, and the gap between 29 and this page's 86 turned out not to be an error in either. CNN measures each of its seven components against its own RECENT range — the index against its 125-day average, new highs against new lows, the VIX against its 50-day average, stocks against bonds over 20 days — so it reads how sentiment has MOVED; this page measures two components against their RECORD extremes, so it reads how much fear is priced in AT ALL. On Sep 18–19 2026 both were true: almost no fear in prices (86) and sentiment falling hard (CNN 55 → 33 → 29). The (i) now says this explicitly, because the contrast is the most educational thing on the panel, and CNN is listed as a source for the contrast rather than for the score.

It also exposed a real bug. The wheel's side came from `marketRising()` reading `sp500AnnualReturns` — the year-to-date return, +12.5% — a figure that cannot turn until the year does. The market was up 14.8% over twelve months and **down 0.75% over the last month**, with the S&P lower on the week, the Dow off more than 1.5% and breadth narrow while the 10-year sat near 5%. So the direction became a dated one-month trend (`marketTrend = {pct:-0.75, asOf:"Sep 18 2026"}`, a refresh item), and with it the reading moved from **Thrill to Anxiety** — one step off the top, on the falling side, which is where CNN's 29 and this page's 86 agree. That is the same level-versus-direction mistake the app has now made three times, one layer up each time: the growth line coloured by level, the power panel named against its number, and now the wheel's side read off the wrong window. Data also moved to the Sep 18 close: VIX 14.81 (−4.1%), S&P 7,650.50, Dow 51,682.64. And on Keren's word — "I don't need this at the top, I already have the data below the cycle" — the Sentiment and Valuation drawers' context lines were dropped; they only re-listed the rows beneath them.

**Version 231.** Keren, unprompted and correct: "do the Buffett indicator and the Shiller CAPE belong to investor sentiment? I'm not sure — I think it's valuations, and I think sentiment is only the VIX, unless you know another KPI that is important." The app already half-agreed with her: the Insights strip carried Valuation and Market fear as separate tiles while the drawer below folded them together with the Buffett indicator under one tag. The argument for splitting is horizon, not taxonomy — sentiment mean-reverts in weeks and is read contrarian; valuation predicts a decade and is famously useless over twelve months (CAPE passed 30 in 2017 and the market rose for four more years) — and one drawer under one tag made them look like one timescale. Shiller's own thesis, that valuation IS sentiment crystallised into price, was put to her as the fair counter-argument: a good argument in a book, where you have paragraphs, and the wrong cut on a dashboard, where the reader gets a tag and a colour. `psychology` therefore became two objects: **`sentiment`** (VIX, high-yield spread, margin debt) keeping the Leading drawer, and **`valuation`** (Buffett, CAPE) in a new drawer placed beside Economic power, both being slow, structural, and not readings of how she feels.

Keren then sent the cycle-of-market-emotions chart twice — first as a sine wave, then the ring version, "actually I think this one is better" — and the ring is right, because a ring is the app's own grammar. **The mood ring** (`MOOD_WHEEL`, `moodRingSvg`, `.mood-*`): fourteen emotions clockwise from **Euphoria** at the top, captioned *maximum risk*, seven steps down the falling side to **Despondency** at the bottom, captioned *maximum opportunity*, and seven back up the rising side. Colour is a diverging ramp by distance from the top — critical, serious, warning, teal — so both extremes read as the story and the middle reads as quiet; the season dial's palette is never borrowed. The current mark is filled and its word sits in the middle in Cormorant italic, the hub's own pattern, with the score beneath it; every mark names itself on hover.

**The score.** `greedFrom(vix, oas)` returns 0–100, 0 extreme fear, 100 extreme greed, from two legs — the VIX (equity fear) and the high-yield spread (credit fear, the same figure the Desire card renders, never a second source) — each measured against its own published record extremes, inverted, then averaged. One methodological fix worth recording: both are normalised on a **log** scale (`logPct`), because both series are log-normal and a straight line against the VIX's once-ever 82.69 would score almost any calm day as extreme greed. Today: VIX leg 76.2, credit leg 94.8, **score 86**. `moodFrom(vix, oas, rising)` adds the market's own direction (`marketRising`, off `sp500AnnualReturns`) because a level is not a position — 50 climbing is Relief, 50 falling is Desperation — and that is faithful to the chart, which is drawn along the price curve. 86 with the market rising is one step short of the top: **Thrill**. The Feeling word tile now reads this same model rather than the old `feelingFrom`, so the tile, the ring, the drawer's row and the panel's lead row are one reading — the same consolidation V229 did for Energy.

**Margin debt** went in as a row and deliberately NOT into the score: $1.454T in Aug 2026 against $1.060T a year earlier, +37.2%, with the documented note that leverage growing faster than 30–40% a year marked the late stage of 1998–2000, 2006–2007 and 2020–2021. FINRA's full monthly history is not reachable from the build — finra.org is refused by the egress proxy — so its scale is not anchored to published record extremes the way every other meter on the page is, and a figure that cannot be normalised honestly should not move a number the rest of the page leans on. Its sub-line says "context, not in the score". **Open:** source the FINRA series properly and fold it in as a third leg.

**Also:** the VIX's `optimal:{lte:20}` was wrong in kind — a low VIX is not good news, it is complacency — so it became a usual band of 13–20 with its ends read **Complacent … Panicked**; the spread's read **Greedy … Fearful**, margin debt's **Deleveraging … Piling on**, and valuation's **Fair … Rich**, which required `meterHtml`'s `ends` (added V229 for one branch) to apply on all three branches. The VIX note's claim that it is "formally a leading indicator" because the University of Michigan survey is an LEI component was removed: the ten components were checked against the Conference Board's own release and Michigan is not among them (the tenth is the Conference Board's *average consumer expectations for business conditions*), and in any case it would not have made the VIX one. **Bug caught in build:** the mood block was first placed above `sp500AnnualReturns`, which `marketRising` reads — a var, hoisted as undefined — the same class of ordering mistake as V216's `growthWord`; moved below the returns table. Verified at 390 and 1280 in both themes, no page errors, and the score re-derived by hand in Python against the page's own output.

**Version 230.** Keren, in four words: "twenty-five out of a hundred is twenty-five percent." Correct, and the battery settles it — a battery reads 25%, never 25/100. The `/100` was the old stress index's notation carried over out of habit; Economic power is a percentage of a defined full charge, so it is written as one in all four places it shows (the drawer's row, the panel's lead row, the meter's zone label `≥ 70% (ample reserve)`, and the three Insights figures, which gained `unit:"%"` on the existing `kpiTileHtml` point shape — no new markup). The (i) gained the sentence that keeps the percent honest: a full charge is every one of the five markers at the best value the United States has actually recorded, so this is a percentage of her own best, not a share of anything measured out in the economy.

**Version 229.** Keren, the morning after 228: "instead of financial stress let's call it **economic power**, because we are talking on a nation level, and it feels right. And we don't need the Exhausted energy header, because we already have a drawer that says exactly the same — so drop the lightning-bolt Energy tile and put it in what is now financial stress. I'd like the icon instead of the red ring on the left, and the meter of exhausted to energetic would give us the whole range of depletion and energetics." Then, mid-build: "the **battery** icon is a really good metaphor for economic power, because if it's low power it's like a depleted energy, and if it's high power it's like fully charged." Both went in. Version 228 had put the same fact in two places pointing opposite ways — a tile saying Exhausted and a drawer saying 75/100 stress — and this collapses them into one reading in one place. The panel is **Economic power**; its number is `powerScore` (`powerOf(stress) = 100 − stress`, today **25/100**), with `stressScore` kept as the maths underneath so nothing silently changed meaning; its word and state come from the single top-level `powerWord = energyFromReserve(powerScore)`, whose bands are the stress bands mirrored, so the row, the lead row, the icon and the Insights tile cannot disagree. `energyFromReserve()` now also returns `bars` (0–5), which drives `batteryIconSvg(level)` — redrawn taller for the 48px chip (body `x1.6 y5 w18 h14`, five 2.8-unit steps of fill, empty at zero) and rendered through `iconMark()` in place of the red ring. `boltIconSvg()` is deleted, a version after it arrived. The panel's lead row is **Power in reserve**, "what the 5 markers below leave in reserve", and its bar is `powerMeter` — 0–100, optimal `≥ 70`, labelled **Exhausted** … **Energetic ≥ 70 (ample reserve)** via a new optional `ends:{low, zone}` on `meterHtml()`'s `gte` branch, which renames the two end words for that one bar and leaves the other twelve meters untouched. The Insights KPI tile flips with it (today 25, '07 pre-crisis 63, '20 COVID 42 — today below both), as does the (i) note, now phrased as what the five pressures leave rather than what they add. The Energy word tile is gone from `#words-strip`, which is back to Action and Feeling; the menu's guide loses "Energy" from its heading and gains a second paragraph pointing at the drawer. Verified at 390 and 1280 in both themes, no page errors, the drawer reading "Economic power · 25/100 · Exhausted" with a one-bar red battery.

**Version 228.** Keren, on the model rather than the interface: "what I want to be regarded as energy is the correlation to stress. In the body, stress is directly connected to energy levels through the hormonal and nervous systems — a stressor triggers the sympathetic system, the body prioritises short-term survival and diverts energy away from digestion, growth and tissue repair to fuel the muscles and brain; chronic stress is resource depletion, the endocrine system working overtime until the physical reserves are exhausted. This is Gyneconomy in its most classic form. So I think energy is connected to what is now called financial resilience. Maybe it should become financial stress. And I want a lightning icon, and the measurement should be energetic to exhausted." Agreed, and argued in reply: the ISM PMI is how hard the factories are running this month — exertion, not reserve — while the five markers behind the stress composite (debt burden, interest burden, deficit rate, productivity growth, institutional trust) are exactly what she has left to spend on a shock. **Energy is now `100 − stressScore`**, read by `energyFromReserve()` on the stress score's own bands mirrored (70+ Critical → 30 or less is Exhausted; 50+ Serious → Tired; 30+ Elevated → Steady; below that Energetic), so the word and the panel can never disagree: today 25/100, Exhausted. Its mark is `boltIconSvg()`, a lightning bolt in the app's icon language, replacing the battery. The panel is **Financial stress** (Keren's choice of three), which also settles an old contradiction — it was called resilience while its own lead row said "Overall stress 75/100 Critical", the name and the number running opposite ways. The PMI stays among the coincident signs as **Effort** (her choice), keeping its econ term "Industrial output"; the manuscript's framework table keeps its own row (`body:"Energy"`) with the indicator renamed, since that table is the book's. The menu's guide paragraph now explains the stress-energy relationship in the body. **Known limit, deliberate:** the five markers exist for today only, so a cycle opened from the Calendar shows Action and Feeling and no Energy rather than inventing a reserve for a year we never measured — backfilling the five markers at each cycle's close (1999, 2007, 2017, 2021) would light it up historically and is the obvious next step if Keren wants it.

**Version 227.** Keren: "when I click the info icon on the growth per cycle it's too long. I want bullet points, only the central information, read in one clean swoop — not a book. This is applicable to all info popups." Every (i) now opens the same shape: a `.lede` line saying what the thing is, then `facts([...])` — one fact per line, a 5px dot, 15px, 9px apart. The two long narrative popups were rewritten by hand (Growth per cycle from about 2,800 characters of prose to six facts; Temperature to four). The per-marker notes and the card notes keep their own words, because they carry figures, dates and CBO projections better not paraphrased: `factsFrom(text)` splits them on sentence boundaries into the same list, applied in `labRowHtml`, the Overall stress note and `infoIcon()` — so every short note that was a paragraph is a list without a word changing. **Performance, measured** (Keren: "I want the app to be top 1% in performance and build time"): 380 KB raw, 108 KB gzipped, one file, no build step; desktop first paint 40 ms, DOMContentLoaded 221 ms; a phone at 4× CPU throttling first paint 100 ms, first contentful paint 424 ms, DOMContentLoaded 711 ms; 1,900 DOM nodes, 840 of them SVG, 9.5 MB heap; every interaction under 50 ms even throttled (tab switch 26 ms, drawer 19 ms, resize redraw 1 ms). The one real find was the fonts: eleven Google Fonts files requested, six rendered — Cormorant italic 500 and 700 never used, IBM Plex Mono 500/600 and Public Sans 500 used by a handful of rules. The request is now `Cormorant+Garamond:ital,wght@1,600`, `Public+Sans:wght@400;600;700`, `IBM+Plex+Mono:wght@400;700`, with those rules moved to the nearest kept weight (`.serif` to 600, the card metric and the chart tooltip to mono 700, five 500s to 400): five fewer font files on a first load, worth more than anything left in the HTML. Not done, deliberately: stripping the 56 KB of source comments would save about 8% of the gzipped file and cost the artifact its own history, which past sessions have relied on.

**Version 226.** Keren: "we still don't need different colours inside the dropdown; when I pick Israel I don't need to see 'Israel' on the label because it's already on the right; and make the dropdown and the expansion/contraction label be in the middle of the white space, a little lower." The per-country colours were the last trace of the comparison chart — nothing wears them now that the line is red and green — so the dots in the rows and on the trigger go, with `.peer-isr/jpn/eu/usa` and the leftover `.peer-line`, `.peer-dot` and `.gdp-end-label` rules. The picker's rows are radio, name, code on a 16px/1fr/auto grid. The phase tag drops the economy's name. And `#slot-growth .cv-head` becomes `margin:16px 0 18px` (12/16 on a phone), so the phase and the dropdown sit centred in the air above the chart instead of pinned to its top.

**Version 225.** Keren: "I don't want it to compare with. I want one country at a time. That means the United States is default — you don't need to write 'United States alone', just make it the default. When I press Israel I see the graph in red and green; when I click Japan I see Japan in red and green." So the chart stopped being a comparison and became a switch: `shown` is whichever economy is chosen, the United States when none is, and everything downstream reads from it — the quarters drawn, `regimeOf()` for the colour, the y-scale, the badge and the end reading, and the average line, which is the cycle's own closed-year average for the United States and the mean of what is drawn for another economy. One line, the full treatment: wash, dots, badge, no dotted overlay, no end code. The dropdown's rows are the four economies with "United States" first and selected; the trigger always shows a dot and a name. The phase tag follows what is drawn — "Japan · ↓ contraction" — from that economy's latest regime by the same rule, and `shownEraModel` lets the picker repaint it without a full cycle render. Mid-build Keren added: "we don't need the legend below the graph, I see expansion and contraction when I hover and we have the expansion label at the top left" — so `#growth-legend`, `renderGrowthLegend()` and the markup are gone from the Growth card. One bug caught in testing: the tooltip kept the previous economy's reading after a switch, so `drawGrowth` now clears it on every redraw. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes: United States by default, Israel and Japan each drawn whole with their own average and phases, and back again.

**Version 224.** Keren: "the choose countries doesn't need to be multiselect — only one country at a time, because I want to see each one with red and green, expansion and contraction." So a comparison country is now read the way Mrs. Market reads herself: `regimeTrack(series)` applies the book's growth rule — the trend through the last `GROWTH_WINDOW` year-over-year readings, the same ±0.025 flat band, a flat stretch continuing whichever regime came before — to that country's own figures, and its line is painted from it, green where IT expands and red where it contracts. Three of those in the same two colours would be unreadable, which is exactly why one at a time: the panel's rows became radios with a "United States alone" row to clear the choice, picking one closes the panel, and the trigger reads the country's name or "Compare a country". Two things had to be fixed on the way: `.peer-line` set `stroke` in CSS, which beat the gradient passed as an attribute (the line stayed purple until the CSS stroke came out), and the legend was only built when the cycle rendered, so it never followed a change of country — it is `renderGrowthLegend()` now, called from both. With both lines in the same palette, weight alone did not separate them, so the comparison is **dotted** (1 4.5) and bare — no wash, no badge — while the country's own colour stays on its end code, its dot in the trigger and the legend. The legend's third item names it: "Israel, the dotted line". Verified at 390/1280 in both themes: Israel shows its 2023 contraction and its expansion since, switching to Japan replaces rather than adds, and "United States alone" clears it.

**Version 223.** Keren, a version later: "I don't need the annualized quarter. It's confusing. And if you're saying the most reliable metric is the year over year, we'll stick with it. Also, in the hover, write YoY — the initials — to save room." She is right that carrying two numbers a reader has to reconcile cost more than the confusion it was meant to settle. `gdpQuarterlySaar`, its hover row, its `.sub` style and its source line are all withdrawn (Version 222 was live for one version; the series and where to fetch it are recorded above if it is ever wanted). What stays is one sentence in the Growth (i): every reading here is year over year, American news usually quotes the quarter against the quarter before compounded to a year, and so the two can differ without either being wrong. The hover now reads "real GDP +2.1% YoY". The Temperature card's sub-line keeps the words in full — it has the room.

**Version 222.** Keren: "according to official news the U.S. economy is continuing to expand at a modest pace — real GDP grew at an annualized rate of 1.5% to 2.1%. How did they do this calculation? We should stick with convention." Two conventions, both real, and the range she read is the BEA's: one quarter against the quarter before, compounded to a yearly rate — 2026 Q1 +2.1%, 2026 Q2 +1.5% (FRED A191RL1Q225SBEA, fetched and checked). The app's line is the other: year over year, which the OECD, Eurostat and the World Bank headline, which is why Israel, Japan and the EU can share the axis, and which is steady enough for a season's direction to mean something — the BEA print swings −0.6, +3.8, +4.4, +0.5 across 2025 alone, and −28.0 then +34.9 across 2020. Keren agreed with keeping the line and the model year over year, and chose to carry the BEA figure beside it: `gdpQuarterlySaar` (154 quarters, 1988 Q1–2026 Q2) now rides in the chart's hover under the year-over-year reading — "+1.5% annualized, the quarter alone", or its own row when peers are on — the (i) opens by naming both conventions and saying which one the line draws, and the series is credited in Sources. Nothing else reads it: the season, the colour and the phase tag stay on the year-over-year series. Then: "but you said we are looking at a year and a half, not year over year" — a fair catch at the wording, which had been naming the two layers in separate breaths. Each READING is a quarter against the same quarter a year earlier; the season's DIRECTION is the trend through six of those readings, a year and a half of them. The How to read, the Growth (i) and the Season Model text now say both together.

**Version 221.** Keren: "so why not take one year, why a two-year trend?" The eight-quarter window was mine (Version 106), never the manuscript's, and the argument against it is real: `gdpQuarterlyYoY` is already a year-over-year series, so fitting eight of its points is a two-year trend over already-annualised data, and it lagged a turn by about a year. Measured across 1988–2026 before choosing: four quarters gives 34 regime runs, median 4 quarters, 14 spells under a year; six gives 26 runs, median 5, six short spells; eight gave 21 runs, median 6, three short spells — and four and eight disagree on 39% of all quarters, so the window repaints the dial, not just the chart. Keren chose **six**. `GROWTH_WINDOW = 6` now drives every slice (`seasonTrackAll`, `cycleModel`), the flat band is untouched (±0.025 pp per quarter is a slope, so it does not depend on the window), and the wording that named the window follows — the How to read, the season prose ("over the past six quarters"), the Growth (i). **Consequence worth knowing: today's season moves from Autumn — Stagflation to Summer — Inflation.** The current cycle's regimes under six quarters run expansion to 2022 Q1, contraction 2022 Q2–2023 Q2, expansion 2023 Q3–2024 Q3, contraction 2024 Q4–2025 Q4, expansion from 2026 Q1; today's fit is +0.21 points a year, above the ±0.1 flat band but not by much, and with CPI at 3.4% (hot) an expansion reading makes Summer. Under eight quarters the same quarter read contraction and made Autumn — Stagflation. Verified: the dial's hub, the phase tag and the chart's colour all agree, and the chart now turns where the line turns.

**Version 220.** Keren, reading the repainted chart: "I see Q4 2022 to Q4 2023 is rising — isn't that expansion? And Q1 2022 to Q4 2022 is falling — isn't that contraction?" Both stretches were painted the other way, and the numbers say why: the regime is the direction of the **eight-quarter** fit, so through 2023 the window still held the 2021 rebound (12.4, 5.2, 5.8) and the two-year fit still sloped down while the line climbed from 1.3% to 3.4%; through 2022 it still held the 2020 collapse and rebound, so the fit still sloped up while the line fell from 4.0% to 1.3%. The model lags the line by about a year, in both directions. Asked to choose the rule, Keren answered: "I want the app to be consistent. Make a judgement call as to what is most accurate and follow through throughout the app." The call: the season model's definition stands, because it is the book's and because the seasons, the dial, the Season Model table and the Content tab already read it — a second, faster definition would make one word mean two things. So every direction on screen now comes from `r.regime`: the phase tag (`#growth-phase`, shown in both views), the drawer row's mark, and the line's colour, which already did. `eraGrowth`'s fit through the cycle's ANNUAL rates — a third measure that had been naming directions since V216 — no longer produces a word at all, and the "This cycle" block says the total alone. `growthWord`/`growthArrow`/`growthState(trend)` are replaced by `regimeArrow`/`regimeState(regime)`. To make the lag legible rather than hidden: the chart's hover names the regime for the hovered quarter ("Q2 2026 · contraction"), and the (i) explains it with the 2023 stretch as its example — a two-year trend can still be falling while the last few quarters rise, which is the point of measuring a season over two years rather than one. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes, hovering four quarters across the cycle.

**Version 219.** Keren: "the line chart should be red on contraction and green on expansion. If we're in a contraction phase, then where is the red on the line chart?" — a real contradiction, and hers to catch. The line had been coloured by the LEVEL of growth since Version 156 (green above zero, red below), while the word beside it means the DIRECTION: contraction is growth falling. So the panel could say contraction over a line that never turned red. The line, its wash, its dots and its hover dot now take the season model's own per-quarter regime: `regimeByQ` reads `seasonTrackAll`'s `reading.regime` (expansion while growth is rising, contraction while falling, a flat quarter continuing whichever came before), `quarterRegime()` falls back to the sign for quarters older than the model's history, and `hardStopGradientX` — the existing stepped gradient turned on its side — steps the colour at the midpoint between the last quarter of one regime and the first of the next. The Subprime cycle now reads as it should: red down the 2008–09 slide, green up the recovery, alternating after. Also on her list: the direction tag moves off the drawer's row (where the mark already carries its colour) into the panel's head as `#growth-phase`, opposite the dropdown and shown only inside the drawer, since a cycle opened from the Calendar has it in the "This cycle" block; the row drops the years and reads "+11 % · cycle total growth"; and the country codes at the line ends get 13px of separation instead of 11, from each other and from the badge. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes and on the Subprime cycle.

**Version 218.** The change held back from 217, taken across the app on Keren's "ok go": `.subject-value` is IBM Plex Mono at −0.02em, so all six drawer rows' headline figures wear the figures' face, as the Insights strip and the charts already did. The words beside them do not: `.subject-value .unit` is pinned back to Public Sans, and so are the labels inside the Coincident and Lagging rows' chips (`.subject-value .chip i`), which sit in the same element and had gone mono with the first build — a word in a figure face is exactly what the type rule forbids. The Calendar's era rows share the class but name their own faces (Cormorant for the cycle name), so they are untouched. Checked at 390/1280 in both themes and on the Calendar.

**Version 217.** Keren: "can you take this component and ask Lovable to stylize it in the DSM language?" The GDP growth panel was described to the Gyneconomy DSM's agent in words — the summary row, the country multiselect, the chart and its chrome, the rule that a chart in a drawer is not a card in a card, and the expansion/contraction vocabulary (the screenshot could not be attached: Google Cloud Storage, where Lovable's upload URLs live, is blocked from this session's proxy). It planned, paused for approval, was told to go ahead, and built `GrowthPanel` on the Components page (`Added GDP GrowthPanel`, then `Fixed GDP growth chart series`; 1.5 credits for the plan turn). Its decisions ported back here: comparison lines at 1.1px and 0.82 opacity rather than 1.5px flat, so the cycle's own line stays the subject; a country's end label written in an **ink** rather than its paint (`--peer-ink`) — the DSM's rule that text never wears a paint token, which matters for the European Union's gold, unreadable on white, now the amber ink; the average's label on a small `--surface` plate (`.chart-label-plate`) so it reads wherever it lands, which also retires the collision-dodging that had been growing around it; the Growth chart's end line dropped to `--border-strong` from primary at 35%; the picker's panel at radius 8 with 8px padding, its own "Compare with" label, rows as 44px targets separated by rules with the country's code in mono at the right and the chosen ones on the accent's wash rather than three different washes; the trigger's dots overlapping by 4px with a surface ring, like a stack of chips; and a clear 24px (20px on a phone) between the control and the chart. Not ported: the DSM sets the row's figure in IBM Plex Mono, which is right by the type rules but belongs to every drawer's row, not this one — a separate change if Keren wants it. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes with all three peers drawn and the menu open.

**Version 216.** Keren: "there's white space above the countries dropdown — minimise it, to look more elegant and consistent with the spacing across the app. Also, positive growth and negative growth are expansion and contraction in our Gyneconomy language, so when we write 'falling' in the preview it needs to be contraction. And you lost the styling." Three fixes. The air: `#slot-growth` carried an 18px margin and the card a 16px top padding, so the dropdown sat 34px down — now 8px and 0, one step of air rather than two. The words: `growthWord`, `growthArrow` and `growthState` (function declarations, since the subject summaries run before that line — the first build made them `var`s and every row that read one threw), so the drawer's row, the "This cycle" block and the chart's legend all say expansion, contraction or steady; "positive growth"/"negative growth" are gone from the legend, and the season rule already read the same way (rising growth is expansion, falling is contraction). The styling: the direction is a tag on the row again — "↓ contraction" in the severity's wash — the way every other subject row carries its verdict, so the row reads "+11 % · cycle growth, 2022–2025  ↓ contraction". Mid-build Keren settled the colour question from 215: green in expansion, red in contraction. With the tag beside it naming the direction, the colour is explained rather than alarming, which is what had made it read wrong when the tag was missing. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes, on a Calendar cycle ("↑ EXPANSION" in its block) and with the (i) tap.

**Version 215.** Keren: "can you tell Lovable to create the icon and import it from Lovable?" — and, mid-build, "this cycle +11% total growth and the years, falling, should be at the header of the container instead of the 2.1%, because we are looking at things from a cycle point of view; just write cycle growth, the years and falling." The DSM's agent was given the brief in words (what the mark shows, the 24 grid, currentColor, round caps, no interior detail, and that it must hold at 27px in a 48px chip) and built `SproutMark` at `src/components/gyneconomy/SproutMark.tsx`, put it on the Icon page beside the lotus at 96/48/27/20px with three state chips, and reintroduced the amber ink/wash pair for them; the edit cost 2.5 credits. Its four paths — soil, stem, larger right leaf, smaller left leaf — were rendered against V214's drawing at 96/48/27/20 and in chips before importing: the DSM's is cleaner small (a plain soil arc, both leaves anchored straight on the stem, one stroke fewer), so `sproutSvg()` now carries its paths verbatim with a comment pointing at the DSM as the source — change it there first. The row: `eraGrowth(currentEra)` gives the total, the years and the trend, so it reads "+11 % · cycle growth, 2022–2025 · falling"; `#slot-growth #growth-stats` is hidden, since that block said the same thing one line below. The mark's colour is taken from whether the cycle grew (total ≥ 0 → good, else critical) rather than from the trend word: the trend mapping painted the sprout warning-red for a cycle up 11%, which over-signals, and "falling" is already said in words. A `norm` chip was added to `.subject-icon` while that was settled. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes and on a Calendar cycle, which still shows the card's own title and "This cycle" block.

**Version 214.** Keren, with a sheet of sprout icons: "choose one of these for growth, the current icon is not clear enough." She was right about why: at the 27px the drawer's row shows, the hand's palm and curling fingers turn to mush. `sproutInHandSvg` becomes `sproutSvg` — a seedling out of the ground, a small leaf left, a larger one right, one straight stem, a gentle line of soil. Four drawings were rendered at 96/48/27/20px and compared: midribs inside the leaves blur at row size, a wavy ground line reads better than a straight rule, and a straight stem beats a curved one. Mid-build she added: "drop the Q2 2026 text — I can see it on the graph; I don't need 'now'; I don't need 'real GDP'; I don't need 'year over year'; the trend I already have. Only leave the +2.1%." So the Growth chart's end block is the reading alone beside its line (`padT` drops from 46/62 to 28, and the value clears the line with 46px rather than 84px of room); the quarter, the series and the trend word live in the hover, the row above and the cycle block. One correction for the record: the series IS year over year — each quarter against the same quarter a year earlier, published quarterly — so the label was accurate; it came off because it was redundant, not because it was wrong. The Temperature chart's read-out is untouched.

**Version 213.** Keren, with a reference picture of a seedling held in an open hand: change the GDP growth mark from a green dot to that; put the (i) next to the "GDP growth" title at the top; write "US", not "United States"; and "I see 2.2 growth, in the graph I see 2.1, and the total cycle is +11 — we should maybe use borders or something to organise the view and understand what is the current data and what is the overall cycle data." The three numbers were three different things, and two of them were two different measures: the drawer's row carried the World Bank's 2025 annual figure while the chart's end line carried Q2 2026 against the same quarter a year earlier. The row now reads the last entry of `gdpQuarterlyYoY` — "+2.1 % · US, Q2 2026" — so the annual figure is gone from the drawer and the row and the chart agree; the cycle's total sits in its own bordered block above the chart under a "This cycle" kicker (`#growth-stats.cycle-stats`), and the chart's end read-out names itself "now · real GDP, year over year", or "at the close" for a cycle opened from the Calendar. The mark: `sproutInHandSvg()`, drawn here rather than taken from the reference (a watermarked stock illustration) — an open palm seen from the side with the fingers curling back, a wrist, and a two-leaf sprout, in the app's icon language (24 grid, no fill, currentColor, 1.7 stroke, round caps). Four candidates were rendered at 96/48/27/20px and compared: the cupped-hand versions read as cups at small sizes, the side-on palm reads as a hand. It is shown by `iconMark(key, state, svg)` — the icon in the state's ink on its wash in a 48px round chip (42px on phones), the word tiles' pairing — beside `ring()` and `dot()`. The (i) moved to the row: `#subj-label-gdp` gets `infoIcon(growthDetail)` on first render, the card's own kicker is hidden inside the drawer (`#slot-growth #growth-kicker`) and still shows for a Calendar cycle, and the shared modal handler now calls `preventDefault()` for an `.expand-btn` inside a `<summary>` so the (i) opens the modal without toggling the drawer. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes and on the Subprime cycle; the (i) tap opens the modal with the drawer still shut.

**Version 212.** Keren: "I don't get it. How is it that you have GDP growth per quarter for the US, then not for any other country? And if so, maybe we don't need the other countries." She had found a real inconsistency, and the answer was that it was ours: the US line is quarterly because the season model needs an eight-quarter window, while the peers had only ever been the World Bank's annual series — one figure a year — from Version 16. Quarterly national accounts exist for all of them; we had simply never gathered them. Offered the choice, she asked for quarterly data and narrowed the list: "only Israel, Japan and the European Union. We don't need too many countries." Gathered through the tools (direct curl is blocked by the proxy; WebFetch reaches both): Israel and Japan from the OECD's Quarterly National Accounts (`DSD_NAMAIN1@DF_QNA_EXPENDITURE_GROWTH_OECD`, key `Q.Y.<area>.S1..B1GQ......GY.`, SDMX-JSON — the CSV formats come through as binary) and the European Union (EU27 from 2020) from Eurostat's `namq_10_gdp` (CLV_PCH_SM, SCA, JSON-stat); both APIs return the period list unordered or index-keyed, so the pairing was done in `peers.py` and checked before use. Validation: every series' 2020 Q2 and 2009 Q1 land where they should (Israel −8.1 and +0.3, Japan −9.5 and −8.2, the EU −13.1 and −5.5), Israel's 2023 Q4 shows the war quarter at −4.0, and each year's average of the four quarters matches the World Bank annual figure already in the file to within a few tenths. `gdpYears` and `gdpCountries` are replaced by `gdpPeers` — three economies, each with a `q` map keyed "YYYY Qn" like `gdpQuarterlyYoY`, 2005 Q1 to 2026 Q2, none on by default. `drawGrowth` now draws a peer on exactly the quarters the US line draws on, in the US line's Catmull–Rom curve at 1.5px with the same dot spacing (`every`, hoisted above the peers), so Version 211's held-flat lead-in and lead-out are gone with the annual data that needed them; the tooltip reads the peer's own quarter; the end labels tuck in before the cycle's end line and are pushed clear of the badge, and the average's label now dodges the peers' points as well as the US line's. Colours: Israel the purple, Japan Winter's periwinkle, the European Union Autumn's gold. The drawer's summary reads `usRealGdpGrowth` directly instead of the deleted `gdpCountries`. Sources add the OECD and Eurostat (`gdpPeerSrc`) beside the World Bank, which still measures each cycle as a whole. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes and on the Subprime cycle from the Calendar, where the comparison finally says something: Japan and the EU fell far harder than the US in 2009 while Israel barely dipped. NOTE: the standalone sources.html is one release behind — regenerate it at the next refresh so its list matches the app's.

**Version 211.** Keren, on the Cycle tab's GDP growth container: drop the "Israel +2.9%" line at the top left, make the US the only default, drop the "Compare with" label, put the dropdown at the top right, drop the "GDP growth" title inside the container since the preview above already carries it — and "the Israel graph is not at the beginning of 2022, also Japan, Germany; it's not in the entire period." Five changes and one fix. Israel's `on` becomes false, so the chart opens on the US alone. The drawer's summary passes "" as its context, so the second headline naming a peer is gone. `#growth-peers` moves into `.cv-head` with `margin-left:auto` (`#growth-card .cv-head{align-items:center}`) and the panel is right-aligned (`right:0`) so it opens inside the card on a phone; the "Compare with" span goes. The kicker's words are wrapped in `.kicker-text`, hidden by `#slot-growth .cv-kicker .kicker-text{display:none}` — inside the drawer the (i) is left alone on the row, opposite the dropdown, while a cycle opened from the Calendar still shows "GDP growth". The fix: a peer's annual figure is the whole year's, so the line now runs from its first year's January (or the axis's start) to its last year's December (or the axis's end, whichever comes first), the first and last readings held flat over those stretches; the dots stay on the mid-year readings and the code label follows the line's new end. 2026 is not drawn — the World Bank has not published it. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes: US-only by default with the trigger reading "Choose countries"; ticking Israel, Japan and Germany draws three lines spanning Jan 2022 to Dec 2025; the panel's right edge sits on the card's; the Subprime cycle from the Calendar keeps its title; no errors.

**Version 210.** Keren: "make the country comparison in the GDP growth chart a multiselect dropdown." The five pills of 209 become one trigger — a dot per selected country, then "Israel, Japan +1 more" (or "Choose countries" when none), with the caret rotating when open — over a panel of checkbox rows grouped as the data groups them (Core / Europe), all in the system's grammar: surface, `--border-strong`, radius 14, rows in Public Sans 14 with the country's colour as a dot, the checkbox in the accent and a 12% wash under a row that is on. Toggling still flips `c.on`, clears `tempState.key` and redraws both charts. The document's outside-click and Escape handlers are bound once (`renderPeerPills.bound`) rather than per render, since the picker is rebuilt for every cycle — the V208 dropdown's bug. Verified at 390/1280 in both themes: the panel opens on the phone without overflowing, ticking Japan and Germany gives lines ISR/JPN/DEU and the trigger "Israel, Japan +1 more", an outside click closes it, no errors.

**Version 209.** Keren: "in the GDP growth container, merge the old line chart into the new one, and use the new design system for the button of the countries." Since V206 the drawer had held the cycle's Growth chart and, under it, the old comparison chart (six countries' World Bank annual growth 2010–2025, a dropdown picker, its own axes). Now one chart: `drawGrowth` takes the peers that are on (`gdpCountries` minus the US, which is the chart's own line), puts each year's figure on the year's middle month of the Temperature axis for the years the cycle covers up to the axis's end, folds them into the y-scale, and draws each as a 1.5px straight-run line (no curve invented between annual points) with hollow dots and its country code after the last point — or above-before it when the cycle's end line is too close — spaced 11px apart. Colours come from the system with no new tokens: `.peer-isr{--peer:var(--accent)}`, `.peer-jpn` Winter, `.peer-deu` Autumn, `.peer-fra` Summer, `.peer-gbr` Spring; the line, dots, label, pill and tooltip swatch all read `--peer`. The tooltip lists the US quarter and each peer's year. The picker is `renderPeerPills(m)`: a "Compare with" label and one pill per peer in the DSM's tag grammar (outline + hollow dot off, 14% wash + solid dot on, `aria-pressed`), hidden for cycles ending before 2010; a tap flips `c.on`, clears `tempState.key` (the first build forgot this and the cached key skipped the redraw) and redraws both charts. Removed: the `section.hero` with `#gdp-svg`, the `.gdp-country-*` CSS, the comparison renderer (its `allSources.concat(gdpSrc)` kept), the three `--gdp-*` token lines; `#slot-growth .card` loses its bottom rule since it is now the drawer's last thing. Verified with Playwright: toggling Japan and Germany on and Israel off draws `peer-jpn`/`peer-deu` with labels JPN/DEU; hover at Q4 2024 reads "United States +2.4% · Japan 2024 −0.2% · Germany 2024 −0.5%"; the Subprime cycle from the Calendar draws the peers from 2010 with the pills shown; the Dot-Com cycle hides the pills and draws none; screenshots at 390/1280 in both themes; no errors.

**Version 208.** Keren, with a screenshot of the "4 markers flagged" pill sitting above the Overall stress row: "remove this, it is redundant." The drawer's summary row (75/100 · Critical) and its context line ("Overall stress, from five markers · 4 flagged") already carry it, and the table's own flags show which. The `.longcycle-head` row is hidden inside `.subject-body` — its h2 was already hidden there since the drawers arrived — which also removes the Sentiment drawer's tag, redundant in the same way against its summary. The tag elements and the code that fill them remain (ids referenced), just unseen. Verified at 390px: both drawers screenshotted, no scroll.

**Version 207.** Keren: "in mobile view I don't want to scroll left and right to see the reference range in the tables — in Financial resilience, make the title, then below it the marker, like the Season Model." The two lab-report tables (Financial resilience `#labtable-body`, Sentiment `#psych-table-body`, both `table.labtable` with `min-width:560px` inside `.labtable-scroll{overflow-x:auto}`) were the only sideways scrollers on a phone. One `@media (max-width:640px)` block: the scroll box loses its overflow, table and tbody become blocks, the header row is hidden, each `tr` is a grid with areas `"name flag" / "range range"` and 14px padding, the three `td`s become blocks (the first the name and sub-line at 15px, the last the flag right-aligned at the top, the second the reference labels and track full width with 12px above the labels), and the stress row keeps its surface-2 ground and 2px rule (`table.labtable tbody tr.stress-row td` outweighs the desktop stress-row padding/border rule that sits later in the stylesheet — the first build left 2px lines under the name and the flag). Desktop keeps the three-column table unchanged. Verified with Playwright at 390px in both themes: `.labtable-scroll` scrollWidth 310/310 (560/310 before), document scrollWidth 390; screenshots of both tables and the desktop table; script syntax check.

**Version 206.** Keren: "in the current cycle the dial should be first; decouple the Temperature and GDP growth graphs so each stands alone, and merge them into the appropriate drawers imported from the Analysis tab." Two things were true: the tab handler had been appending the cycle view to the end of the panel since 205, so returning to the tab put the drawers above the dial — now `insertBefore(#today-analysis)`. And the two charts: slots added in the Lagging drawer (`#slot-temp`, before the Activity/Temperature cards) and the GDP growth drawer (`#slot-growth`, before the peer chart); `placeCharts("drawers" | "view")` moves the two card elements between the slots and the cycle view — drawers on the Cycle tab, view for a cycle opened from the Calendar — and inside a drawer the cards shed border, background and radius (no card inside a card). The shared crosshair (`chartLink`, `linkedShow/linkedHide`) is retired; each chart's hit-rect drives its own `showAt/hide`. Verified with Playwright: the cards' parents are the slots on load, the cycle view after opening the Subprime cycle from the Calendar, the slots again on returning; the dial is the panel's first child; both drawers screenshotted open; no errors.

**Version 205.** Keren: "the Current Cycle tab should have the cycle analysis that is now in the Analysis tab, in the same drawers that open and close; and the Analysis tab should hold a cyclical analysis of all the cycles — an overview of past cycles and what is likely to happen in the future." Asked which pieces the new Analysis should carry (a table of the cycles, season strips, the typical cycle, what comes next); she chose the season strips. Built: (1) the whole Analysis panel body — the Insights strip and the six subject drawers — moved by string surgery into the Cycle panel after `#cycle-view`, wrapped in `#today-analysis`; nothing in their JS changed (they render by id), and they stay outside the shared cycle view because they are today's figures. (2) The Analysis tab: a kicker, a caption and `#cycle-strips`, rendered from `marketCycles` (newest first) via `cycleModel`: the track's quarters grouped into runs by `seasonGroup`, each run a pill whose `flex` is its quarter count (a one-quarter run a dot), in the `--season-*` tokens — the ring laid flat; the open cycle scaled to `typicalCycleYears` with the remaining quarters as small muted dots; a foot line with the year and season or the close, length, closing season and the S&P 500 total. Under the strips a "What usually comes next" card reuses `seasonReading[currentSeason].next`. The How-to-read "tabs" paragraph rewritten. Verified: syntax; five strips and six drawers counted on the page; the Analysis tab and the moved drawers on the phone, light and dark; no errors.

**Version 204.** Keren: "the font in the Calendar tab — AI Cycle, COVID-19 Cycle, the previous cycles' titles — should be more feminine." They were the one cycle name not in the voice: the rows carry `.era-drawer-name` but the Cormorant rule was scoped to `.era-drawer`, so on the Calendar list the names fell back to `.subject-value`'s bold Public Sans. A matching `.era-row` rule sets them in Cormorant italic 600 at 26px, the reading-head size. Verified light and dark; no errors.

**Version 203.** Keren: "when I drag the year marker and click outside, I want it to go back to the default." The document-level click handler, which had only cleared a hovered or tapped quarter, now sends a parked badge home: outside the dial, if `dialState.parked` is set and no scrub is in progress, it calls `goTo(-1)` — badge to its resting angle, resting number, hub back to today's reading. Verified with a real pointer drag in Playwright (badge parked on Q4 2022, hub reading it; one click on the page → badge home, hub "Today, Sep 18"); no errors.

**Version 202.** Keren: "the grey dots should be only on the inner ring, and close the cycle; at the start of the cycle let the grey overlap a little on the first tab; make the end and the start about five pixels apart — this is for cycles that have a grey area." The seasons-ring dots of 201 are gone; the band's dots run to the track's end. The layout is now anchored on the first shape: `START` 2.5° (its cap's edge), the track's path from `START − LEAD + capDegT` (LEAD 3°) so its rounded end peeks out ahead of the shape, round to `SEAM_END − capDegT` with `SEAM_END = 360 + START − GAP` (GAP 5° ≈ 5px on a 346px dial); `ORIGIN = START − 1.2` and `ARC = SEAM_END + 1.2 − ORIGIN` so a full cycle's last cap lands on SEAM_END. Checked at 3×: track end, five-pixel gap, the grey lead, then the orange shape. Verified light and dark on the open cycle and the Long Boom; no errors.

**Version 201.** Keren: "on the current cycle, make the gap a little smaller and add grey dots to complete it." `GAP` 12° → 8°, and the future-quarter loop now places a second dot on the seasons ring (r 2.4, `.dial-dot.outer`, opacity 0.5) beside the band's (r 1.7), from just past the badge to the track's end at the seam, so the open cycle reads as a full circle of quarters with the seasons filled in so far. Closed cycles unchanged. Verified: light and dark on the open cycle, the Long Boom; no errors.

**Version 200.** Keren: "close the cycle — I want a small gap between the start and the end." The dial's layout was a 24° open seam plus a 24° margin for the drop; now `GAP` = 12° is the visible gap between the track's two rounded ends (the track runs from GAP/2 + its cap radius to the mirror), `ORIGIN` = GAP/2 − 1.2° so the first run's cap begins exactly at the seam's edge, and `ARC` = 360 − 2·ORIGIN carries the years to the other edge; every angle — runs, band, badge, future dots — is now `ORIGIN + years × degPerYear`, and the dots run to the track's end. A cycle that fills the ring (the Long Boom, the Subprime, or any closed cycle drawn at its own length) left no room for the badge before the seam, so when `badgeDeg + the badge's half-width` would cross the seam the badge sits at 360° — on the seam, a clasp where the cycle closed; the scrubber's circular distances needed no change. Verified: the open cycle (badge after today), the COVID cycle (short, badge after its end), the Subprime and Long Boom (clasp), light and dark; no errors.

**Version 199.** Keren, with Clue's appearance screen: "make the appearance a new page like the attached — in our design system"; and mid-build: "in the design system there's no drop of blood in the cycle — a better choice, we already have a lot going on." The inline control of 198 became a menu row (its small line names the current choice) opening `#sheet-appearance`, built like the Sources and Contact sheets: the back arrow, "Choose your app appearance" in Cormorant at 30px, a card of three radio rows — a 64px tile each (sliders on surface-2 for Use system setting; a light and a dark miniature of the app, page colour with a card corner carrying the lotus in plum) — and a 26px purple check that appears on the chosen row. The `.menu-row svg` rule (18px, muted stroke) had shrunk the tiles' icons and hidden the check; overridden with `.menu-row .appearance-…` selectors. The drop: `.dial-drop`, its CSS and its placement maths (`qGap`, `dropDeg`) removed; the seam stays open and the year badge still marks the cycle's position. Verified: the sheet in both themes, the choice applied and reflected on the menu row; the dial without the drop on the open cycle and the Long Boom; no errors.

**Version 198.** Keren: "my app on my iPhone shows only dark mode — do you know why? Maybe add Appearance to the menu so I can choose dark or light." The page had followed `prefers-color-scheme` with no override, so a phone set to dark (or to automatic after sunset) always got the dark theme. Added an **Appearance** section to the menu with a Theme row and a System · Light · Dark segmented control (`#theme-toggle`, the DSM's state-toggle style); the choice is saved as `localStorage` `gyneconomy-theme` and applied as `data-theme` on `<html>` by a script at the very top of the page, before first paint, so there is no flash; System removes both. The stylesheet's `:root[data-theme="dark"]` and `:root:not([data-theme="light"])` rules were already written for it. Verified with Playwright under a dark colour scheme: body background dark → Light chosen → #f7f3ee, persists across reload, System restores dark and clears the key; no errors.

**Version 197.** Keren: "I completed the design system in Lovable myself — Gyneconomy DSM. Take what I did there and apply it on our app." Read the project's `src/styles.css`, its plan "Simplify the colour system", the Type page, `CycleDial.tsx`, the shell and the components page (18 edits of hers after the seeded build). Applied: **the voice is Cormorant Garamond italic** (Google Fonts request changed; every Fraunces rule moved; the hub's `opsz` setting dropped), never below 20px — reading heads, body-terms, cycle names, the popup note and blockquotes went 15/17 → 20, the modal title and the Insights quote → 26, the hub's season word ≈40px and its phase line ≈20px. **Seven colours:** purple for brand and action, green/red for direction, four fixed season colours (#5b7fd6 / #9aa7d4 / #d86f32 / #d8a23c; dark #7f9ce6 / #a6baf0 / #ec835a / #f2c24d), the rest neutral — rose, amber, coral and cold retired as roles: `--rose*` are aliases of the purple (used only by the hub's phase line), `--warning` and `--serious` are the red at 55% and 78% over the surface with the red ink (the severity scale keeps its four steps as values of one hue), `--cold` is Winter's periwinkle; every kicker and name went to muted or primary text; the callout, list bullets and blockquote rule went neutral; the Insights quotation mark is purple. Muted text #6d666f; washes as color-mix over the surface (accent 10, good 14, critical 12; dark 14/16/18); an `--overlay` token behind sheets. The phone tab pill is solid surface with a strong border and no shadow, labels muted, the active label primary with only the chip purple. The year badge is a primary-text disc with page-coloured text; the peak mark is the DSM's "here" mark — a surface disc ringed and cored in the band's colour. The info glyph is 18px, transparent, secondary text. Not ported: the DSM's investment card and holdings (no counterpart in the app yet). The design-system doc was re-based on the DSM and names it the source of truth. Verified: syntax; all four tabs on the phone, light and dark; no errors.

**Version 196.** Keren: "the gradients on the main cycle are confusing — switch back to a solid colour." The Version 191 fade (sub-arcs with inline `color-mix` strokes, `fadeStroke`, `FADE_DEG`, `EDGE_SHARE`, `SUB_DEG`) is removed and the runs loop is back to one path per quarter in the run's flat colour; the Season Model table's two small gradients, which she liked, stay. Verified: syntax; no `fadeStroke` left; the AI Cycle light and the Long Boom dark; no errors.

**Version 195.** Keren: "I can still barely see it — the initial design was the best one, the mark slightly outside the shape." The Version 158 mark is back: a disc r 5.2 in the band's colour at 55% white, a white dot r 1.9, sitting a hair wider than the 6-unit band; the one change from the original is the ring, pure white instead of `--surface`, which is what had drawn the black outline on the dark theme that started the 185 detour. The invisible hit circle is gone (the disc is the target again). Legend row and How-to-read restored to match. Verified: syntax; zoomed both themes; hover reads the peak year; no errors.

**Version 194.** Keren: "the peak-year sign is too subtle — make a borderline white, something more prominent." The dot keeps its `--surface` fill, grows to r 1.9 and takes a pure-white 1.3-unit stroke: on the light theme that is simply a larger white dot; on the dark theme a dark centre ringed in white, which is what makes it read. Outer diameter ≈5.1 of the band's 6. Legend row and How-to-read ("the white-ringed dot") follow. While zoomed at 3× the Autumn fade showed its 2.5° steps as faint bands on the light tint, so `SUB_DEG` went to 1.5°. Verified: syntax; zoomed both themes; hover still reads "2023 · Bull year · Peak year"; no errors.

**Version 193.** Keren: "in the Season Model table make Winter — Deflation above Spring — Deflation: all the blues one by one, then all the yellow-oranges; and in the legend, Winter at the top, then Spring, Summer, Autumn." `seasonRules` reordered (Winter, Spring–Deflation, Spring–Reflation, Summer, Autumn–Disinflation, Autumn–Stagflation — it drives the table only) and the legend's array starts with Winter. Verified by reading both lists back from the rendered page; no errors.

**Version 192.** Keren: "make Summer and Autumn on the yellow-orange range — the pink range isn't working." `--season-summer` is now `color-mix(in srgb, var(--serious) 60%, var(--warning))` (coral and amber, the system's own warm paints — ≈#da6a2f light, ≈#ec8f36 dark) and `--season-autumn` a 48% white tint of that orange pushed 30% toward amber, so the light end leans yellow. Nothing else changed: the ring, its edge fades, the table's bars and gradients and the flame icon all read the same two tokens, and the legend, How-to-read and code comments now say orange. The orange also settles the old worry — a hot Summer over a bear year is unmistakably two colours. Verified: syntax; the AI Cycle light, the Long Boom dark, the table on the phone; no errors.

**Version 191.** Keren: "I really love the gradient — can we make the transition seasons in the cycle component a gradient as well? Q4 2022 and Q4 2023 would be slightly reddish on the edges; it would explain visually that Spring and Autumn are transitions." SVG has no gradient along an arc, so a Spring or Autumn run is drawn in 2.5° sub-arcs (`SUB_DEG`), each with an inline `style="stroke:color-mix(in srgb, var(--season-summer|--season-winter) N%, var(--season))"` where N = 55 × (1 − distance-to-nearer-end / 9°), clamped at 0 (`fadeStroke`, `FADE_DEG`, `EDGE_SHARE`); the two `.cap` stubs take the edge mix; Summer and Winter runs are unchanged. Every sub-arc keeps its quarter's `data-q`, so `readTarget` still resolves one quarter and `mark()` (which already collected all matching elements) swells all of them — checked: hovering Q2 2023 lights four paths and the hub reads "Q2 2023 · Autumn · Disinflation". Path count 58 on the open cycle. The colours resolve through the same four tokens, so the ring, the table's gradients and the icons still move together. Verified: syntax; the AI Cycle and Long Boom in light and dark; a 3× zoom of the 2022–23 stretch; no errors.

**Version 190.** Keren: "make the transition seasons a gradient — Spring should start at the colour of the ice icon and gradually become the light blue; the same with Autumn, from the fire icon's colour on the right to the light pink; just a small gradient." Two CSS rules on the Season Model table: `.lag-row.spring .range-bar b.on.below` is `linear-gradient(90deg, var(--season-winter), var(--season-spring))` and `.lag-row.autumn .range-bar b.on.above` is `linear-gradient(270deg, var(--season-summer), var(--season-autumn))`; the within-range zone stays the flat tint, so the fade is confined to the outer zone — small, as asked. Still four tokens, nothing new declared. Verified: syntax; the table on the phone; no errors.

**Version 189.** Keren: "change the colour of the fire icon in the Season Model table to be the same as Summer — Inflation." `.range-cell svg.hot` reads `--season-summer` and, for symmetry, `svg.cold` reads `--season-winter` (the same periwinkle as before, since Winter is `--cold`). Verified: syntax; the table on the phone; no errors.

**Version 188.** Keren: "yes, do it" — the pinker cut of the red offered in 187. `--season-summer` is now `color-mix(in srgb, var(--critical) 60%, var(--rose))` (≈#d04953 light, ≈#e76368 dark) and `--season-autumn` a 45% white tint of that, so the pair stays on one hue. Checked at 3× on the 2022 stretch: the raspberry outer run and the bear-red band beneath it now read as two colours in both themes. Verified: syntax; computed strokes; no errors.

**Version 187.** Keren: "I think we made a mistake — the colour that is now Spring should be Autumn and vice versa, because Spring is below or between the range, so it belongs in the blue hues, and Autumn is between or above, in the red-pink hue. Summer should be a stronger pink leaning to red, the same shade as the fire icon on the right of the Season Model table." So the ring's hues are the temperature's, exactly as the table's snowflake and flame already say: `--season-winter: var(--cold)`, `--season-spring: color-mix(in srgb, var(--cold) 48%, white)`, `--season-summer: var(--critical)`, `--season-autumn: color-mix(in srgb, var(--critical) 42%, white)` — declared once in `:root` and theme-following through the two base tokens, so the dark blocks lost their season entries. The legend's small text names each season's range ("below or within the range"); the caption and How-to-read explain deep = wholly outside the range, light = straddling it. The rose/plum expansion scheme of 182–186 is retired. Consequence noted to Keren: Summer's red is also the band's bear-year red (2022 shows red over red). Verified: syntax; the table on the phone; the AI Cycle light and the Long Boom dark; no errors.

**Version 186.** Keren: "the Season Model needs three rows — the season, what the season is, then the target range; and the shades of Spring and Autumn are very similar, can barely be noticed — fine-tune." Phones: `grid-template-areas: "name" "meta" "range"`, the bar `flex:1` across the row (12px tall, 15px icons), the header row reduced to "Season". Colours: the two light tints were neighbouring pastels (lilac #d6bfe0 at ~285° and periwinkle #b9c8ec at ~225°, both pale); Spring moved toward pink (#d3a3d8, dark #d9acdc) and Autumn toward sky (#a4c4ee, dark #a9c8f0), a touch more chroma in each, so they sit two hue-steps apart; the deep ends unchanged. Verified: syntax; the table on the phone; the Subprime and Long Boom dials light and dark; no errors.

**Version 185.** Keren: "make the peak cycle marker be inside the lines; drop the black borderline and make it more subtle because there's a lot going on." The mark had been a pale disc (r 5.2, wider than the 6-unit band) with a `--surface` ring — which reads as a black ring on the dark theme — and a white dot. Now: a single dot in `--surface` (r 1.8, opacity 0.95) inside the band, plus a transparent r 6 `.hit` circle so hover and tap still read the year out ("2023 · Bull year … · Peak year"). The legend's Peak year row is a short band with the dot in it; How-to-read says "the small dot inside the band". Design-system §3 gains a line: marks on a band are a surface dot inside the stroke, never a disc wider than it or a dark ring. Verified: syntax; zoomed screenshots light and dark; hover on the mark; no errors.

**Version 184.** Keren: "the Season Model on the Content tab is really tiny — how can we fix this?" It was four columns at 11px on phones. The rows now emit growth and temperature twice — as two `.cell`s (desktop) and as one `.meta` line "Expansion · Cooling" (phones) — and the phone layout is a two-row grid: the season name at 15px across the whole row (`grid-template-areas: "name name" "meta range"`), then the meta line in secondary text with the target-range bar (88px, 12px tall, 14px icons) to its right; desktop rows go to 14px in four columns. First cuts had the name beside the bar (the row is only 298px inside the card, so "Autumn — Disinflation" wrapped) and a wider bar (the meta line wrapped); measured with Playwright and settled at name-across, bar 88px, `white-space:nowrap` on the meta. Verified: syntax; the table on the phone and desktop, light and dark; no errors.

**Version 183.** Keren: "Yes, make the change [plum for expansion]. Also I want the colours in the target range: below range deep periwinkle, above range deep [plum]; heating (Spring) light; cooling (Autumn) light periwinkle. We don't need the extra dots on the left side." Asked whether "make the change" meant plum (her words still said rose); she confirmed plum. Tokens: `--season-spring` #d6bfe0 lilac, `--season-summer` #8a4d97 (the brand plum itself; dark #d9c3e3 / #a96fbb); periwinkle pair unchanged. The Season Model table: each `.lag-row` now carries its season's group class and the lit zones of its target-range bar read `var(--season)` — so the table shows exactly the mapping she described, row by row, from the same four tokens as the ring and the legend; the base `.range-bar b.on` rule lost its per-zone cold/good/critical colours (the bar is used nowhere else); the V182 dots are gone. Legend and How-to-read say plum/lilac. Verified: syntax; the dial light and dark; the legend and the table on the phone; no errors.

**Version 182.** Keren, with a seasonal-colour-analysis chart (hue / value / chroma): "two gradients, one in the pink-red zone and another in the white-blue zone, according to our design system — expansion is the pink hue, contraction the blue hue; open to other gradients, but two scales for expansion and contraction is a good idea; colours that complement the inner wheel and don't compete with it." And mid-build: "also change the colour in the season model and in the legend — make them import from the same variable so one change fixes the entire app." Two value scales in the system's own hues: rose for expansion (Spring light #e9b7cb, Summer deep #bf5885), periwinkle for contraction (Autumn light #b9c8ec, Winter deep #4f72cc); dark #e8b9cd / #d06f9b / #bcc9ee / #7592e0. Chroma deliberately soft (the chart's "muted / soft" end) so the band's teal and red remain the louder ring; the deep rose sits far enough from the band's orange-red in hue to read apart at 11 units. The Season Model table's rows now lead with a 10px dot in the season's class; ring strokes, legend bars and table dots all read `var(--season)`, which the `.spring/.summer/.autumn/.winter` classes set from the four `:root` tokens — the tokens are declared in the light block and the two dark blocks, and nowhere else. Legend rows say "expansion · light rose" etc.; caption and How-to-read rewritten. Verified: syntax; the three cycles in light and dark; the legend and the Content tab's table on the phone; no errors.
