**Version 181.** Keren: "switch them — Spring in the design of what is now Reflation, white, bigger; Reflation smaller, in pink, where Spring is." `hubSet` now puts `meta.name` (the season) in the big Fraunces word and the theme in the small rose `.who` line; the theme line is omitted if it would repeat the name. Element ids and classes unchanged; the year hover (Bull year / Bear year) untouched. Verified: syntax; the hub today ("Autumn / Stagflation"), on a hovered quarter ("Autumn / Disinflation") and on a hovered year; no errors.

**Version 180.** Keren (dictated): "use the weather gradient from blue to orange in the season model — Spring is blue, Summer dark orange, Autumn yellow, Winter [garbled] … divide the seasons according to the season model; treat both cases of Spring and Autumn as one season." Asked which Winter colour she meant; she chose ice blue (paler than Spring). The ring's runs now group on `seasonGroup(key)` (springdeflation → spring, lateautumn → autumn), so the two Springs and the two Autumns each form one shape, and the whole run wears one colour: `--season-winter` #a3bfea, `--season-spring` #4a72d4, `--season-autumn` #efb336, `--season-summer` #e56a26 (dark #b4c6ee / #6b8fe8 / #f2bd46 / #ea7440) — the same blue-to-orange ramp as the V176 temperature scale, four steps of it. The temperature colouring went with it: `--temp-1…9`, `tempStep`, `tempWord`, the `.temp-scale` legend bar and the hub's "Temperature 6.4% · hot" line are gone; the legend lists Spring / Summer / Autumn / Winter with bar swatches and the themes in small text; How-to-read rewritten. Per-quarter paths stay, so a tap still reads one quarter and its six-way season. Verified: syntax; no leftover temperature symbols; the open cycle, the Subprime and the Long Boom in light and dark on the phone; no errors.

**Version 179.** Keren: "Moon." Republished V178's file unchanged with the Artifact tool's `icon` set to "moon"; confirmed in the built-in browser that the wrapper page now carries `data-artifact-icon="Moon|red"` with `/images/artifact-icons/Moon-180.png` as the apple-touch-icon. She will need to remove and re-add the home-screen bookmark once more for the phone to pick it up.

**Version 178.** Keren: "make the current cycle as big as you can while keeping it centered." `--dial` was a fixed 340px on desktop and `min(100vw − 72px, 380px)` on phones; it is now sized by the screen — desktop `min(100vw − 76px, 100vh − 220px, 560px)` (the height term keeps the wheel and its card head on one screen; 560px is where the 11-unit ring would start to look heavy), phones `min(100vw − 46px, 100vh − 220px)` with the wrap on −16px side margins so the wheel spans the card to 2px inside its border (346px on a 390px phone, was 314). Everything inside the SVG scales with it, the hub text included. Verified: the wheel measures 344 / 560 / 560 px on a 390-, 1280- and 820-wide viewport with equal margins each side; screenshots light and dark; no errors.

**Version 177.** Keren: "divide the outer wheel by season, not by year." The runs are now consecutive quarters in one season (`runs` in `drawDial`, grouped on `seg.season`), each inset by the cap plus the band's gap so the track shows through where the season changed; the temperature colouring per quarter is unchanged. Rather than the Version 176 "squeezed" fallback (a fixed 0.6° dot), a short run shrinks continuously — `inset = min(insetDeg, (len − 0.6)/2)` — so a one-quarter season on a ten-year cycle keeps a 0.6° core and its round caps reach toward the neighbours; on the Long Boom, whose seasons flipped quarter by quarter through the mid-nineties, the ring shows a chain of dots, which is what the data says. Legend intro, caption and How-to-read say "one shape per season". Verified: syntax; the open cycle, the Subprime and the Long Boom in light and dark on the phone; no errors.

**Version 176.** Keren: "the app icon is not changed on my phone even after I remove and add it to the home screen again; remove all redundancies from documentation; make the season wheel split into different shapes with rounded corners like in the market inner wheel; one gradient for the temperature (a Kelvin colour-temperature chart attached) will be the clearest indicator rather than many colours." **The ring:** the outer ring is split one round-ended shape per calendar year, inset by the stroke's cap plus the market band's own 1.2° so the two rings' joints line up radially (a per-season or per-quarter split was rejected: with round caps on an 11-unit stroke a single quarter — 7.8° on the ten-year cycles — has no room for two caps, and the Long Boom has nine single-quarter seasons); inside a year the quarters stay butt-joined with the 0.35° overlap, and `.cap` stubs (`data-cap`) round each year's ends, swelling with the active quarter (`mark` now toggles both). **The colour:** nine steps on one cold→hot ramp, `--temp-1…9` — periwinkle #4a72d4 / #7d9fe4 / #a9bfe8, sand #e2d09c at the 2% target, gold #f0c862 / #efb336, amber #ee9c30, orange #ec8230 / #e56a26 (dark variants a shade lighter) — chosen so the band's 1% and 3% edges fall on step boundaries (`tempStep`: <0, <1 | <1.67, <2.33, ≤3 | ≤4, ≤5.5, ≤8, above; 3.0 stays warm as the card counts it). First cut had the hot end at a red-orange that merged with the 2022 bear year under it; pulled back to orange, and the two pale steps deepened a touch. The six `--season-*` tokens, `seasonColourName` and the legend's season rows are gone; the legend opens with a **Temperature** scale bar (`.temp-scale`, "cold · below 1% / warm · 1–3% / hot · above 3%") and a caption; the hub adds "Temperature 6.4% · hot" (`tempWord`) under a hovered quarter's season; How-to-read rewritten. Noted for Keren: the Temperature chart keeps its three state colours (red / teal / periwinkle), so level on the ring and state on the chart are two scales. **The icon:** inspected the live page in the built-in browser — the artifact renders inside a sandboxed iframe on `claudeusercontent.com`, and the claude.ai wrapper page carries its own `<link rel="apple-touch-icon" href="/images/artifact-icons/Chart-180.png">` (`data-artifact-icon="Chart|red"`), which is what a phone's home screen reads; nothing in the artifact's own markup can reach it, and the host's icon set is a fixed catalogue of line icons (chart, moon, sun, heart, orbit, activity, temperature, calendar, star, globe, book, clock …; no lotus or flower). The V174 favicon / apple-touch-icon / web-app-title / theme-colour links were removed from the page (10 KB of dead weight); the lotus files stay in the scratchpad. The only lever is the Artifact tool's `icon` word — left as is until Keren picks one. **Documentation:** the history's "Standing notes" (backtest, palette and type audits) were duplicates of this archive and became a pointer; the app doc lost its copies of the palette rule, the chart-language spec and the tab bar's look (all in the design-system doc), a stale "header pill" line (the compile date lives in the Sources sheet), and the stale icon wording in the dial paragraph; design-system §4 rewritten for the temperature ring. Verified: syntax and ids; the dial in light and dark on the open cycle, the Subprime (cold years) and the Long Boom; hover on a year-end quarter (stub swells, hub line reads "Temperature 6.4% · hot"); the legend on the phone; no errors. Live was still V175 at publish time.

**Version 175.** Keren, on seeing 174: "the season icons don't look good — only colour, like the bull/bears: a continuous line that changes colour along the way — the simplest and most coherent structure; avoid green and red in the seasons, they are already in use everywhere." The seasons ring is now one continuous 11-unit stroke on the 16-unit track, one butt-joined arc per quarter (`.dial-moon.<season>` with `data-q`; a round-capped stub under the first and last quarter; a 0.35° overlap so no anti-aliased seam shows), in six new tokens `--season-winter/springdeflation/spring/summer/autumn/lateautumn` — periwinkle #5b7fd6, violet #7f66c2, plum #8a4d97, gold #c98500, coral #e2714f, rose #c85f8b (dark #7f9ce6 / #a48fe0 / #c9a0d6 / #eda100 / #ec835a / #e08bae) — a progression that avoids the market's teal and red. The hovered/parked quarter swells to the track's full width instead of wearing a ring. The grey track starts 4° right of 12 o'clock so it sits under the first quarter. `seasonGlyphs`/`seasonIcon`/`MOON_R` are gone; `seasonColourName` names the colours for the legend (bar swatches, `.season-sw`) and the How-to-read guide. Verified: syntax; the dial in both themes on the open and a closed cycle; no errors.

**Version 174.** Keren: "change the moon infographics to seasonal ones (see attached reference) — in a way that matches our current design system"; and "make this the app icon" (a white line lotus on a dark disc). The dial's quarters are now **season icons** — a glyph on a disc in the season's paint colour, the reference's flat style: snowflake on periwinkle (Winter · deflation, `--cold`), sprout on teal (Spring · deflation, `--good`), leaf on teal (Spring · reflation), sun on red (Summer · inflation, `--critical`), acorn on amber (Autumn · disinflation, `--warning`), bare branch on coral (Autumn · stagflation, `--serious`). `seasonGlyphs[key]()` draws each on a 24-unit box; `seasonIcon(key, r)` wraps it in `<g class="season-icon key">` with the disc; the glyph wears `--season-glyph` (white light / #100d12 dark); a vein or seam (`.cut`) is drawn in the disc's own colour; the hovered quarter's disc takes the accent ring. The legend rows, the How-to-read wheel paragraph and the legend caption say "icon" and name each glyph; `moonPhase`/`moonShape` are gone (the year badge keeps the `--moon-*` tokens for its black disc). The **app icon**: a white line lotus (seven petals) on the brand plum #8a4d97 — an SVG favicon (circle) and a 180px PNG `apple-touch-icon` (square, iOS rounds it) as data URIs after the `<title>`, with `apple-mobile-web-app-title` "Gyneconomy" and `theme-color`. Verified: syntax (an apostrophe in a single-quoted string was the one slip, fixed with \u2019); the dial and legend in both themes, on the phone and desktop, on the open and a closed cycle; no errors.

**Version 173.** Keren: "change the name of the trade cycle to COVID-19 Cycle." Done in `marketCycles`.

**Version 172.** Keren: "in the AI cycle colour the future years in grey." The open cycle's Calendar ring is now scaled the way the dial is — to a typical cycle (`typicalCycleYears`, the median closed length, or longer if the cycle already is): the years so far in colour, the year in progress only as far as today (`cycleYtdFraction`), and the rest of the ring left as the grey track. Closed cycles fill the ring as before. Verified: syntax; the row on the phone; no errors.

**Version 171.** Keren: "change The Long Boom to Long Boom Cycle." Done in `marketCycles`.

**Version 170.** Keren: "remove 'The' from the cycles in the calendar." The five names are now Long Boom, Dot-Com Cycle, Subprime Cycle, Trade Cycle, AI Cycle (the `marketCycles` entries; the top bar and the Calendar rows show them bare). The two places that use the name inside a sentence — the popup's "year 5 of the AI Cycle" and the Temperature sub-line "CPI, year over year · the AI Cycle, since 2022" — take a lowercase "the".

**Version 169.** Keren: "change The Global Trade Cycle to The Trade Cycle." The `marketCycles` name changed; everything that names the cycle reads from it. Verified: syntax.

**Version 168.** Keren: "change The Subprime Mortgage Cycle to The Subprime Cycle." The one `marketCycles` name changed; everything that names the cycle (the Calendar row, the top bar when it is open, the popups, the hub) reads from it. Verified: syntax.

**Version 167.** Keren, on the Calendar: "I don't need the divisions inside the cycle — make it smooth; I don't need the peak in the preview; the S&P 500 and the percentages should be below, three lines per cycle preview." And on the dial: "when I change the years and press This season, it's not updated to the recent change." Built: `eraRingSvg` draws the years butt-ended so the colour simply changes where a year turned, with only the ring's two ends rounded (a short round-capped stub under the first and last year), no peak mark; every row is three lines — years, name, then `.era-market` with the S&P 500 chip on its own line; the caption says so. `quarterPopup` is prose for every quarter now, not only the present: heading, the cycle note (present only), then the season's *In the economy / In the body / What usually comes next* — the data popup of Versions 116–166 (stat rows, the why-sentence, Fed/VIX/OAS/PMI) is removed entirely. Verified: syntax; a parked quarter's popup (Q2 2023 · Disinflation reads as prose); the Calendar on the phone; no errors.

**Version 166.** Two of Keren's asks, built on top of the refresh below. (1) **"This season" reads as prose** — Keren: "I want to see the text 'four years into an AI-driven bull run…' and then an explanation about stagflation; the data already exists in the app, I don't need it again in the popup." For the present (the hub's default and the open cycle's badge) `quarterPopup` now returns the heading, the cycle's note in Fraunces italic (`cycleNowNote`, or `era.blurb` for a closed cycle's close), then the season explained the way the Content tab does it — *In the economy*, *In the body*, *What usually comes next* from `seasonReading[season]` — and no stat rows. Past quarters (moons, the scrubber) keep their data popups, since nothing else holds a past quarter's readings. (2) **The Calendar** — Keren: "make some space between the cycles… I want the preview not to be the growth but a ring that shows the S&P 500 during that cycle — the least amount of information we need to recognise the cycle." `#cycle-list` is a flex column with a 14px gap; each row's preview is `eraRingSvg(cyc)` — the dial's market band in miniature (64×64, R 26, seam at 12 o'clock, one round-capped segment per calendar year from `sp500AnnualReturns`, teal up / red down, the year in progress at 0.5, the peak year's pale disc + dot) — and the growth chip and trend tag are replaced by one chip, "S&P 500 +70% so far" (`eraMarketTotal`, the compounded return across the cycle's years). `eraSparkSvg` and the spark CSS are gone; the Calendar caption explains the ring. Merge note: the weekday refresh had published a newer version (below) while this was being built, so its seven data hunks were patched onto this build and the live file was read in full before publishing. Verified: syntax; ids; the popup and the Calendar on the phone and desktop; no errors.

**Version 165 (scheduled refresh, other session).** The weekday refresh task: `DATA_COMPILED` → Sep 18, 2026; the Treasury curve; VIX 15.44 (Sep 17 close) with its note; CAPE 40.99 (41.0×); the OAS date; the S&P 500 2026 YTD 12.52. No structural change. (It read the app doc by its old path, before the rename; its prompt now points at the renamed docs.)

**Version 164.** The type (Keren: "go ahead … and keep a file for the design system including typeface"). One scale — 11 / 12.5 / 14 / 15 / 17 / 20 / 26 / 30 / 40 — every `font-size` in the stylesheet snapped to it (26 sizes → 9; 10→11, 11.5/12→12.5, 13/13.5→14, 14.5/15.5→15, 16/16.5→17, 19/22→20, 24/25→26, 32→30); 23 mono rules moved to Public Sans (every uppercase kicker/section label, `.tag`, `.status-badge`, `.temp-today-date`, `.cv-trend`, `.flagcell`, `.details-link`, `.labreport-meta`, table heads, timing labels) so the mono carries figures and axis ticks only; 28 rules demoted from 700 to 600 (figures, the mono, Fraunces, the display numbers and pills keep 700); the Content tab's reading text and the reading blocks at 15px/1.6; the Google Fonts request drops Public Sans 800 and adds Plex Mono 700. The faces themselves are unchanged. **`claude/gyneconomy-design-system.md` created** — the standing reference for colour (paint/ink/wash roles and every hex), type (faces, jobs, scale, weights), the charts' language, components, fixed wording and accessibility floors; update it whenever the system changes. Verified: syntax; screenshots of the three tabs in both themes and the phone; no errors.

**Version 163.** The palette applied (Keren: "plum and periwinkle is good"). Every meaning now has three tones — the token keeps its name as the PAINT and gains an `-ink` (words) beside its `-wash` (pill grounds): `--accent` plum #8a4d97 / `--accent-ink` #5e2a6b (dark #c9a0d6 / #e3c6ea), `--on-accent` for button text; `--rose` #c85f8b / `--rose-ink` #a8436f (dark #e08bae / #f0a9c6); `--good` = the teal #1ea8a3 / `--good-ink` #0f6f6c (dark #3dc2bb / #5fd6cf) — the second green is gone; `--warning` #c98500 / `--warning-ink` #8a5a00 (dark #eda100 / #f2c24d); `--serious` #e2714f / #b0431f (dark #ec835a / #f5a385); `--critical` = the dial's red #d63a2e / `--critical-ink` #b33d29 (dark #eb4839 / #f28a78); `--cold` periwinkle #5b7fd6 / `--cold-ink` #3c5cb5 (dark #7f9ce6 / #a6baf0); `--text-muted` #7d7680 in light (4.5:1). Every rule that writes in a semantic colour moved to its ink: tags, status badges, `.cv-trend`, kpi values, spread-tile values, flagcells, word-tile icons, the contact error, and all rose text (kickers, cycle names, hub names, reading heads, callout labels, body-terms); the paint rules (dots, rings, range bars, spark bars, swatches) are unchanged. The model table's hard-coded #4f8fd6/#e0442c became `--cold`/`--critical`. The chart badge's dot is `--surface` so it shows on the dark theme's pale disc. Verified: syntax; screenshots of Cycle/Analysis/Content in both themes and on the phone; no errors.

**Version 162.** Keren, with a dashboard reference (big stat numbers over the chart, a rounded curve): the Growth card is titled **GDP GROWTH**; the total expansion over the closed years is the card's big number (`#growth-stats`, `.cv-stat-v` 30px mono) with "TOTAL GROWTH, 2022–2025" and the trend word with an arrow (↓ falling, in the tag's colour) beneath it — "over 4 closed years" and the cagr chip are gone, and the sub-line is empty (the read-out names the series); **"average +2.6%" is labelled in the graph beside the dashed line** — after the line's end when there is room, otherwise the first of several spots (either end, above or below) whose box clears every reading, the line's straight runs and the badge, scored by collisions (`avgPts`/`hits`), and it stays visible on phones (`.avg-label` escapes the rule that hides the 2% tag); legend rows read "positive growth" / "negative growth"; the curves are rounder — `curvePath` (Catmull–Rom, K 0.24) replaces `monotonePath` on both charts (the monotone cubic is still in the file, unused; it never overshot but flattened every peak). Mid-build Keren asked for **warm · 1–3% in the app's teal** ("one consistent palette … legible and beautiful, feminine"): `--warm` now carries the dial's teal in both themes (#1ea8a3 / #3dc2bb, was yellow), `--cold` is a token (#4f8fd6 / #6aa5e6) replacing the hard-coded blue, and the guide/(i) say "teal". Verified: syntax; ids; Playwright four ways plus closed cycles; label placement checked on the phone for the open and a closed cycle; no errors. Open for next: Keren's palette wish — a consistent, feminine, legible token set across the whole app — not started; propose an audit before touching anything.

**Version 161.** Keren: "i don't need the pink trend, last eight quarters. i already have trend falling on the chart and the average." The pink eight-quarter trend line (drawn on the Growth chart since Version 131, haloed since 153) is gone, with its CSS, its legend row and every mention in the How-to-read guide and the Growth (i). The fit itself is untouched — `r.growthSlopeQ`/`r.growthTrend` still decide the season's growth direction — and the chart's end read-out ("trend falling") and the head's tag carry it in words. The dashed closed-years average stays. Verified: syntax; screenshots; no errors.

**Version 160.** Keren's legend wording: the market group is headed **S&P 500** (was "Market"); Bull year — "positive return"; Bear year — "negative return"; Year in progress — "in progress"; Peak year — "highest return". Verified: syntax; the legend popup's text on the phone; no errors.

**Version 159.** Keren: "change 'The cycle's peak' to 'Peak year' in the legend and anywhere else where it appears." The legend row reads **Peak year** (small: "the cycle's most profitable year — the S&P 500's best annual return"); the hub's line on that year ends "· Peak year"; the How-to-read guide says "the white dot marks the peak year"; the quarter popup's market row says "peak year — the cycle's most profitable". No reader-visible "the cycle's peak" remains (code comments keep the phrase). Verified: syntax; hub read-out on the subprime cycle's 2013; no errors.

**Version 158.** Keren: the cycle's peak "is supposed to be calculated per year — at the current state the cycle peak is always at the end, but I want to know what year was the most profitable; in the subprime mortgage cycle it's 2013." The peak is now the cycle's most profitable YEAR: `peakYear` = the calendar year with the highest S&P 500 total return (the year in progress at its return to date), not the compounded high (which in a rising market is always the last year). The mark sits in the middle of that year's band segment rather than at its end; the hub names it on that year ("+43.7% since 2008 · the cycle's peak"); the legend reads "its most profitable year — the S&P 500's best annual return"; the How-to-read guide and the quarter popup say the same. `cumByYear` is unchanged (the hub's "since <year>" line). Peaks now: 1995 (Long Boom), 2003 (Dot-Com), 2013 (Subprime), 2019 (Global Trade), 2023 (AI). Verified: syntax; each cycle's peak computed independently from the returns table; the dial mark's `data-year` on the subprime cycle = 2013; no errors.

**Version 157.** Keren, with a second reference (pastel cyan/pink lines, tiny hollow dots, a wash melting to the bottom, dotted rows): "make the graphs more delicate and feminine… and stay as two aligned panels with a single crosshair"; then, mid-build: "all cycles back button should be in the top menu instead of a link under it." Charts: line 1.75px (was 3), dots r 2.75 with 1.5px strokes and a closer cadence (≥13px apart), wash opacity 0.2 with a softer fade (0.9 → 0.04 at the baseline), badges r 5, trend 2.25px with a 5.5px halo, faint dotted gridlines on the label rows (`.grid-line.fine`, the target/zero rows excepted), legend dots finer. One crosshair: `chartLink` collects each chart's show/hide as they draw (reset by `drawTemperature`), and both hit-rects call `linkedShow/linkedHide`, so pointing or tapping either panel reads the same month on both — the Growth panel's crosshair sits on the hovered month (one line through both panels) while its dot sits on the quarter's point; a month with no quarter hides Growth's reading only. Back button: the `.back-link` under the top bar is gone; `#topbar-back` (the sheets' round back arrow, grid column 1, hidden by default) appears when a cycle is opened from the Calendar, the top bar's title becomes the cycle's name ("Current Cycle" for the open one), and the arrow returns to the list and the "Calendar" title; every tab switch hides it. Verified: syntax; ids; Playwright mobile/desktop light/dark, tap and hover on both panels, the Calendar open → back → other tab flow — no errors.

**Version 156.** Keren, after the UX Planet line-chart article and a reference chart she loves (a thick smooth line, hollow dots, a soft wash, a filled end dot, minimal axes): "as a first step, let's just convert the charts into line charts." The Temperature and Growth charts are now lines in the dial's language: a 3px round-capped monotone-cubic line (`monotonePath`, Fritsch–Carlson — no invented peaks between readings), coloured by where it runs through a vertical hard-stop gradient (`hardStopGradient`: red/yellow/blue at the band edges for Temperature, teal/red at zero for Growth — the `<stop>`s take their colours from CSS so the theme switch recolours them), a wash from the line to its baseline (2% target / zero) under a mask that fades toward the baseline (`fadeMask`), hollow dots on the readings (`dotEvery` keeps them ≥18px apart: every other month on desktop, every third on the phone; one per quarter for Growth), the existing badge on the last reading, and a filled dot that follows the hover/tap alongside the crosshair and tooltip. The band track, dotted target/zero, dashed cycle average and pink trend stay; the trend's halo lightened to 0.6 so the line shows through. Legend swatches became hollow dots; the (i) texts, How-to-read guide and Growth legend wording updated (no more "columns"/"pills"). Pill CSS removed. Verified: syntax; Playwright mobile dark/light, desktop light/dark, hover states, a closed cycle (2018–2021, which shows all three colours and the blue dip of 2020) — no page errors. Next, per Keren: stacking line charts to compare (e.g. Temperature and Growth) — layout to be agreed first.

**Version 155.** Keren: one grey dot still missing. The cut-off left the last quarter-dot short of the track's rounded end; dots now run to the track end itself (`dotTo = 360 − TRACK_GAP + 1`), which adds the final dot beside the drop (19 dots on the current cycle). Verified: syntax; dial screenshot; no errors.

**Version 154.** Keren: the dial is missing its grey dots at the top-left, beside the drop. The quarter dots ahead stopped at the end of the years' arc (`ARC`, 24° short of the track's end) rather than at the open track's end, leaving the stretch next to the drop bare. They now run to the track's end beside the drop (`dotTo = 360 − TRACK_GAP − cap`), the loop allowing two extra years of dots, so the ring reads as one continuous run up to the seam. Verified: syntax; Playwright 390px dark dial screenshot, 18 dots; no errors.

**Version 153.** Keren: GDP by months, aligned with inflation, and the trend line is not clear enough. Real GDP is only published by quarter, so each quarter's figure is now drawn across its three months — one pill per month with the Temperature chart's exact width and gap, so the two charts share one rhythm and every column lines up; the legend and the (i) say so. The eight-quarter trend is now drawn over the pills, 3.5px in pink with a surface-coloured halo beneath it, extended to the edges of its first and last quarters, with a matching wider swatch in the legend. Verified: syntax; Playwright 390px light/dark and 1280px; no errors.

**Version 152.** Keren: the GDP growth chart should show by quarters like the inflation chart — and the legend's colours were still the old purple. `drawGrowth` rewritten: one pill per quarter from `gdpQuarterlyYoY` (every quarter from the cycle's first year up to the last month on the Temperature axis), three month-slots wide with round caps, from a dotted zero line — teal for growth, red for a quarter that shrank — on the Temperature chart's exact x-axis with the same year labels; the same end read-out as Temperature (quarter label, the value, "trend rising/falling/flat") and the badge on the last pill; the pink line is now the fitted trend through the last eight quarters — the season model's own growth reading — and the dashed line the cycle's average over its closed years; hover/tap resolves any month to its quarter ("Q2 2026 · real GDP +2.1% year over year") with the touch behaviour of Version 144; chart height matches Temperature. The legend swatches now take the pills' colours (teal, red, a pink trend stroke); the yearly-bar code, the `ytd` bar and their labels are gone; the Growth (i) rewritten and GDPC1 added to its sources (BEA via FRED). Verified: syntax; Playwright 390px light/dark and 1280px — current cycle and the 2020 cycle (red pills in 2020); no errors.

**Version 150.** Keren: add a Contact section with a simple form that sends a title and a message to her private email without showing it. A published page has no server, and the two runtime features that could store or forward messages (`db`, `mcp`) make an artifact organisation-internal — no public link — so, with Keren's agreement, the form hands the note to the visitor's own mail app: a Contact row under Resources opens `#sheet-contact` (Title, Message, Send); Send validates that a message exists, builds `mailto:` with the subject "Gyneconomy — <title>" and the message as body, and opens it, with a hint if no mail app responds. The address is assembled at send time from three parts and never appears in the markup (0 literal occurrences); the visitor's mail app will still show it as the recipient. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright 390px dark and 1280px light — sheet opens, empty-message hint, no errors.

**Version 151.** Keren, after sending a fitness-app chart as a reference: the charts should match the cycle dial — the app's main piece — and stay legible and practical. Rather than copy the reference's area-and-hatch look (which suits a single line only), every chart now borrows the dial's own vocabulary. Temperature: each month is a stroke with round caps (a pill), like the dial's segments, in the dial's red / the warm yellow / blue; the 1–3% band is the dial's grey track laid flat with rounded ends; the 2% target is dotted; horizontal gridlines are gone (axis labels stay); today's column carries the dial's badge — a dark disc with a white dot — with the value above. Growth: capsule bars in the market band's colours (teal for a year that grew, red for one that shrank, the year in progress lighter), values inside the capsule in dark ink (white on the light theme's red) so the average and trend lines never cross them. Analysis: the spread line is the market band's stroke (3px, round caps) in the primary ink with teal/red washes above/below zero and the recessions in track grey; the yield and GDP lines thickened to 3.25px with round caps; gridlines dotted everywhere. A `r` variable shadowing the reading object broke the first build (caught by the page-error check) and was renamed `cap`. Verified: syntax; Playwright 390px light/dark and 1280px — Temperature (current and a closed cycle), Growth, spread, yields, GDP; no errors.

**Version 149.** Keren: merge the menu's footnote, the compile line and the disclaimer into the Sources screen's lede, as short as possible. The menu now ends at the Resources card; the Sources sheet opens with one paragraph — primary sources, the two labelled compilations, grouped by section and the same list the (i) notes cite, then "Data compiled <date>" (still `#asof-text`, filled from `DATA_COMPILED`, now inline in the paragraph) — a snapshot, not a live feed; figures can revise — and "For information only, not financial advice." The `<footer>` element and its CSS are gone. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright 390px screenshots of the menu and the Sources sheet; no errors.

**Version 148.** Keren: remove "reading Mrs. Market". The eyebrow under the menu's title is gone (and its CSS); the Guide heading moves up to fill the space. Verified: syntax; Playwright 390px menu screenshot; no errors.

**Version 147.** Keren asked what else belongs in the side menu; of the suggestions she chose "How to read" and "About the book". Built: the menu now has a Guide section (How to read · About the book) above Resources (Sources). Each row opens a sheet in the Sources screen's frame (`#sheet-howto`, `#sheet-book`, `#sheet-sources`) through one mechanism — a `.menu-row[data-sheet]` opens `#sheet-<name>`, `[data-sheet-back]` or Escape returns to the menu — with prose cards (`.menu-prose`) instead of link rows. How to read covers the wheel (moons, market band, peak dot, press-and-hold), the six seasons and the fixed 1–3% band, the Temperature and Growth charts, the Rates/Action/Feeling/Energy tiles and the four tabs, drafted from the app's own (i) texts. About the book is a FIRST DRAFT for Keren to edit: the idea (body = economy, markets = nervous system, seasons), the framework's two influences (Marks; Pope & Wurlitzer), the board as the book's companion, and an author paragraph that says the book is in progress. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright 390px dark and 1280px light — all three sheets open and close back to the menu, Sources still 55 links; no errors.

**Version 146.** Keren asked whether the 1–3% inflation range is fixed or drawn from the Fed. It is fixed, and the Fed publishes no range — its objective is a point, 2% on PCE — so the app now says so where it matters: the Temperature (i) explains that the 1–3% band is the board's own fixed tolerance around the Fed's 2% point, read on CPI because that is the series readers know; the Season Model (i) on the Content tab says the same, adds that some central banks (Bank of England, RBA) make such a band explicit, and states plainly that nothing is drawn live from the Fed. Wording only; no figures or thresholds changed. Verified: syntax (an unescaped apostrophe inside a single-quoted string was caught by the check and fixed); Playwright opened both (i) sheets and found the new text; no errors.

**Version 144.** Keren: on the phone, tapping a bar in the Temperature chart opens the reading and nothing closes it. The chart tooltips (`attachHoverTracking`, shared by the Temperature, Growth-peer, spread-history and yield charts) only had a touchstart handler — a tap showed the reading and there was no touch path to hide it. Now a tap shows it, a drag scrubs along the chart, and tapping the same bar again, tapping anywhere else on the page, or scrolling closes it; the synthetic mouse events a tap fires afterwards are ignored for 800ms so they can't re-open it, and mouseleave no longer fights a touch-opened reading. Verified with Playwright's iPhone 13 profile: tap shows → tap another bar moves → same bar hides → tap outside hides; syntax ok, no errors.

**Version 145.** Keren: rename "in range" to "warm" and make it yellow. A `--warm` token (#e2b300 light / #f0c41b dark, a true yellow rather than the amber `--warning`) now colours the Temperature chart's 1–3% bars, its legend swatch and the target band; "in range" became "warm" in the legend, the bar tooltip, the chart's read-out ("warm · rising") and the Temperature (i). Verified: syntax; Playwright 390px light/dark screenshots of the card; no errors.

**Version 143.** Keren: give the cycle container a title in the Temperature card's style — "Gyneconomy" with an (i) beside it that opens the legend. The kebab is gone; the season card now opens with the same `.cv-head`/`.cv-kicker` header the Temperature and Growth cards use (`#cycle-kicker`, "GYNECONOMY" + an `info-btn expand-btn` whose detail index the legend builder sets), first in the card on phones (`order:-2`, ahead of the wheel's `order:-1`), and the card's padding now matches the Temperature card's (18px) so the two kickers sit on one vertical line at 390px and 1280px. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright 390px dark and 1280px light — the (i) opens the Legend sheet, kicker alignment measured; no errors.

**Version 142.** Keren: make the info icons open in the new popup format as well. The small (i) buttons on Temperature, Growth, Rates and the three Insights tiles used a floating popover (`#info-pop`) anchored to the button; they now open the same sheet/modal as every other (i). `infoIcon(html, title)` files its text through `expandBtn()` — plain text is wrapped in an `<h4>` of the icon's label (Temperature, Rates, Financial stress, Valuation (CAPE), Market fear (VIX)) and a caption paragraph; Growth's text already carried its heading — and the button keeps its `info-btn` look with the `expand-btn` behaviour. The popover element, its CSS and its positioning script are gone. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright 390px dark and 1280px light — all six info buttons on the Cycle and Analysis tabs open the sheet with the right heading; no errors.

**Version 141.** Keren: going to the sources page and tapping back reloads the app — "a weird glitch". The back arrow was a real page load (index.html#menu), so the whole dashboard re-rendered before the menu popped open. Fix: Sources is now a screen inside the app. The menu's Sources row is a button that opens `#sources-view` — the same full-screen sheet as the menu (z-index 71), a plain "Sources" title, the lede, and one card of outbound links per section — built on first open from `window.__sources` with the same URL-keyword grouping `gen-sources.py` uses (55 links, six groups; anything unmatched would land in an "Other" group), each link opening in a new tab. Its back arrow just hides the sheet, so the menu is exactly where it was, instantly; Escape closes the Sources sheet first, then the menu. `sources.html` stays published (its back arrow still lands on index.html#menu, which the app still honours) but nothing in the app links to it now. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright 390px dark and 1280px light — 55 links in six groups, back returns to the menu with no navigation, Escape order correct; no errors.

**Version 140.** Keren: the X is a bit too big — make it the menu button's size; and after refreshing the app, opening the sources page has a delay. Built: the popup's X is 44px on every screen (the menu button's size), sheet body padding trimmed to match. For the delay: the app's head now preconnects to the two Google Fonts hosts and prefetches `sources.html` while the dashboard is open, so the page is already in the browser cache when the menu row is tapped; the sources page itself loads its font stylesheet without blocking the first paint (`media="print" onload="this.media='all'"` with a `<noscript>` fallback), so it renders in the system stack at once and swaps the web fonts in. Both files published together. Verified: syntax check; Playwright 390px light/dark and 1280px — sheets open, scroll and close; no errors.

**Version 139.** Keren, with Clue's article sheet: the kebab goes to the top-left of the cycle container, and the popups should expand to full screen with an X like Clue's. Desktop keeps the centred card, with the same round accent X (40px, an SVG cross instead of the × character). Verified: syntax check; Playwright 390px light/dark and 1280px — season, resilience (i) and legend sheets open, body scrolls, X closes; no errors.
