**Version 132.** Keren: "Can we make the sources come from verified official sources? I want this app to be reliable." The full citation audit: all 59 links reviewed, every secondary source (Fox Business, Cato, PGPF, usgovernmentspending, the Senate JEC, multpl's debt table, History.com, the FDR Library, Yahoo Finance, Advisor Perspectives, GuruFocus ×3, Macroption, Sherwood, fedratecalc, infineo, Current Market Valuation, Forbes, Trading Economics for OAS and M2V) replaced by the primary series — FRED's OMB fiscal series, Treasury Fiscal Data, CBO, BLS via FRED, Cboe via FRED VIXCLS plus Cboe's own post naming the 9.14/82.69 records, Fed Z.1 + BEA GDP, Shiller's dataset, the Fed's FOMC calendar, Census Historical Statistics for 1933, FRED T10Y3M/T10Y3MM for the un-inversion record, S&P DJI as originator of the annual returns with NYU Stern as cross-check — and every figure re-checked against it (FRED read through the desktop app's built-in browser, since the fetch summarizer misreads long tables). What the re-check changed: the Buffett Indicator is now computed from Z.1 — 256% for Q2 2026 (equities $83.1T ÷ GDP $32.5T), a new record, so the meter's max moved from 237 to 256 and the note no longer says "fourth-highest"; productivity's max 12.3 → 20.9 (Q2 2020 is the BLS series' true high); the deficit range −2.4/29.6 → −2.3/26.9 (FRED's OMB series); VIX Sep 16 close 17.77 → 17.71 (Cboe's close; the Yahoo figure was off) and the market-reaction sentence now quotes FRED closes (S&P −0.45%, Dow −1.2%, 10-year 5.01%, highest since 2007); OAS 2.76 (Sep 15) → 2.70 (Sep 16); April 2020 unemployment 14.7 → 14.8 (14.7 as first reported); the 2007/2020 stress-history rows re-read from FRED (debt 34.8/98.3, deficit 1.1/14.5, 2020 productivity 5.3 current vintage); the un-inversion lag rows re-derived from FRED daily + monthly (1989–91 "Sep 1989 – Jan 1990", 6–10 mo; 2001 "Jan–Feb 2001", 1–2 mo; range 1–10 months) and today's count made precise — 21 months from December 2024 (first monthly average at zero), 12 from September 2025 (unbroken positive run; last negative daily close Oct 16, 2025) — replacing the looser 23/14; the 10Y–2Y dates corrected to FRED's (inverted Jul 6, 2022; un-inverted Sep 6, 2024 after touching zero Aug 27); the interest note's "3.6% by 2033" → CBO's "4.6% by 2036". The stress score moved 72 → 75 (still Critical), from the productivity and deficit ranges. Two compilations remain, labelled: Slickcharts (S&P DJI's calendar-year figures) and Trading Economics (ISM's members-only record high/low). `sources.html` regenerated (55 links, six groups) with a lede that says so. Both scheduled-task prompts rewritten for the primary-source policy (the refresh task may raise the Buffett max when a record posts and must advance the lag counts monthly). Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright opened every (i) and Details modal and found each new figure and citation; diff confined to data, notes and source lists.

**Version 131.** Keren: the Growth ring and the growth chart below it duplicate each other and clutter the view — move the chart up under Temperature, drop the ring ("not really indicative of the growth itself"), and "I want the years to align: when I see years in Temperature, I want the same years aligned in Growth." Built: the Growth card now sits directly under the Temperature card and is drawn by a new `drawGrowth(m, scale)` on the Temperature chart's own x-axis — `drawTemperature` hands over its width, padding, month-slot scale and year-label rule at the end of every draw (so a resize redraws both) — one bar per calendar year spanning that year's twelve month-slots, the value inside the bar's end (white; outside when the bar is too short, and always outside for the lighter year in progress), the year labels at the middle of each year; the Temperature chart's year labels moved from January to mid-year to match, and a scripted check confirmed the two charts' year labels land on identical x-positions at 1280px and 390px. The year in progress, for the open cycle, is new: the average of the year's quarterly year-over-year readings so far, drawn at half opacity and marked * ("2026 so far (2 quarters) · +2.4%"), left out of the averages. The cycle's dashed average and fitted trend are drawn behind the bars so they never cross the labels. The Growth ring is gone from the vitals strip (Rates stands alone, full width), `eraGrowthChartSvg` and the old `.gdp-era-chart` block are removed, the Growth (i) rewritten, and the card's legend says what each bar colour is (the "year that shrank" item only when one did). Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright light/dark/phone on the AI Cycle and the Subprime cycle (ten bars incl. 2009's −2.6%); no errors.

**Version 130.** Keren, from her phone: the tabs and the legend button don't work on mobile. No WebKit in the sandbox to reproduce, so this is reasoned from a known iOS Safari rule: a tap fires touchstart and synthesized mouse events before its click, and if the handlers for those change the page, Safari suppresses the click. Since Version 116 a `document`-level `touchstart` listener called `reset()`, which rewrote the hub's HTML on every touch anywhere — so every tap on the phone mutated the page before its click, and every button died; Chromium (desktop and touch-emulated) doesn't apply the rule, which is why it never showed here. Fix: `reset()` now does nothing unless a hover/tap is actually showing (`shown` flag set by `readTarget`), and the outside-the-dial reset moved from `touchstart` to `click` (after the tap completes). Verified in touch-emulated Chromium with a MutationObserver on the hub: zero mutations during a tap on the legend button or a tab (both now open/switch), a tapped moon still shows in the hub and a tap elsewhere still clears it, "This season" still opens. Keren asked to confirm on the phone.

**Version 129.** Keren: colour the "Bull year" / "Bear year" word in the hub in the corresponding green and red, accessibly — and, a moment later, "it can even be a background colour, a lighter shade of that same colour." Built as a soft pill: the word in a darker cut of the band's colour on an 18% wash of it (`--bull-ink`/`--bull-wash`, `--bear-ink`/`--bear-wash`; classes `.bull`/`.bear` on the theme element, set in `hubShowYear`, cleared in `hubSet`). Contrast was computed, not eyeballed: the band's own teal is only 2.9:1 on white, so the light-mode inks are #0f6f6c (5.0:1 on its wash) and #b33d29 (4.6:1); dark mode uses the band's teal #3dc2bb (5.3:1 on a 22% wash) and a lighter coral #f28a78 (5.3:1 — the band's own #e8654f was 3.9:1). All four clear WCAG AA for normal text, well past the 3:1 the display-size word needs. Verified in both themes with hover screenshots (sent to Keren); no errors.

**Version 128.** Keren: drop "This cycle's peak" from the hub — the mark on the band and the legend say it. Every year's read-out is now the same three lines: Bull/Bear year, the S&P 500 total return, "+69.6% since 2022". The popup's S&P row still notes the peak year. Verified by hover; no errors.

**Version 127.** Keren: break the peak line into two — "This cycle's peak" / "+xx% since xxxx". Done in the band's year read-out: on the peak year the hub shows "This cycle's peak" on one line and "+69.6% since 2022" on the next; other years keep the single "−18.1% since 2022" line. Verified by hover; no errors.

**Version 126.** Keren: "drop the icons, they don't look good; when I hover over the current year just write 2026 Today, and put the cycle's peak in numbers below the total return." The bull/bear glyphs are gone (markup, CSS, `marketGlyph`/`marketGlyphSvg`, the `year` argument to `hubSet`). The band's year read-out: the date line is "2026 · Today" for the year in progress (no more "year to date · the cycle's peak"), then Bull/Bear year, the S&P 500 total return, and a new line — the compounded total return since the cycle's first year at that year's end (`cycleModel().cumByYear`), written "The cycle's peak · +69.6% since 2022" on the peak year and "Since 2022 · −18.1%" on any other. The popup's S&P row carries the same figure. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright hovering the 2026 segment, a 2022 segment and the peak mark, and the popup row; no errors.

**Version 125.** Keren, three things. (1) "We don't need the market container at the bottom of each cycle page because I already see it in the cycle itself. Do you agree?" Agreed, with one honest note: the year cards also showed each year's return and the peak-year ring; the return is still one hover away (a band segment reads out in the hub, and the season popup gives the year's return), and the peak moved onto the band (3). Removed: the Market card's markup, `yearCardsHtml`, `calendarDetail`, the year-card and calendar-legend CSS; the Slickcharts source stays in the sources list since the band and the popup still use the returns. (2) "A bull or bear icon below the year and above the title, inside the cycle, but only if it will look good." Drawn as solid glyphs with the face cut out in the surface (the idiom the season faces used): a bull with horns in the band's teal for a year the S&P 500 closed up, a bear in its coral for one that closed down, lighter for the year in progress; `marketGlyphSvg(year, ongoing)`, in `#season-wheel-hub-mkt` between the date line and the theme word, following whichever year the hub shows (today's, the scrubbed quarter's, or a hovered band segment's). It looked good at 22px — the first bull read as a rabbit; the horns were redrawn as thick curved strokes and the muzzle widened before it went in. (3) "The cycle peak should be the highest point of the S&P 500 in the current cycle, with a white dot like the attached" (a Clue ovulation mark on the band). `cycleModel` now computes `peakYear` as the S&P 500's highest level in the cycle — the annual total returns compounded from the cycle's first year, the year in progress at its return to date (annual resolution, as the band is; previously the "peak year" was the single best year's return, a different idea). `drawDial` draws `.dial-peak` on the band at that year's end — or at today for the year in progress — as a pale disc in the band's own color (55% toward white) with a white dot; it takes the pointer like a band segment. The legend gained the row; the hub's year line and the popup's S&P row say "the cycle's peak". Peaks: the Long Boom 1999, the Dot-Com Cycle 2007, the Subprime Cycle 2017, the Global Trade Cycle 2021, the AI Cycle 2026 to date. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright — the glyph reads bull·ytd today and bear on a 2022 quarter, the peak mark sits on 2026 and reads out on hover, every closed cycle shows its peak, the Market card is gone, the legend has the new row, phone screenshot; no errors.

**Version 124.** Keren: the legend button doesn't work when clicked on its left side. Cause: on a phone the wheel fills the card, and the wheel's square box (`.season-wheel`, `position:relative`, later in the DOM) overlapped the button's left half and took the click — `elementFromPoint` at the button's left edge returned `season-wheel` at 390px (fine at 1280px, where the wheel sits well clear). Fix: `z-index:2` on `.legend-btn`. Verified the same way: both edges of the button now hit the button at 390px and 1280px.

**Version 123.** Keren: "Spring · reflation should be waxing gibbous; Autumn · stagflation should be waning crescent." `moonPhase` is now `{winter:0, springdeflation:0.16, spring:0.375, summer:0.5, autumn:0.625, lateautumn:0.84}` — symmetric about the full moon: crescent → gibbous → full → gibbous → crescent, the two quarters unused. Legend names follow. Verified with the check sheet, the legend text and a wheel screenshot; no errors.

**Version 122.** Keren reassigned the phases: "deflation is waxing crescent, reflation is first quarter, disinflation is waning gibbous, stagflation is third quarter." `moonPhase` is now `{winter:0, springdeflation:0.16, spring:0.25, summer:0.5, autumn:0.625, lateautumn:0.75}` — the two Springs swap (the crescent, still at 0.16 for legibility, is Spring–Deflation; the first quarter is Spring–Reflation), Autumn–Disinflation moves from the last quarter to the waning gibbous, and Autumn–Stagflation from the waning crescent to the third quarter; the waning crescent is no longer used. The legend's phase names follow ("third quarter" in her words). The ring now runs new → crescent → first quarter → full → gibbous → third quarter, which also happens to be the Season Model table's order. Verified with the rendered check sheet (the gibbous draws with its dark sliver on the right, as the chart has it), the legend text and a wheel screenshot; no errors.

**Version 121.** Keren, with timeanddate's moon-phase chart: "are you sure the moon phases are correct? new moon is winter and full moon is summer." Checked by rendering each of the six `moonShape` outputs at 90px: they match the chart's northern-hemisphere convention exactly — new dark, waxing crescent lit on the right, first quarter the right half, full, last quarter the left half, waning crescent lit on the left — and the ring runs Winter (new) → Springs (waxing) → Summer (full) → Autumns (waning). What was wrong was legibility, not geometry: at moon size on the ring the textbook crescents (phase 0.125 / 0.875, a lit sliver a third of a radius wide) read as new moons. The crescents now sit at 0.16 / 0.84 — the sliver about half a radius wide, still unmistakably a crescent — and a comment above `moonPhase` states the convention. Verified with the rendered check sheet and a wheel screenshot; the check sheet was sent to Keren.

**Version 120.** Keren: "How to read the wheel" is too much information — she wants just an icon in the wheel's card, no text, opening the moon legend and the bull/bear legend; "maybe you can invent an icon that says legend." Built: the legend card below the wheel is gone (markup, CSS, the per-cycle render). A small round **legend button** (`#legend-btn`, `.legend-btn`) sits in the card's top-right corner, styled like the (i) buttons, with an invented key glyph — a full moon, a half moon and a bar, each beside a short line, the three rows of a key. It opens the detail modal through a slot in `detailTexts` built once at load: a "Legend" heading, the six seasons in Keren's table order as their moons with the lunar-phase name at the right, the market band's three colors (bull, bear, year in progress), and one caption line on the badge (press and hold, drag, it stays, drag back for today; hover or tap a moon). `dialWhyFor()` — the long how-to-read-the-wheel text — is removed outright. Verified: syntax and id checks; Playwright opening the legend (all rows present), then the hub's season popup (still opens on its own slot), light desktop and phone screenshots; no errors.

**Version 119.** Keren, in two messages. First: the badge should stay where she lets go of it, and she should be able to drag it back to where it started; and instead of the GDP/CPI lines in the hub, "a link below Autumn that will open a pop up with all of this quarter's information, as much as possible — tell me what you think." Then: the cycle's name ("we see it in the calendar tab"), the season line and the note beside the wheel should go too, with the note moving into the popup, which she'd call "the current season". Built: (1) **Sticky scrub.** `dialState.parked` remembers the quarter the badge was left on; release no longer snaps home — the hub keeps that quarter, the moon stays ringed, the badge's number stays that quarter's year, and hovering another moon then leaving returns to the parked quarter. `quarterAt` now knows a home zone: dragging the badge back past the last moon (within 30° of its resting angle, and nearer to it than to any moon) returns it — and the hub — to today (or the close). The badge glides between positions (`transition:transform`). (2) **The hub is quiet again:** date line, theme word, season name, and one link, "This season ›" (a `.details-link`, the only thing in the hub that takes the pointer). It opens the existing detail modal through one reserved slot in `detailTexts` (`hubDetailIdx`), rewritten whenever the hub changes, so the link always opens what is shown. (3) **The popup** (`quarterPopup`) holds everything the data has for a quarter: the theme and season, its year of the cycle, for the present the cycle note (`cycleNowNote`, or a closed cycle's `blurb`) in the serif, the full "why it is this season" sentence (the per-season rule sentences moved into a shared `seasonRuleSentence` map, used by `seasonWhyFor` too), then rows: real GDP y/y for the quarter, the eight-quarter growth trend in pts/yr, CPI at quarter end with hot/cold/in-range, the quarter's three monthly CPI readings, the twelve-month price trend in pts/yr, the S&P 500 that year (bull/bear, to-date if current), place in the cycle, and the seasons before and after. For the present it adds the live figures — Fed funds, VIX, high-yield spread, PMI — or, for a closed cycle, its `cycleEndReadings`; the history holds no quarter-by-quarter Fed/VIX/PMI, so other quarters show what there is (said so in the code comment). (4) **The card is the wheel alone**, centred; the `.season-info` block (kicker, name, season row, note) is gone from markup, CSS and render. A closed cycle keeps its name in the Calendar's back link ("← All cycles · The Dot-Com Cycle, 2000–2007"), since nothing else on the view says it now. The wheel's own (i) (`dialWhyFor`) moved from the cycle name to the legend card as "How to read the wheel (i)", rendered per cycle from `drawDial`; the season's (i) is covered by the popup. Verified with a Playwright script: the hub, the today popup (all rows incl. Fed funds/VIX/OAS/PMI), scrub-and-release parking on Q1 2024 (badge stays, year 3, moon ringed), hover another moon → back to the parked one on leave, the parked quarter's popup (three CPI months, before/after seasons), dragging home (badge back, year 5, today), a closed cycle's back link and popup (the blurb first), the legend with its (i), and a phone screenshot; no errors. On "tell me what you think": said yes — the hub reads better with one line and one link, and the popup can carry far more than three lines could; the honest limit is that only GDP, CPI and the S&P are held quarter by quarter.

**Version 118.** Keren, three things: "something wrong with the f design inside the stagflation… the season's title, I don't know what, but it's not looking good"; "drop the action item — harvest, growing, ripening, all that"; "change the quarters to Q3 2023". (1) The title: every theme word but one contains "fl" (Stagflation, Deflation, Reflation, Inflation, Disinflation), and at the hub's display size the browser picks Fraunces' display optical cut, whose italic f is the swashy high-contrast one and joins l in a ligature — almost certainly the "f design" she meant. Two fixes, since the sandbox can't load Google Fonts to confirm which of the two it was: ligatures off (`font-variant-ligatures:none` + `liga`/`dlig`/`clig` 0) on every element that sets a season word in the serif (hub theme, hub season line, the card's season name, the legend, the Season Model table's first column), and the hub theme word pinned to the text optical size (`font-optical-sizing:none; font-variation-settings:"opsz" 20`) so it gets the calmer text-cut f. Keren to confirm on her screen. (2) The hub's season line is now the season name alone — `meta.action` no longer joins it; the Action word tile on the Cycle tab is untouched (the request read as the hub). (3) `qLabel(q)` turns the data's "2023 Q3" into "Q3 2023" wherever a person reads it: the hub's quarter line, `seasonWhyFor`'s GDP clause, the Growth ring's sub-line and the spread-history hover — the data keys stay "2023 Q3" because they sort as text. Verified: Node syntax and id checks; Playwright reading the hub (Today · Stagflation · Autumn · Growth… · Prices…), a moon hover ("Q3 2023 · Disinflation · Autumn …") and the Growth ring ("falling · Q2 2026, year over year"); no errors.

**Version 117.** Keren: while pressing and holding the year badge, "I want the year inside the marker to change" — Q2 2022 in the AI Cycle should show 1, its first year. Built: `badgeTo(deg, yearNum)` now also sets the badge's `.num` text; the scrub passes `Math.floor(seg.from) + 1` (the quarter's year of the cycle, from the track segment's fractional year offset), and on release `badgeTo(dialState.badgeDeg)` with no year restores the resting number (`m.yearIndex`). Verified with a scripted press-and-drag: 2022 Q3 → 1, 2023 Q1 → 2, 2024 Q2 → 3, 2025 Q2 → 4, 2026 Q1 → 5, back to 5 on release; no errors.

**Version 116.** Keren: "Let's drop the icons. They don't look good." And instead: press and hold "the year marker on the dial" to move between quarters, with everything the moon tooltip used to say shown inside the circle — "so we don't even need the legend below, besides bull and bear years" — and, a minute later, "drop the 'read at quarter end' from the tooltip, it's redundant." Built: the faces are gone (`weatherFaces`, `weatherFaceSvg`, the hub face element and its CSS). The hub now carries the reading: the date line (Today / Closed / the quarter), the theme word, then three short lines — season · action in the rose serif, Growth direction · GDP, Prices direction · CPI · hot/cold/in range — via `hubLines`/`hubSet`/`hubShowDefault`/`hubShowQuarter`/`hubShowYear`; the floating `.dial-tip` tooltip and its `data-tip` attributes are gone, moons carry `data-q` (an index into `dialState.quarters`, built by `drawDial` in ring order with each moon's angle span) and band segments `data-year`. Interaction, wired once: hover or tap a moon → that quarter in the hub, with the moon ringed in the dial accent (`.dial-moon.active`); hover or tap a band segment → the year and its S&P 500 return; **press and hold the year badge and drag** (pointer events with capture, `touch-action:none`, `cursor:grab`) → the angle under the pointer picks the quarter (`quarterAt`, nearest moon within 30°, so the empty rest of the ring keeps the last quarter shown), the badge rides along the ring (`badgeTo`), the hub's date line turns rose while scrubbing, and on release everything snaps home (badge to its resting angle, hub to today's or the cycle's closing reading). The legend card keeps only the two market pills. `dialWhyFor` rewritten to describe the interaction. `dialState` is declared with `var` and no initializer so the declaration can't reset a render that already ran. Verified: Node syntax check, id/getElementById cross-check, and a Playwright script exercising each path — default hub, hover on a moon (2023 Q2 · Disinflation · Autumn · Harvest · Growth falling · GDP +2.8% · Prices cooling · CPI 3.1% · hot), hover on a bear-year band segment (2022 · Bear year · −18.1%), leave → default, a full press-and-drag round the ring (2022 Q3 → … → 2026 Q2, badge moving, snapping back on release), a closed cycle's default hub (2000s: Closed, Dec 2007 · Stagflation), and a touch tap on a moon on a 390px phone; light/dark/mobile screenshots confirm the three lines clear the market band.

**Version 115.** Keren sent the fifth reference — a cloud before a crescent moon with a level face, "good for disinflation" — and asked that the icons match her references' quality: "make the resolution of the icons more like the attached icons — higher quality." The references are solid silhouettes with the face cut out, so the thin monoline outlines of Version 114 read as faint beside them; "resolution" here meant weight, not pixels (SVG has none). Redrawn: every face is now one filled shape in the ink (`currentColor`) with the eyes and mouth cut out in the card surface (`var(--surface)`, so the cut-outs read in both themes), weather details (rays, rain, breeze) as thick round-capped strokes; the hub face grew from 0.17 to 0.21 of the dial. `weatherFaces` now builds each glyph from four attribute strings (`C` ink fill, `S` surface stroke, `SD` surface fill, `RAY` thick ink stroke) plus the shared `CLOUD` path; `weatherFaceSvg` no longer sets stroke defaults on the `<svg>`. Autumn–Disinflation is her cloud-before-the-moon; five of six faces are now hers, only Spring–Deflation (cool breeze) remains a placeholder. A build slip for the record: the first assembly dropped the line defining `C`/`S`/`SD`, which the syntax check cannot catch (a ReferenceError at run time) but the Calendar walk-through did — the era rows never rendered — fixed and re-verified. Verified: Node syntax check, id/getElementById cross-check, Playwright light/dark/mobile, and the closed-cycle walk-through (all five cycles show the right face).

**Version 114.** Keren: "i want to give a face to each season" — sending, one at a time, four reference icons from a period-tracker's mood set: a glum cloud beside a smiling sun ("Mood swings") for stagflation, a smiling sun for Summer, a glum cloud in heavy rain for Winter, and the sun peeking up behind a contented cloud for Spring (an earlier "dizzy cloud with a swirl" she first sent for stagflation was superseded by the mood-swings one). Asked where the face should live; she chose the dial hub, under the theme word, and "cloud faces that represent the weather" over the plain expressions first proposed. Built: `weatherFaces` — six original monoline SVGs in the same style as `actionIcons` (viewBox 28, so the weather has room round the cloud), keyed by season key, drawn fresh rather than copying the reference icons — plus `weatherFaceSvg(key)`, a `#season-wheel-hub-face` element under the hub's theme word, sized off `--dial` like the rest of the hub and drawn in the hub's own ink (not the app's rose, which the dial avoids), and one line in `renderCycleView` so the face follows whichever cycle the view shows (verified on every closed cycle: the 1990s' Spring · reflation, the 2000s' Autumn · stagflation, 2008–17's Spring · deflation, 2018–21's Summer · inflation, and today's). Four of the six faces follow her references; the other two — a cool breeze past a level-mouthed cloud for Spring · deflation and gentle rain under a calm closed-eye cloud for Autumn · disinflation — are placeholders of mine, marked as such in the code comment, until she picks. A build slip worth recording: the first pass of the Summer/Winter faces landed on the `wheelMeta` lines instead of the faces block (a substring match on "  summer:"), which the Calendar walk-through caught immediately as "Ended in undefined" on the 2018 cycle — restored, re-verified, and the diff against Version 113 is now additions only. Verified: Node syntax check, id/getElementById cross-check, the additions-only diff, Playwright light/dark/mobile and the closed-cycle walk-through above.

**Version 113.** Keren: "remove 'late' from late autumn across the app." A naming change only: the second Autumn is now **Autumn — Stagflation**, mirroring the two Springs — one name per side of the wheel, the theme word carrying the difference. `wheelMeta.lateautumn.name` went from "Late Autumn" to "Autumn"; the key `lateautumn` stays, as do `moonPhase`, `seasonRules`, `legendOrder` and every reading — `readSeason()` was compared with the live version with comments stripped and is identical, so no quarter changes. Every remaining "Late Autumn" in copy and comments was rewritten (Autumn — Disinflation's "what comes next" pointer, `seasonWhyFor()`'s stagflation sentence, the Season Model (i) paragraph, the dial's (i) moon-order sentence, the rule comment above `readSeason()`), and a grep confirms none is left. One knock-on fix, made without asking because the rename forces it: with two Springs and two Autumns sharing a name, every place that showed a bare `meta.name` would have shown duplicate labels — the dial legend already read "Spring, Spring, …" since Version 111 — so those now use `seasonTitle(meta)` (name · theme): the legend pills, the Cycle tab's Action word-tile sub-line, the Content tab's reading kicker and its (i) caption (which dropped its own ", theme:" clause so the theme isn't said twice), and the wisdom-card kicker. Verified: Node syntax check, id/getElementById cross-check, the stripped-comment `readSeason` comparison, a 20-line scoped diff plus the four label edits, and Playwright light/dark/mobile confirming the card ("Autumn · stagflation"), hub ("Stagflation"), legend (all six distinct), the Season Model table and the Content tab's kicker and caption.

**Version 112.** Keren, finalizing the season table: "I do think that stagflation should be also in or above range just to keep it symmetrical even though it's not plausible" — making Late Autumn — Stagflation match Autumn — Disinflation's scope (within or above the range) instead of above-only, so contraction's two non-Winter seasons split purely by direction, the same way expansion's two Springs now do. Also asked to swap the table's row order: Spring — Deflation listed above Spring — Reflation.

Checked the numbers before building, since "even though it's not plausible" turned out to undersell it: 17 quarters since 1990 hit the "contraction, heating-or-steady, within range" case this newly claims for Late Autumn, including **2025 Q4** (CPI 2.65%, steady, growth falling) — a quarter on the current AI Cycle's own dial, not a historical footnote. Flagged this concretely (the exact 2025 Q4 numbers) before touching code; Keren confirmed she wants the symmetric rule regardless: "Yes, make it symmetric anyway."

Built: `readSeason()`'s contraction branch simplified to match the expansion branch's shape — cold is still Winter outright, and everything else splits by direction alone (`season = cooling ? "autumn" : "lateautumn"`), with no more range check gating Late Autumn. `seasonRules` (the on-screen Season Model table) reordered — `springdeflation` before `spring` — and Late Autumn's row updated to `zones:{within:1, above:1}` (matching Autumn's) with range text "Heating — within or above the range". `legendOrder` reordered to match. `seasonWhyFor()`'s Late Autumn sentence and the Season Model (i) popup's contraction paragraph rewritten to state the new symmetric rule instead of the old "never inside the range… the mild case" language — the old "mild Autumn" case (heating-or-steady, within range) no longer exists as a separate concept; it's folded into Late Autumn now.

Audited old vs. new, using the real committed `readSeason` source: 17 quarters change — 1995 Q3, 1996 Q2, 2001 Q1, 2005 Q2, 2007 Q3, 2009 Q4, 2010 Q1, 2014 Q1, 2016 Q2–Q4, 2017 Q1–Q2, 2019 Q4, 2020 Q1, 2021 Q1 and 2025 Q4, all Autumn → Late Autumn (the "heating/steady + within range" cases). Today's reading (2026 Q2, already Late Autumn on the hot side) is unchanged.

Verified: Node syntax check, id/getElementById cross-check, a scoped diff against the live file (6 edits, all accounted for), a direct extraction-and-run of the real committed `readSeason` confirming the 17-quarter audit independently of the build script, Playwright light/dark/mobile confirming the reordered Season Model table and the rewritten (i) popup text, and the Calendar's Long Boom era (1990–1999) with a specific check on 1996 Q2's dial tooltip ("growth falling, prices steady at 2.8% — within target" → Late Autumn · stagflation) to confirm the new symmetric case renders correctly, not just the pre-existing above-range cases.

**Version 111.** Keren retired the Goldilocks Zone. The conversation ran over several messages, each correcting the last, so the final rule is recorded here rather than the intermediate ones: she first proposed splitting expansion into direction-based seasons the way contraction already was ("spring reflection is expansion heating... good deflation... expansion cooling... something to do with Goldilocks"), initially calling the new season "Winter–Deflation (good/bad)" alongside the existing Winter, then reconsidered once she'd settled where it sits in the wheel — "this is why I think the name spring deflation is good, and we can drop the good winter, bad winter." Final rule: in expansion, hot (above the 1–3% range) is still **Summer — Inflation**, any direction; everything else — within the range *or* below it — now splits purely on direction, heating is **Spring — Reflation** (unchanged definition, just no longer limited to "cold"), cooling is the new **Spring — Deflation**, replacing the Goldilocks Zone entirely (it doesn't distinguish direction within that space, so it's gone, not renamed). Contraction is untouched: Winter — Deflation (cold, any direction), Autumn — Disinflation (cooling, within or above), Late Autumn — Stagflation (heating/steady, above), Autumn again for the unnamed mild case (heating/steady, within).

Two follow-up questions were surfaced and resolved before building: where Spring — Deflation sits in the moon-phase wheel (she chose Goldilocks's old slot — first quarter, between Winter and Spring — over the alternative of a new slot between Summer and Autumn, which was the macro-realistic position but would have reordered the wheel), and what to do with the Content tab's hand-written narrative for the retired Goldilocks entry and the new season (she chose to leave the new season's body/economy/next/watch blank rather than have placeholder copy drafted, so `seasonReading.springdeflation` ships with empty strings — the Content tab shows the card with empty sections until she writes it, which was verified not to crash by forcing `seasonOverride` to it in a test build).

Built: `readSeason()`'s expansion branch simplified from a three-way level check (hot/cold/else) to hot-or-not, then direction (`season = cooling ? "springdeflation" : "spring"`) — the contraction branch is untouched. Every place that named the six seasons was updated to match: `wheelOrder`, `wheelMeta` (new `springdeflation` key, name "Spring", theme "Deflation"), `moonPhase` (`springdeflation:0.25`, the old Goldilocks slot), the dial legend order, `seasonWhyFor()`'s per-season sentences, the Season Model table rows and its (i) popup paragraph, the `dialWhyFor()` moon-order sentence, and the big code comment above `readSeason()`. Winter's own "what usually comes next" pointer, which used to name "The Goldilocks Zone," was corrected to point at Spring (either variant, depending on which way prices are moving as growth turns) since that pointer's existing description ("growth turns up while prices are still cooling") already matched Spring — Deflation's definition exactly.

Audited old vs. new across all five cycles (1990–2026, 147 quarters), using the actual committed `readSeason` source extracted and run directly, not a hand-copy: 49 quarters change — the 45 quarters that used to read Goldilocks (26 become Spring — Reflation, 19 become Spring — Deflation, split by which way prices were moving that quarter) plus 4 quarters (2014 Q4–2015 Q3) that were Spring under the old any-direction rule and become Spring — Deflation now that direction matters. The one visible-today effect: 2024 Q2–Q4, previously Goldilocks, now read as Spring — Deflation. Today's own reading (2026 Q2, Late Autumn) is unchanged either way.

Verified: Node syntax check, id/getElementById cross-check, a scoped diff against the live file (15 edits, all accounted for), a direct extraction-and-run of the real committed `readSeason` confirming the 49-quarter audit independently of the build script, Playwright light/dark/mobile on the Cycle tab, the Content tab's Season Model table and (i) popup text, the Calendar's Long Boom cycle (1990–1999, which contains real Spring — Deflation quarters) with its dial tooltips checked for correct labels and no `undefined` values, and a forced-`seasonOverride` test build confirming the blank `springdeflation` narrative entry renders without errors on the Content tab.

**Version 110.** Keren, reviewing the current cycle: Q4 2023 computed as Summer — Inflation, but she wanted it to read as Autumn — Disinflation, reasoning "whenever growth is flat, I would want it to continue the previous season according to the different conditions" — Q3 2023 was falling growth with falling prices (Autumn); Q4 2023 growth went flat with prices still falling, so for her it's still disinflation, not a fresh inflation reading; Q1 2024 (rising growth, prices still falling but hot) correctly stays Summer. Asked about the very first computable quarter, with no previous season to inherit from, she pointed out the underlying data is continuous across market-cycle boundaries already — "you do have a previous quarter, it's just not in the current cycle" — citing the existing 2022 bleed-year behavior (Summer carried over from 2021) as proof it's both correct and already how the data behaves.

Built: `readSeason(cpi12, gdp8, prevRegime)` takes a third argument; growth is `regime = "expansion"` when rising, `"contraction"` when falling, and — new — `prevRegime || "expansion"` when flat, so a flat quarter now continues whichever regime came before it instead of always defaulting to expansion. The function returns a new `regime` field, and `seasonWhyFor()` reads it directly instead of re-deriving it from `growthTrend`. To make "the previous quarter" always resolve — including reaching back before a market-cycle era's own start year — a new `seasonTrackAll` is computed once across the FULL `gdpQuarterlyYoY`/`cpiYoYHistory` history (not per era), threading `prevRegime` from each quarter to the next with no reset at cycle boundaries; `cycleModel(era)` now slices this one continuous sequence for its `track` and looks up `reading` from it, rather than recomputing seasons itself. Audited old vs. new across all five cycles (1990–2026, 147 quarters): only 6 quarters change — 1992 Q1, 2010 Q1, 2013 Q4, 2014 Q1, 2017 Q2 (all Goldilocks/Summer → Autumn) and 2023 Q4 (Summer → Autumn, the case Keren cited); today's reading (2026 Q2, Late Autumn) is unchanged.

While reviewing this, Keren separately said "I think stagflation is contraction + hot, not heating" — proposing Late Autumn trigger on the temperature level alone rather than level + direction. Checked the numbers first: December 2023 CPI was 3.32% (still hot) even though falling, so that redefinition would have flipped her own Q4 2023 example to Late Autumn — Stagflation instead of Autumn — Disinflation, directly conflicting with the fix above (and would also have changed 1991 Q2–Q3, 1992 Q1, 2001 Q2 and all of 2022 Q4–2023 Q3 the same way). Flagged the concrete conflict to her; she chose to keep the existing stagflation rule (hot **and** heating-or-steady) so Q4 2023 stays Autumn — Disinflation, so only the regime-carry-over change above shipped; the stagflation redefinition is not built.

Also updated the code comment above `readSeason()` and the user-facing "Season Model" (i) text on the Content tab, both of which still described flat growth as unconditionally counting as expansion.

Verified: Node syntax check, id/getElementById cross-check, a scoped diff against the live file (the regime logic, `seasonTrackAll`, `cycleModel`, `seasonWhyFor`, and the two doc-comment updates — nothing else touched), Playwright light/dark/mobile on the Cycle tab and the Calendar's AI Cycle and Global Trade Cycle, and — since this changes computed history, not just display — a direct extraction-and-run of the actual committed `readSeason`/`seasonTrackAll` source (not a hand-copy) against the real data, confirming the 6-quarter result above independently of the build script.

**Version 109.** Keren: "Drop the proliferation pronoun. We've only [the] cholelac [Goldilocks] zone, both in the content and everywhere else in the app. And also in mobile, the cycle turns a bit to the right, center it in the middle of the screen." Two fixes. (1) "Proliflation" removed everywhere the Goldilocks Zone's theme word appeared — the data definition (`theme:"Proliflation"` → `theme:""`), the Content tab's "In the economy" reading, a code comment, the `seasonWhyFor()` sentence, the dial's hover tooltip, the dial hub's center label, the cycle-view's season-name line, the Content tab's reading-kicker caption, and the Season Model table row — so the Goldilocks Zone now reads as just "Goldilocks Zone" with no "· theme" suffix anywhere, while every other season keeps its own theme word unchanged. A new `seasonTitle(meta)` helper (`meta.theme ? name + " · " + theme.toLowerCase() : name`) centralizes the now-conditional join so future seasons without a theme word don't need each call site touched again. (2) The mobile dial was shifted right rather than centered: `.season-wheel-wrap`'s `--dial:min(calc(100vw - 48px), 380px)` under-subtracted the actual horizontal padding (20px page wrap + 16px season-card padding, per side = 72px total, not 48px); changed to `calc(100vw - 72px)`. Measured before/after on a 390px viewport: the wheel sat 37px from the left and 11px from the right (visibly shoved right) before the fix, 37px/35px after — properly centered. Built on top of a same-day scheduled data refresh (Version 108, below) rather than the stale copy in the working scratchpad — the live artifact was re-read and diffed first, the same 11 edits were reapplied onto the fresher data, and the result was re-verified (Node syntax check, id/CSS cross-check, Playwright light/dark/mobile screenshots, a scoped diff against the refreshed live file) before publishing.

**Version 108 (scheduled refresh, other session).** The weekday refresh task ran while V109 was being prepared: `DATA_COMPILED` moved to Sep 17, 2026, plus the yield curve, Buffett/CAPE readings, the high-yield OAS reading and its date, initial jobless claims, and the current year's S&P 500 YTD return all updated to that day's figures. No structural changes.

**Version 107.** Keren: "make the moons in the cycle black and white instead of yellow and purple." The two moon tokens changed — `--moon-lit` #ffffff, `--moon-dark` #14121a (near-black ink) in light; #f6f3f7 / #0a090d with a pale edge in dark, so a new moon still shows against the dark surface. The year badge and the legend swatches follow the same tokens, so they went black and white with it. Nothing else touched.

**Version 106.** Keren, after liking the temperature chart: she wanted inflation per cycle, in the Calendar tab, without "too much information all at once." I proposed a cycle picker with a Temperature · Growth · Market switch; she went further — "when I click on each cycle in the calendar tab, I move into a new page that looks like the current cycle view, so we don't have to maintain different views… every time we work on it, it will apply to the current cycle or any other cycle" — and then, that the Calendar's yearly GDP chart and S&P year cards "are pretty much the definition of the cycle," so they belong in that view too. Built: one **cycle view**. `cycleModel(era)` computes everything for a cycle (season at its end, the quarter-by-quarter track, ring numbers, its months of CPI, the readings behind Rates/Feeling/Energy) and `renderCycleView(model)` paints the single `#cycle-view` element: card + dial, temperature chart, Growth and Rates rings, word tiles, then the yearly GDP growth card and the Market year cards (moved in from the Calendar). The Cycle tab shows it for the current cycle; the Calendar is now a list of the five cycles and opens the same element for the tapped one, with "← All cycles" back. To make past cycles computable, `cpiYoYHistory` now runs from Jan 1989 and `gdpQuarterlyYoY` from 1988 Q1 (from the backtest's FRED level files; the 2020+ overlap matched the live arrays exactly), and a fixed `cycleEndReadings` table holds each closed cycle's Fed funds target, VIX close and ISM PMI at its last month — researched on the Fed's open-market history, the exchange's daily closes and ISM's releases; the ICE BofA high-yield OAS could not be sourced because FRED now restricts that series to three years, so closed cycles' Feeling reads from the VIX alone and says so. Two dial fixes fell out: the years are laid over the arc short of the seam (a cycle as long as the ring used to end under the drop — the 8- and 10-year cycles showed it), and moons shrink a touch past 32 quarters. `feelingToday`/`energyToday` became `feelingFrom(vix, oas)`/`energyFrom(pmi)`. Verified: Node syntax and id/CSS checks, Playwright walking every cycle from the list on desktop and phone (light and dark), the Cycle tab taking the view back, the back link, the Analysis and Content tabs still reading today's figures.

**Version 105.** Keren: "This looks really good. I don't need the bear year drop of blood. you can remove it." The bear-year shading, its drop and the legend item are gone from the Temperature chart; the dial's market band still carries the bear years. `tempInfo` lost its bleed sentence.

**Version 104.** Keren sent a screenshot of Natural Cycles' temperature chart (daily basal temperature as columns from a baseline, a today line with the cycle day, the period shaded with drops) — "i like this temperature chart can we use it in our app?" Mapped one-to-one: monthly CPI across the current cycle as columns from the 2% target, the 1–3% range as a green band, a today line with the month / cycle year / reading, the bear year shaded with the dial's teardrop. Asked two things: where it lives (she chose **replacing the Temperature ring** on the Cycle tab, over a new card under the rings or folding it into the Analysis tab's CPI card) and how the columns are colored (she took the recommended hot/in-range/cold tap colors from the Season Model table over the reference's single green). Built: `#temp-card` between the dial legend and the rings, the vitals strip down to two rings (Growth, Rates), `tempRow`/`tempPct` removed, `tempInfo` rewritten to explain the chart; the chart draws at 780 wide on desktop and 400 on phones (redrawn on resize) so the phone version is as tall as the reference. Checked against the dataviz skill's mark specs (≤24px columns, 4px rounded data end square at the baseline, 2px gap, status colors with a labeled legend, hover on every column). Verified: Node syntax, id/CSS cross-check, scoped diff against the live file, Playwright light/dark/phone with the hover tooltip exercised.

**Version 103.** Keren, with the exact numbers: "US GDP was 2.8 in 2024, 2.2 in 2025, but the line goes higher instead of lower" — reported as a data bug. Checked the live data and render math directly (Node, the actual pixel coordinates, and a screenshot): `usRealGdpGrowth` has 2024:2.79, 2025:2.16, correctly decreasing, and the US (blue) line does step down between those two years. What actually happens: Israel (orange, on by default alongside the US) jumps from 0.95% to 2.93% over the same span and crosses above the US line right at the last point — a genuine data bug it is not, but a real misread it invites, since nothing in the chart named which line was which without hovering. Fix: when ≤4 countries are on, each line now gets a direct end-of-line country-code label (USA, ISR, …) at its last point, colored to match; falls back to legend/hover-only above 4 countries to avoid clutter. `padR` widened from 20 to 44 to leave room for the labels. Verified in Node (syntax, id/CSS cross-check), a scoped diff against the live file, and Playwright screenshots (light/dark/mobile, plus a 5-country check confirming labels correctly disappear past the 4-series cutoff).

**Version 102.** Keren: "gdp growth doesn't show the actual data. also drop japan from the default view and make a dropdown for the countries instead of pills." The "doesn't show the actual data" half turned out to have no reproducible defect at the time (chart rendered correctly with real per-country data in every test) — see Version 103 above for what she was actually seeing. The other two: Japan's `on` flag set to `false` (United States and Israel now the only defaults); the pill-toggle legend (`.gdp-legend`/`.gdp-chip`) replaced with a dropdown — a trigger button naming the countries currently on, opening a checkbox panel grouped Core/Europe (`.gdp-country-picker`/`-trigger`/`-panel`), closing on outside-click or Escape. Also fixed a related inconsistency while in there: the Analysis tab's collapsed "GDP growth" summary line used to hardcode a mention of the "Core" group (implicitly always naming Japan); it now reads the same `gdpCountries` "on" state the chart itself uses, so the two can't disagree. Verified: Node syntax check, id/CSS cross-check, a scoped diff against the prior live file, and Playwright across light/dark/mobile (default state, dropdown open/close, checkbox toggling re-rendering the chart, outside-click-to-close).

**Version 101.** Keren: two fixes on the Content tab. (1) Renamed the season-table kicker from "How the season is read" to **"The Season Model"** — she suggested "the season's framework" but the framework table right below it is already titled "The framework", so a name that collided there would have made the two sections harder to tell apart; "The Season Model" was the alternative offered and used. (2) In the manuscript's framework table (`frameworkRows`), Keren pointed out that "asset prices" belonged under Psychology (sentiment/valuations), not Energy (capital) — Energy's economy text trimmed from "Capital — GDP, profits, asset prices" to "Capital — GDP, profits"; Psychology's economy text extended from "Investor sentiment" to "Investor sentiment, asset valuations". She confirmed the other six rows are fine as-is.

**Version 81.** Keren rethought the rule: "growth, even though it's flat, is still positive" — so the growth column became expansion / contraction, the temperature column heating / cooling with the range, and the expansion-plus-cooling cell became the **Goldilocks Zone** (replacing Early Spring — Proliflation). Built: `readSeason()` rewritten to her table with the tie-breaks recorded in Part 1, the season renamed throughout (key `goldilocks`, theme "Soft landing", action Growing, the waxing crescent), its reading entry rewritten, the Content tab's table recolumned as Growth / Temperature.

**Version 100.** Keren: the subtitle under "How the season is read" repeated the (i) — removed; its two definitions now live inside the (i).

(A straight apostrophe in the new (i) text broke the script on the first build — caught by the syntax check before publishing.)

**Version 98.** Keren: stagflation also needs the green bar — contraction with prices heating inside the range is Late Autumn, not the Autumn fallback. Rule and bars updated; the ring is unchanged (no such quarter in the AI Cycle).

**Version 97.** Keren: disinflation is cooling both above and within the range — Autumn now lights the green and red bars; the rule already sent contraction-within-range to Autumn, so its comments and (i) were aligned and nothing on the ring changed. (She said "blue marker"; blue is below the range and belongs to Winter, so I read it as the in-range bar and said so.)

**Version 96.** "Soft landing" removed from the Goldilocks Zone's reading on the Content tab at Keren's nod; the reading now describes expansion with prices inside the range, nothing more.

**Version 95 (health check).** Keren asked for a health check before moving on. Ran: syntax, id and CSS cross-checks; Playwright on desktop and phone, all four tabs, every (i) popover and expand modal (28) opened with content, every collapsible drawer/subject had content, no page errors; a stale-term scan of all rendered text and of the source. Found and fixed: one stale code comment still describing the removed "Flagged right now" feed, one unused variable. Confirmed consistent: the season on the card, the hub, the word tiles, the Temperature/Growth/Rates rings, the Content table's marked row and the reading entry all say Late Autumn / stagflation from the same reading; the sources page matches the app; both scheduled-task prompts match the V91+ structure.

**Version 94.** Keren: the moon phases should follow the new order — Spring is now the waxing crescent and the Goldilocks Zone the first quarter (`moonPhase`); the dial and legend redraw from it.

**Version 93.** Keren: swap the Spring and Goldilocks rows so the bars read left to right — blue, green, red, red, red, blue. Done in the table and in the dial's legend.

**Version 92.** Keren: expansion with cold prices is reflation, not deflation — the last direction-dependent cell on the expansion side went; the expansion half of the rule is now purely the level (cold Spring, lukewarm Goldilocks, hot Summer), and the table's Spring row reads "Cold".

**Version 91.** Keren: the backtest section is not needed — removed from the app (record kept above); the season table revised a third time — temperature is now mostly a level (Goldilocks / Heating / Hot / Cooling / Heating / Cold), Spring lights only the blue bar and Autumn only the red; `readSeason()` follows it with the tie-breaks recorded in Part 1. The ring and today's call are unchanged.

**Version 90.** The backtest. Keren asked for an honest take on the app; I said the framework had been illustrated but not tested and proposed testing it first, then a confidence word on the season call, then her words, then the Analysis tab; she said start with the test. Built "The seasons since 1990" on the Calendar tab (see above). Result: the expansion seasons are the market's good weather (Goldilocks +19%/yr, Spring +15%), Late Autumn its worst (+2%, negative the quarter after), Winter poor while it lasts and the best quarter-after — the book's story holds on this history, with the caveats one tap away. Sources page gained the multpl series.

**Version 89.** Keren: the table is right, the rule should follow it — the Goldilocks Zone is only the green area. `readSeason()` now sends expansion-with-cooling to Summer when prices are above the range, to Winter when below, to the Goldilocks Zone only within; two 2023–24 crescents on the dial became full moons. She also asked whether the Temperature (direction) column is needed at all if the range alone could decide — answered in chat: three of the six season names are directional (reflation, disinflation, stagflation), and a level-only grid would call the 2023 disinflation year stagflation.

## V524 — the retired half-dial (Fear & Greed's mark, V465—V523)

Removed when Fear & Greed took the heart. Drawn as two arc segments with the needle passing through the
upper-right break, the needle FILLED and tapered rather than stroked, because a stroked line of even weight
is a clock hand and the taper is what made it read as a dial pointer at 15px. Checked at 15/22/44/96 beside
Pressure's gauge, the one mark it could be confused with: that one is a closed disc on a stand with a thin
needle to the upper LEFT, this an open C with a heavy pointer to the upper RIGHT.

```js
function dialSvg(){ return markSvg(
  '<path d="M6.06 19.14 A8.4 8.4 0 0 1 16.2 5.93" stroke-width="1.8"/>' +
  '<path d="M19.27 9 A8.4 8.4 0 0 1 17.94 19.14" stroke-width="1.8"/>' +
  '<path d="M13.5 14.7 L19.1 6.1 L10.5 11.7 A2.15 2.15 0 0 0 13.5 14.7 Z" fill="currentColor" stroke="none"/>'); }
```

# Part 4 — Research the app was built from

Two documents the app was built from — reference, not app rules: the indicator table the season model comes
from, and the research behind Horizon's un-inversion panel. Both settled.

## 4.1 — Seasonal Behaviour: the indicator table

Working reference for the "Seasonal Behaviour" chapter, mapping the menstrual-cycle framework onto economic indicators. Categories assigned against standard classification (Conference Board LEI components, credit-cycle literature) and fertility-awareness science (BBT vs cervical mucus timing relative to ovulation). **This table is the origin of the app's season model.**

| Indicator | Body | Economy | Category |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hormones | Rising estrogen / LH surge | Credit (money supply, lending growth) | Leading |
| Cervical fluid | Cervical mucus change | Credit spreads / yield curve | Leading |
| Psychology | Emotional state | Investor sentiment | Leading |
| Energy | Energy | Capital — GDP, profits, asset prices, stock prices | Coincident |
| Desire | Desire / libido | Risk tolerance | Coincident |
| Activity | Physical activity | Labor / employment | Lagging |
| Temperature | Basal body temperature | Inflation | Lagging |

**Why each sits where it does.** Credit expansion predicts cycle turns with a lead time (Schularick & Taylor, 1870–2008), as rising estrogen initiates the next phase before it is outwardly visible. Cervical mucus changes *before* ovulation, making it predictive rather than confirmatory — unlike BBT; the yield curve is an explicit named component of the Conference Board LEI. Consumer expectations are themselves one of the ten official LEI components, so sentiment is formally forward-looking. Energy is a composite and the one imperfect fit: GDP is the standard real-time benchmark, but stock prices within the same bucket lean leading. Libido peaks at the fertile window itself — real-time, like risk appetite revealed in current positioning. Unemployment is a textbook lagging indicator, typically 2–3 quarters behind a recovery. BBT rises only *after* ovulation, confirming rather than predicting, exactly as CPI does.

Sources: Conference Board LEI component list; Schularick & Taylor (NBER w15512); StatPearls, *Physiology, Ovulation and Basal Body Temperature*; Financial Edge Training, *Leading, Lagging & Coincident Economic Indicators*.


## 4.2 — T10Y3M un-inversion to recession lag

Compiled Sep 15, 2026 for Horizon's "what happened every other time" panel. Cross-verified pass on **inversion → un-inversion → NBER recession start** since the late 1960s.

**Read this before trusting any pre-1985 date.** FRED's daily T10Y3M starts January 1982; earlier, the spread must be reconstructed from GS10 minus TB3MS at monthly resolution, so no daily crossing dates are knowable. No source with rigorously dated 1969–70 or 1973–75 months could be independently corroborated — those two rows are illustrative only. Even in the daily era, sources disagree by 1–4 months because these crossings are choppy, which is the same phenomenon the app's own chart marks on the 2024–25 un-invert.

| Cycle | Inversion | Un-inversion | NBER start | Lag | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1969–70 | ~Dec 1968 | ~Feb 1970 | Dec 1969 | recession began ~2 mo BEFORE un-inversion | LOW |
| 1973–75 | ~Jun 1973 | ~Nov 1974 | Nov 1973 | ~12 mo BEFORE | LOW |
| 1980 | ~Nov 1978 | ~Apr 1980 | Jan 1980 | ~3 mo BEFORE | LOW–MOD |
| 1981–82 | ~Oct 1980 | ~Sep 1981 | Jul 1981 | ~2 mo BEFORE | LOW–MOD |
| 1989–91 | ~Jun 1989 (NY Fed: −0.16pp) | Sep or Nov 1989 | Jul 1990 | 8–10 mo | MOD–HIGH |
| 2001 | ~Jul 2000 (alt Apr 2000) | Jan 2001 (both sources agree) | Mar 2001 | 2 mo | HIGH |
| 2007–09 | ~Feb 2006 | Jun or Aug 2007 | Dec 2007 | 4–6 mo | MOD–HIGH |
| 2020 | May 23, 2019 | Oct 10, 2019 (brief 2-day re-inversion late Jan/early Feb 2020) | Feb 2020 | ~4 mo | HIGH |

**The finding that matters, and the reason the panel splits its sample.** The four modern cycles (1989–91, 2001, 2007–09, 2020) show lags of 9, 2, 5 and 4 months — range 2–10, average ~5.0, median ~4.5. The four earliest show the OPPOSITE relationship: the recession began before the final un-inversion, by 2 to 12 months, the curve staying inverted right through the onset. Averaging the two eras together would mislead rather than clarify. This split is the single most important, non-obvious result of the research.

**Recessions with no prior inversion:** none. Every recession 1969–70 through 2020 was preceded by a negative T10Y3M reading.

**1998 was NOT an inversion — a verified correction to the popular story.** The NY Fed's own FAQ gives September 1998 at **+0.12 percentage points, positive**, grouped with November 1995 (+0.42) as episodes where the spread "attained very low positive levels… but did not invert." Describe 1998 as a near-flat-curve episode that did not cross zero; the widely repeated "near miss" more likely refers to 2s10s or to credit spreads during LTCM.

**Other near-misses.** 1966: the NY Fed calls this the only false signal on record before 1970 — a real inversion before a mid-cycle slowdown, not a recession. 1984: the Boston Fed mentions a tightening "reversed fairly quickly without an ensuing recession" but does not confirm the spread crossed zero; unconfirmed, needs its own check before use.

**Sources.** NBER business cycle dates · NY Fed, "The Yield Curve as a Leading Indicator: FAQ" (primary; exact spread values for Jun 1989, Nov 1995, Sep 1998, Dec 2000) · infineo.ai, "The Yield Curve Uninverts" · currentmarketvaluation.com yield-curve model (the only source with months for pre-1985) · Forbes (Chuck Jones) for the exact 2019 dates · Boston Fed, "Predicting Recessions Using the Yield Curve" (2020) · Western Asset whitepaper (qualitative) · Advisor Perspectives (alternate inversion starts, unresolved 3–4 month discrepancy for 2001 and 2007–09) · FRED T10Y3M, GS10, TB3MS.

**Open items.** The inversion-START discrepancy for 2001 and 2007–09 is unresolved. 1969–70 and 1973–75 remain under-sourced; firming them up means pulling monthly GS10/TB3MS directly. The 1984 candidate is unconfirmed.
